# Job files and outputs

*Where Smoozy's own directories sit on a rented machine and what every path under them is called; what a job's immutable submission manifest carries and where it is published; the one reading of a `.blend` that the folders shown, the count, the collision record, the main-output walk, the preview source and the re-run's already-there test all read; how every place a job would write is brought under the job's folder and what is done where one cannot be; what a frame owes and what a job's expected-output inventory is; which viewable derivatives the machine produces, from which file, in which colour treatment, and when they are cleared; and what a job's completeness may and may not be read off.*

Rule 1's plain-English rule is suspended here, per the Architecture stage's exception.

**Scope, from [The shape](The%20shape.md#5-job-files-and-outputs):** server directory layout; immutable submission manifest; job folder and asset area; atomic publication of job outputs into the job folder under `Server protocol and transfers`' staging-and-publication contract; path and name rules; output redirect; remap material; expected-output inventory; main output; collision record; completeness readings; one machine-produced set of viewable derivatives shared by `Queue & History` and `Telegram`; cleanup ownership for the machine-side derivatives and fetched copies under the local lifetimes `State, identity and recovery` defines. **One clause of that scope line cannot be kept for the files a render writes, and [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 2 states which and why.** This document uses transfer publication from `Server protocol and transfers` and identities from `State, identity and recovery`; it gives `Rendering` its launch material, `Dependency Upload & Remap` its asset and remap stores, `Queue & History` its output readings, `Download` its inventory, and `Telegram` its preview source.

**Twenty-five permanent coverage rows are answered here — Rows 118 to 138, Row 313, Row 375, and Rows 493 and 495 — with Row 496's machine half answered here beside them, its entry half `Queue & History`'s; everything else below is a constraint the shape or an input document forced.** *(Rows 493, 495 and 496 were added on 2026-08-25, out of the Design close re-sweep; I16a, H1a and B31a are written for them, and the only other clauses that moved are H1's second condition — widened in place with the change dated — and [Section 13](#13-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document)'s supplies to `Queue & History` and `Render Settings`, a line each.)* *(**Row 313 was added to this table and to the count on 2026-09-02**, and no clause moved with it: `Rows/What a job writes.md` and `Coverage.md` have both named D21f, D21g and D21b-ii as its answer since 2026-08-31, and this table alone did not carry it, so a gate matching row assignments against this document found a row answered nowhere and three clauses belonging to no row. The count read twenty-four from 2026-08-25 to that day. **An unresolved bucket is not an exemption from the table**: Row 313 closes UNRESOLVED — PROMISE STANDING at [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 22, and the *promise standing* half is written here and has to be findable from the row.)* *(**Rows 522 and 525 were added to this table and to the count later on 2026-10-04**, on the build runner's ruling: both were filed that day and answered at [6.6](#66-where-the-job-output-folder-sits) — Row 522 at P18–P22, Row 525 at P23 — and `Coverage.md` and `Rows/What a job writes.md` named them while this table did not, which is the Row 313 fault again. **The count reads twenty-seven from that day.**)*

## What binds this before anything is decided

**[State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#13-rules-that-bind-every-store) §1.3 rule 5 binds every section below: nothing reads a file's presence, its name or its size as state.** Every state fact comes from a record Smoozy or the supervisor wrote. **The one carve-out is this document's and it is narrow**, in that rule's own words: *"The one place a file's presence is read deliberately is the re-run's already-there test, which `Queue & History` and `Job files and outputs` own and which is a rule about what to render, never a reading of state."* [Section 10](#10-completeness-already-there-the-last-frame-the-exit-status-and-the-stop) lives inside that carve-out and stays inside it: the reckoning's result is consumed by the render worker at that moment and is never written as state, never read back as state, and never used to decide anything but which frames to render. **Two other readings of material appear below and neither is a reading of state**: the four-value change test of [4.4](#44-the-file-identity-and-when-a-recorded-reading-goes-stale), which is the same test [Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified) §7.4 S13 already makes before a resume; and the read-back that confirms a placement, which is [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#13-rules-that-bind-every-store) §1.3 rule 4's *confirmed by a real write* applied to the other direction.

**Three observations force that rule and none is softened here.** A killed transfer leaves *"a piece of the file under the final name. No marker, no `.part`, no temporary name, nothing to say it is incomplete"* ([The rented machine](The%20rented%20machine.md)). A machine restarted mid-render left eleven whole frames and *"Six further files sat beside them at **zero bytes**"*, so *"the output folder said seventeen and the truth was eleven"* (same). And on a full disk Blender *"announces the save, exits 0, says nothing about the disk, and leaves a picture that does not end"* (same).

**Publication is not this document's to design.** [Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#7-staging-integrity-resume-and-publication--one-contract-in-both-directions) §7 owns the staging-and-publication contract in both directions for the machine's whole life, including the same-filesystem requirement of S1, the durable *publication pending* pre-state of S15, and the declared degradation of S17 where the POSIX-rename extension is not announced. It is taken entire and no second contract is written. That document also states from its own side that **the server directory layout, the job folder and what a job's paths are called are this document's**, and that *"nothing here synthesises a path except the staging path"* of its S4. What is settled below is where a job's outputs are published *to*, and under what names.

**Facts 78 and 79 are answered in part and open in part, and [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#96-where-a-unit-must-be-atomic-and-the-ground-for-it-is-not-established) §9.6 already says what is done for the open part:** a unit that rests on either is written as atomic and the document resting on it says so. That is taken and not decided again. **Fact 78's interruption boundaries were answered on 2026-08-18 on ext4, xfs and overlayfs**, by real forcible kills at every one of the four commit boundaries, with no part-arrived bytes at a final name on any of the three; **what is left open is the rollback boundary, never interrupted mid-flight and blocked twice over, and Fact 44's outright power loss** ([Facts](Facts.md), Fact 78). **Fact 79's documentation route is answered on Mac and on Linux and answered negatively on Windows, and only its per-system throwaway route is unrun** (same, Fact 79); it governs publication on the user's own computer, and no unit this document declares rests on it. [Section 12](#12-atomic-or-partial--this-documents-declarations) declares this document's units against [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#9-the-atomic-or-partial-boundary) §9, and [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 1 states what breaks.

**The job folder and the asset area arrive owned by root on a rental's second place to write.** Observed — an attached volume *"arrives `root:root 755`"* and refuses the non-root login until a setup step hands it over ([Two more boxes](Two%20more%20boxes%20—%20the%20volume,%20the%20full%20disk,%20and%20a%20day%20left%20running.md), Fact 6). Handing it over is `Server Setup`'s step; [1.3](#13-ownership-and-the-confirmation-that-a-placement-worked) is the requirement placed on it.

**A path this document owns is what protects something already closed.** [Security and licence](Security%20and%20licence.md#10-what-smoozy-leaves-on-a-rented-machine) §10 places the Telegram bot token inside the server package's own state area, *"outside every job folder"*, and states that its protection *"protects against the user's own browsing and against every other Smoozy operation on that machine, because those all run under paths `Job files and outputs` owns."* [1.2](#12-the-areas-and-what-may-never-sit-under-a-job-folder) is that sentence made a rule, and [2.1](#21-every-path-on-the-machine-is-synthesised-here-and-nowhere-else) is what keeps it true.

**Fact 29's name hazards are carried, not closed.** All 33 hostile names reached the machine byte-for-byte and *"coming back is where it breaks"* ([Six machines](Six%20machines%20—%20the%20container,%20the%20webhook%20and%20the%20ready%20action.md), Fact 29); on 2026-08-17 seventeen names written on a rental came back as **fourteen files**, names differing only in capitals collapsing to one holding the last payload fetched, two spellings of one accented name doing the same, and **every fetch reporting success** ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §7.1). `Server protocol and transfers` S4 keeps its staging path off the file's own name, which solves it for that one mechanism. **The final name is this document's and it is unsolved**; [2.5](#25-the-output-names-are-not-touched-and-the-inventory-is-a-statement-about-the-machine) says what is done and [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 10 states what breaks.

**A container rental's recycle destroys everything Smoozy installed and returns on the same address and port with nothing in the connection to reveal it**, with the boot hook *"not deleted but replaced"* by a provider default ([Six machines](Six%20machines%20—%20the%20container,%20the%20webhook%20and%20the%20ready%20action.md), *Recycle*). **No directory's presence is ever read as evidence that Smoozy's installation is there.** The installation identity of [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#23-the-machines-installation-identity) §2.3 is the only thing that tells a rental from an installation, and [Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#45-a-reconnection-is-gated-on-the-installation-identity-and-not-on-reachability) §4.5 is its gate applied to every reconnection.

**Three operating systems on the user's end, Linux alone on the rented end**, and **Windows cannot be posed at all today**. Every path rule below is a Linux rule about the rented machine. Where an answer touches the user's own computer it is named as a seam and not decided here — [2.5](#25-the-output-names-are-not-touched-and-the-inventory-is-a-statement-about-the-machine) and [8.4](#84-the-derivatives-one-set-made-once-on-the-machine).

**The derivatives are one set, made once, on the machine**, which [The shape](The%20shape.md#each-rented-server) settles. [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#113-the-two-rulings-those-documents-were-promised) §11.3 has already ruled both halves: a fetched derivative is **subject-held on its job record and cleared when that record is deleted or when its own age ending is reached, whichever comes first** *(corrected 2026-08-22 — this said cleared when that record is deleted **and at no other moment**, which the Creator's ruling of that date overruled: they chose an age limit over a size cap and over leaving it unbounded)*. **The period is thirty days**, counted from the moment the copy was published on the user's own computer, on that computer's clock — ruled 2026-09-02 under the Creator's standing delegation of that date and written at [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#111-five-classes-and-the-reason-there-is-no-sixth) §11.1 R16, which this document cites and does not re-decide *(corrected 2026-09-02: this sentence said the period is not yet set)*,, and a machine-side derivative **may be cleared only once the fetched copy exists or the job's record is gone**. Those are applied in [Section 11](#11-cleanup-and-the-lifetimes-this-document-applies) and no third rule is invented.

**The job identity's uniqueness scope is given rather than chosen.** It is *"unique across every machine and every rental, not per machine"*, it is opaque, it is never derived from anything the user typed and never derived from anything read off a machine, and **no path is ever synthesised from an identity by anything but the one document that owns that path** ([State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#21-the-rules-that-bind-every-identity) §2.1 rule 7). This document is that owner for every path below.

**Do not read past the input documents.** Every machine observation quoted here is a frozen snapshot of one or two boxes on one or two days, in those documents' own words: *"Read it as *this is what that box did*, never as *this is what a rented machine does*."* The batch of 2026-08-17 was a **container on `overlayfs`** with three testers sharing four cores; every timing from it is an upper bound.

---

## 1. The server directory layout

### 1.1 One root, one mount, chosen once

**L1. Every directory this document owns sits under one root on the rented machine — the package area alone may sit apart, per L4a — and the root is chosen once per installation and never re-chosen.** Its path is recorded in the machine's state area at hand-over, beside the installation identity, and it is recorded against the server row on the desktop side as a machine-derived value carrying that identity ([State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#23-the-machines-installation-identity) §2.3).

**L2. The candidate set is the writable locations `Server Setup` was given, and the choice is made by a per-path free-space reading against each candidate's own path**, taken through [Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#91-a-reading-is-a-choice-of-mount-and-the-mount-is-the-one-the-write-was-attempted-on) §9.1's two routes, reading the **available** figure and never the free one. The largest available figure wins. **No figure is derived from a filesystem list**, which is Fact 28's trap: a plain reading on one container returned **51 filesystems**, 48 memory-backed, one of them *"the **host's** disk, bind-mounted in to deliver the driver files"* showing 20 GiB free the rental may not touch ([Two more boxes](Two%20more%20boxes%20—%20the%20volume,%20the%20full%20disk,%20and%20a%20day%20left%20running.md), Fact 28).

**L3. The root never sits under a shared temporary area, and neither does the package area's location where L4a places it apart.** `/tmp` *"is wiped at every boot"* and `/var/tmp` is *"an easy place to leave one rental's data for the next tenant"* ([Six machines](Six%20machines%20—%20the%20container,%20the%20webhook%20and%20the%20ready%20action.md), Fact 6). This is [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#13-rules-that-bind-every-store) §1.3 rule 2 applied to material as well as to stores, which [Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#72-where-staged-material-sits-and-who-names-it) §7.2 S3 already does for staging.

**L4. A rental's second filesystem may be a quota or may be a real second device, and nothing here assumes either.** **Corrected 2026-08-22; this used to say *a quota rather than a second device*, flat, and that is now disproved.** Both shapes are observed. **The quota shape:** an attached volume presented as a separate filesystem, but *"`lsblk` shows one 1.8 T disk and the volume is a 20 GiB quota on a partition of it"* ([Two more boxes](Two%20more%20boxes%20—%20the%20volume,%20the%20full%20disk,%20and%20a%20day%20left%20running.md), Fact 6). **The real-device shape:** one seller delivers `/dev/vda1` at 97 GB on `/` and `/dev/vdb` at 787 GB on `/ephemeral` as standard — two separate virtio devices, the second eight times the root and empty on delivery ([The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20—%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md); Fact 28's clause). **So the root's mount is chosen for its available space read per mount, and never for an expectation that a second filesystem is faster, separate, independently full — or that it is not.** **On a machine of the second shape the conventional root is the wrong one by a factor of eight**, which is what makes L2's free-space reading load-bearing rather than tidy. **One caution travels with it:** the large mount's name on that seller is `/ephemeral`, **nothing was tested about whether it survives a stop, a start or a restart**, and the name is the seller's own claim rather than an observation — so size alone never chooses it.

**L4a. The package area sits on a writable mount chosen apart from the job area's, where L2's candidate set offers more than one. Added 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 R12.** The root and every other area keep L2's largest-available choice, unchanged; the package area takes the remaining candidate with the largest available figure, by L2's own per-path reading, provided that figure clears the 64 MiB control-state reserve (`Rented-server runtime` M4a) — and where no remaining candidate stands or clears it, the package area sits under the root as a sibling of the others, which is the arrangement every clause below already covers. The choice is made once per installation at the same moment as the root's and never re-chosen, and **where it places the package area apart, that location's path is recorded exactly as the root's is (L1); where it does not, nothing second is chosen or recorded** — the package area is a sibling under the root and its location follows from the root's recorded path, as the other four areas' locations do ([Server Setup](Server%20Setup.md#23-what-the-placement-is-given) R7b). **What it is for:** the package area holds the machine-local queue, the event journal, the per-job note and the captured streams ([State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#12-on-each-rented-server) §1.2) — the machinery that records what a render did — and with all five areas on one mount, a render filling its own output disk was guaranteed to starve that machinery at the exact moment it had the render's ending to record. On a machine of L4's real-device shape the render's outputs never touch the package area's mount. **Narrowed 2026-10-04 with [6.6](#66-where-the-job-output-folder-sits): that holds for a job whose output folder is P19's fallback; a job whose folder sits where its file points writes on whatever mount that folder is on, which can be the package area's** (P22). *Corrected later on 2026-10-04: it holds for a job whose output folder is in the job area; a fallback that uses the server's chosen fallback folder (P23) writes on that folder's mount, as a folder where the file points does.* **What it does not claim is what L4 already forbids claiming:** nothing here expects two mounts to be independently full, so where they are not — and on a machine with one writable mount — the separation is placement and not protection, M4a's reserve remains arithmetic against Smoozy's own stream capture claiming nothing against the render's outputs, and [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 21 states what still breaks. **No refusal is added on the strength of this**: what a placement refuses is M4a's existing rule and nothing wider.

**L5. The user is not asked which mount.** [Open Threads](../Groundwork/Open%20Threads.md) parks *where a job's folder actually sits on the machine* at this stage and says it becomes Specs' only if the user should be offered the choice. They are not, so nothing new reaches them; [Section 15](#15-moments-that-reached-the-creator) records the check. **Added 2026-10-04: where a job's folder sits is now the Specs', on the Creator's ruling of that date** — where the file's own outputs point ([6.6](#66-where-the-job-output-folder-sits)). They are still not asked which mount: L1 to L4a choose where Smoozy's own areas sit, the fallback job folders among them. *Corrected later on 2026-10-04: among them only where the fallback takes the job area — the user may now name a fallback folder per server (P23), which is a place they choose and not a mount Smoozy chooses.*

### 1.2 The areas, and what may never sit under a job folder

**L6. Five areas, and no path in any one of them is ever under another: four sit directly under the root as siblings, and the package area sits at L4a's location — directly under the root too, exactly where L4a chose no second mount.** **The fifth — the Blender area — was added 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close, routed by the runner from [Server Setup](Server%20Setup.md#13-unresolved) §13 item 8**, which composes it and had nowhere under this layout to put it. **None of the per-job record kinds in the second row below is named in [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#12-on-each-rented-server) §1.2's inventory of machine stores**, which is a seam rather than a fact — [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 3.

| Area | Holds | Contents owned by |
|---|---|---|
| **the package area** | the active-package pointer, the boot-registration record, the durable package-phase record, the installation identity, the machine-local queue, the operation and command records, the event journal, the machine-level desired states, the captured streams, the bot token file | `Rented-server runtime`, `Queue & History`, `State, identity and recovery` §1.2, `Security and licence` §10 |
| **the job-record area** | one **job record directory** per job identity: the immutable submission manifest, the recorded reading, the launch material, the expected-output inventory, the collision record, the two picks, the attempt records, and the machine-side derivatives | this document |
| **the job area** | one **job output folder** per job identity, and nothing else — **from 2026-10-04 only for a job that takes [6.6](#66-where-the-job-output-folder-sits) P19's fallback**; every other job's output folder sits where its file points; **and from later that day only where no chosen fallback folder is used (P23)**, which holds its job output folders the way this area does | this document; what lands in one is the render's |
| **the asset area** | the mirrored copies `Dependency Upload & Remap` places, and the remap material it keeps on the machine | `Dependency Upload & Remap` |
| **the Blender area** | one folder per Blender install Smoozy placed, and nothing else | `Server Setup` |

**L7. Nothing but what a render writes ever sits inside a job output folder.** Not the manifest, not the recorded reading, not the launch material, not the inventory, not a derivative, not staged transfer material for another job, and nothing of the package's. **Three separate promises break if this is relaxed and they break silently.** The expected-output inventory is compared against the folder's contents ([10.2](#102-the-reading-the-reckoning-is-made-against)), so anything else in there is a file the count did not expect. `Download` is told it has one folder to watch and brings it home whole, so anything else in there is a file the user did not ask for. And [Security and licence](Security%20and%20licence.md#10-what-smoozy-leaves-on-a-rented-machine) §10's protection of the bot token rests on the package area being outside every path the user browses, uploads, downloads or renders under.

**L7a. One exception, and it is the only one: a bake or cache the user uploaded, placed at the redirect's own target for that write place. Added 2026-08-22 on the Creator's ruling of Row 312.** [Dependency Upload & Remap](Dependency%20Upload%20&%20Remap.md#67-the-uploaded-cache-and-the-redirect--a-seam-between-two-closed-rulings-ruled-row-312) 6.7 settles that where a recorded write place also holds a baked result the user sent, the setting takes the **job-folder** value D20 assigns it, and the uploaded copy is placed there before the render starts. **So exactly one class of file that the render did not write may sit inside a job output folder**, and it is named rather than left as a general relaxation. **It is that class whether it was sent by the check or uploaded with the `.blend`** — a baked physics cache found on the machine where the file records it is copied in the same way ([5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) D21b-i; *added 2026-10-02*). **And the exception ends where the render writes. Added 2026-08-22, out of the third sanity sweep's verification round:** where the render recomputes over a placed copy, what sits at that path is what the render wrote, so it is not this exception's file at all and comes home under L7's ordinary rule — which is what [Dependency Upload & Remap](Dependency%20Upload%20&%20Remap.md#67-the-uploaded-cache-and-the-redirect--a-seam-between-two-closed-rulings-ruled-row-312) M22f's placed-copy record and [Download](Download.md) N5a's comparison against it exist to tell apart.

- **It is placed by Smoozy, before the render, under one of [5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) D20's four added write places, and nowhere else.** Not the regular output path, not a file output node's folder, not anywhere else under the job folder. A file arriving by any other route is still forbidden and the rule is unchanged for it.
- **L7's three promises are checked against it one at a time rather than waived together.** The **inventory** does not break: [7.3](#73-the-count) I14 already gives an added write place a folder with no per-file entries and no contribution to the count, so a placed bake is inside a folder the inventory never enumerated and no count expected anything there. The **security** promise does not break: it is about the package area being outside what the user browses, and this places the user's own file inside the user's own job folder. **`Download`'s** promise is the one that would have broken, and it is closed rather than accepted — [Download](Download.md)'s N5a skips what Smoozy itself placed, on the placement record, so the user does not re-download the bake they uploaded.
- **The completeness reckoning is unaffected and this is by construction rather than by exemption.** [10.2](#102-the-reading-the-reckoning-is-made-against) C7 compares the inventory's predicted paths against the walk's paths; a placed bake matches no predicted path and no predicted path goes missing because of it. C16's folder rule is untouched — nothing here makes a folder's existence stand for a file.
- **What is placed is a copy, and the master stays in the asset area.** So a second job placing the same bake copies again on the machine rather than uploading again, and **a render that recomputes the simulation anyway overwrites a copy and never the user's own file** — which is the half the discarded alternative could not give.

**L8. The reverse holds too: nothing outside the job output folder is ever counted, listed, watched or brought home as a job's output.** A derivative sits in the job-record area for exactly this reason, and [8.4](#84-the-derivatives-one-set-made-once-on-the-machine) says so where it is decided.

**L9. The job-record area is not the same object as the job output folder, and the shape's *distinct working folder* is the second of the two.** [The shape](The%20shape.md#each-rented-server) requires that *"The server package and its state live outside every job folder"* and that each job has *"an immutable submission manifest and a distinct working folder"*; L6 is that arrangement with the manifest given a home the render never writes into.

### 1.3 Ownership, and the confirmation that a placement worked

**L10. The whole root — and the package area's location, where L4a places it apart — is owned by the login the supervisor runs as, before hand-over.** A rental's second place to write arrives `root:root 755` and refuses that login until a setup step hands it over ([Two more boxes](Two%20more%20boxes%20—%20the%20volume,%20the%20full%20disk,%20and%20a%20day%20left%20running.md), Fact 6). **That step is `Server Setup`'s**; the requirement is this document's.

**L11. The root's placement is confirmed by a real write of at least one byte at the exact path, and never by a writability test and never by creating an empty file.** [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#13-rules-that-bind-every-store) §1.3 rule 4, on the observation that on a full filesystem *"the writability test **returns writable**, and **creating an empty file succeeds**"* and only writing at least one byte fails ([Six machines](Six%20machines%20—%20the%20container,%20the%20webhook%20and%20the%20ready%20action.md), Fact 28).

**L12. A placement of material the render will read is confirmed by reading it back under the login the render will run as.** That is the same shape as L11 in the other direction, and [5.4](#54-the-marker-because-a-launch-script-does-not-fail-loudly) is why it is not enough on its own.

**L13. The supervisor and every worker it starts, the render included, run as one login. That is a requirement this document places on `Server Setup` and on `Rented-server runtime`, and not a decision taken here** — worker identity is `Rented-server runtime`'s. L10's root and L12's read-back both name a login and the whole of [Section 2](#2-path-and-name-rules) rests on the layout L10 hands over; under two logins L10 gives the root to one of them and nothing says how the render writes into the job output folder at all.

---

## 2. Path and name rules

### 2.1 Every path on the machine is synthesised here and nowhere else

**N1. Every path Smoozy composes on a rented machine is composed by this document's rules**, except two, each owned and stated by the document that composes it: the staging path of [Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#72-where-staged-material-sits-and-who-names-it) §7.2 S4, and the paths inside the Blender area of [Server Setup](Server%20Setup.md#53-where-a-build-goes) B8 — **added 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close**, that area being one of the areas of [1.2](#12-the-areas-and-what-may-never-sit-under-a-job-folder) L6, sitting directly under the root on the chosen mount, covered by the hand-over of [1.3](#13-ownership-and-the-confirmation-that-a-placement-worked) like every other area, and holding nothing but Blender installs. This is [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#21-the-rules-that-bind-every-identity) §2.1 rule 7's requirement that the owner be named.

**N2. Every such path is under the root of [1.1](#11-one-root-one-mount-chosen-once) or under the package area's location of its L4a, and a path under neither is a fault rather than a case.** A builder that finds itself composing an absolute path outside both has found a fault in this section. **One exception from 2026-10-04: a job output folder placed where its file points, and every path under it and the run's folders [6.6](#66-where-the-job-output-folder-sits) P20 makes on the way to it.** They are composed by this document's rules all the same, and by nothing else. *Widened later on 2026-10-04: the exception covers a job output folder in the server's chosen fallback folder too, and the folders P20 makes on the way to it (P23).*

### 2.2 What a job folder is named for

**N3. A job output folder's name is the job identity's own textual form and, where a label was given, a bounded rendering of that label. A job record directory's name is the job identity's textual form alone.** The identity alone carries uniqueness; nothing anywhere parses a name back to recover a label; and no scene name, no file name, no date and no counter is any part of either. **The label's contribution is bounded in bytes**, inside [2.3](#23-the-length-budget-in-bytes-checked-before-a-path-is-used)'s budget and N7's prefix arithmetic, and **what cannot enter a name is substituted rather than refused** — the full label stays authoritative in the job's record and on the entry, which is where `Queue & History` shows it. That is `Queue & History`'s Row 50 promise kept and the unresolved half of its own spec answered.

**Neither of the two hazards cited nearby forbids that composition.** [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#21-the-rules-that-bind-every-identity) §2.1 rule 3 forbids deriving an **identity** from anything the user typed, and an identity and a label in one folder name derives nothing from the label. Fact 29's silent overwrite is a name made of the label **alone** — two jobs given the same label are refused by a plain make-directory call *"but the make-parents form accepts it silently — so the second job overwrites the first"* ([Six machines](Six%20machines%20—%20the%20container,%20the%20webhook%20and%20the%20ready%20action.md), Fact 29). With the identity in the name two same-label jobs get distinct names, and N9's plain create-directory rule already refuses a repeat.

**N4. A label is carried beside the identity, in the job's record, and is *"never load-bearing for uniqueness anywhere"*** ([State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#21-the-rules-that-bind-every-identity) §2.1 rule 3, in its own words). `Queue & History` shows it; N3 renders it into one name and nothing else composes with it.

### 2.3 The length budget, in bytes, checked before a path is used

**N5. The limits are in bytes and the check is made before any path is used.** Observed on 2026-08-17: **255 characters for a single name and 4095 for a whole path**, both refused **at open before any byte is sent**, both leaving nothing behind ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §7), matching the `f_namemax = 255` the file-transfer extension reports. The earlier six-machine batch measured the same limit **in bytes** — *"255 **bytes** per name (85 CJK characters)"* ([Six machines](Six%20machines%20—%20the%20container,%20the%20webhook%20and%20the%20ready%20action.md), Fact 29). **So the check counts bytes, never characters**, and a check written in characters passes a name the machine will refuse.

**N6. A refusal for length is never surfaced as the machine's own words.** Observed — the refusal for both over-limit cases is `Bad message`, *"which reads as protocol corruption rather than *name too long*"* ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §7). The check is Smoozy's own arithmetic against N5's budget, so the cause is established before the machine is asked; this is not mining a tool's output for a cause and does not cross [Security and licence](Security%20and%20licence.md#13-logging-redaction) §13 rule 5.

**N7. The prefix is bounded, and what is left is the budget the redirect has to fit the artist's arrangement into.** [Section 6](#6-bringing-a-destination-under-the-jobs-folder) deepens paths by construction, so the prefix is kept short deliberately, and it is **three segments under the chosen mount**: the root segment of [1.1](#11-one-root-one-mount-chosen-once), the **job-area segment** of [1.2](#12-the-areas-and-what-may-never-sit-under-a-job-folder) L6, and the job folder segment. A budget computed from two of those segments is a segment short, and N8's check is measured against this budget. **The job folder segment carries N3's bounded rendering of the label as well as the identity**, and that rendering's bound in bytes is what the name budget leaves after the identity, so a long label costs the label and never the arithmetic. **Where the job output folder sits where its file points ([6.6](#66-where-the-job-output-folder-sits), added 2026-10-04), the prefix is that run plus the job folder segment**, as long as the run is; N8 measures against it the same way, and a destination that does not fit there takes P19's fallback before it is ever a hold. *Widened later on 2026-10-04: in the server's chosen fallback folder the prefix is that folder plus the job folder segment, measured the same way, and a destination that does not fit there takes the job area (P23).*

**A destination whose deepest predicted file path exceeds the whole-path budget, or whose deepest single segment exceeds the name budget, is a destination Smoozy cannot bring under the job's folder**, and it is one of the three conditions of [9.1](#91-the-three-conditions-and-where-each-is-established). It is not a separate promise and it needs none: the spec already grants the hold for exactly this case.

**N8. The budget is measured against the predicted file path and not against the folder.** Names are known before the render starts ([7.1](#71-names-asked-of-blender-for-one-route-and-built-from-the-recipe-for-the-other)), so the deepest path a destination will produce is computable, and checking the folder alone would pass a destination whose files then fail at open.

### 2.4 Creating a folder: the plain form, never the make-parents form

**N9. Every directory this document creates is created with the plain create-directory operation, and never with the make-parents form.** Observed — the make-parents form *"returned **`rc=0`** against the already-existing folder"*, and *"the make-parents form — what a builder usually writes — is the silent collision path, where two jobs land in one folder with nothing reported"* ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §7).

**N10. Because a job identity is never reused, a refusal at a fresh job folder is a fault and not a case.** The file-transfer channel refuses the second creation with a **generic** failure code and *"gives no way to tell *already exists* from any other failure"* without a separate stat and the race that comes with it (same). **So no attempt is made to tell them apart**: any refusal fails the submission, which is the correct outcome for both readings. `Rendering`'s Row 2 already requires that a job either reaches the machine whole or does not exist. **From 2026-10-04 this is the fallback's rule only**: a refusal where the file points takes [6.6](#66-where-the-job-output-folder-sits) P19's fallback (P20), and a refusal at the fallback is this clause's fault. *Corrected later on 2026-10-04: a refusal at the server's chosen fallback folder takes the job area (P23); a refusal in the job area is this clause's fault.*

**N11. The parent directories are made once, at hand-over, so no area is ever created deep.** The five areas of [1.2](#12-the-areas-and-what-may-never-sit-under-a-job-folder) exist before the first job, each at the location L6 gives it. **A chain of directories is created only under a job output folder**, and the subfolders the render itself writes into are made there by **Blender**: *"Blender **makes** a place that is not there rather than refusing it"*, silently, for the regular output path and a node's folder alike, in all four versions ([What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md); *Dependencies 10*, *Writing 4*). **So Smoozy creates the two directories of M5 and M7 per job, and, before the render starts, each added write place's mapped folder together with any of its parents that do not yet exist — each made with N9's plain form, one segment at a time, and never with the make-parents form** ([5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) D21b-i, [1.2](#12-the-areas-and-what-may-never-sit-under-a-job-folder) L7a). **Every other directory under a job output folder is Blender's.** **And from 2026-10-04, above it**: where the job output folder sits where its file points, the run's missing folders are made by [6.6](#66-where-the-job-output-folder-sits) P20 the same way, one segment at a time in the plain form. *And from later that day, the missing folders of the server's chosen fallback folder the same way (P23).*

### 2.5 The output names are not touched, and the inventory is a statement about the machine

**N12. Smoozy writes no output name.** The redirect moves folders and leaves names alone, which is the spec's own rule; what Blender then does with a name is Blender's, and it differs by version — 5.0 replaces every separator in an output's name with an underscore, 4.5 and 5.1 and 5.2 make folders from it.

**N13. A rented Linux machine refuses nothing in a name but length.** Observed — a colon, an asterisk, a question mark, a pipe, a semicolon, letters outside the Latin alphabet, an emoji, a newline, a tab, a leading dash and a trailing space all reached disk unaltered ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §7.1). **A backslash becomes a folder separator, which is Blender's doing and not the machine's.**

**N14. The expected-output inventory is a statement about the machine and never about the user's computer.** Observed on 2026-08-17: seventeen names written on the rental came back as **fourteen files** on the Mac, three destroyed on arrival — names differing only in capitals collapsing to one file holding the **last** payload fetched, two spellings of one accented name doing the same — with **every fetch reporting success**, and a control in the same sitting proving the loss is at the receiving end (same, §7.1). **So frames rendered into distinct names can arrive as fewer files than were made, with nothing said.** The inventory names what the machine holds; **what becomes of a name on arrival is `Desktop app and delivery`'s receiving-filesystem collision judgement and `Download`'s response to it**, under Rows 303 and 307, and this document supplies the fact rather than answering them ([Section 13](#13-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document)).

**N15. Two lines any reading of a folder must survive, because the reckoning of [Section 10](#10-completeness-already-there-the-last-frame-the-exit-status-and-the-stop) takes one.** A newline in a file name **splits a listing across lines** on both routes, so no reading here is line-oriented; and the file-transfer client's text output is lossy where the protocol is not — *"no year or seconds for recent entries, no timezone ever, symlink targets dropped, and non-Latin names escaped into octal"* (same, §10). Every reading below takes the protocol's or the walk's own fields and never a rendered line.

---

## 3. The immutable submission manifest, and the per-job record

### 3.1 What the manifest holds

**M1. One manifest per job, written once and never rewritten.** It holds, and holds nothing that changes:

- the **job identity**, and the group identity where the job came from an all-scenes submission;
- the **Blender version** the job is to be rendered by, as `Render Settings` selected it;
- the **scene** the job is, and the **frame range** the job covers;
- the **settings payload** `Render Settings` composed, carried and not interpreted here;
- the reference to the **remap material** `Dependency Upload & Remap` keeps in the asset area, carried and not interpreted here;
- the **path of the Blender build** `Server Setup` picked, beside the version above ([Server Setup](Server%20Setup.md#55-taking-a-blender-off) B13);
- the **per-job reporting settings** fixed at submission ([Telegram](Telegram.md) V1), carried and not interpreted here;
- the **`.blend`'s path on the machine** and its **four-value file identity** at submission ([4.4](#44-the-file-identity-and-when-a-recorded-reading-goes-stale));
- the **recorded reading** and everything derived from it, where the file was read before submitting, and a statement that there is none where it was not ([4.3](#43-the-rule-runs-once-per-job-and-the-side-that-ran-it-writes-the-map));
- the **card make** the hardware reading `Servers` holds gave for the machine at submission, in M1b's form ([Rendering](Rendering.md#18-the-device-the-technology-and-the-ask) L23, L29). **Added 2026-09-20**;
- the **job output folder's path**, with [6.6](#66-where-the-job-output-folder-sits)'s case beside it — where the file points, or P19's fallback and which of its two cases — **or, where the file was not read before submitting, the statement that the folder is chosen at render start** (P21). *Changed 2026-10-04.* **Beside that statement, the server's chosen fallback folder as the server row held it at submission, or the statement that none was set** ([6.6](#66-where-the-job-output-folder-sits) P23); *added later on 2026-10-04*. Where the desktop chose, the path already says where the folder went and nothing more is carried. *Corrected later on 2026-10-04 with P23's reversal: beside the case, the field also carries whether the server's chosen fallback folder was passed over, yes or no.* *Added later on 2026-10-04 at Build (the Creator's second notes, piece 4): beside the render-start statement the field also carries N3's name for the job output folder, composed on the desktop, where the label is — the machine holds no label, so a name composed there would lose it — and the render worker joins it to the place P18 or P23 gives. A manifest written before this date names its folder as it always did, and a re-run reads that.*

**M1a. Four of M1's fields have a stated form, because closed documents read them and none of them may guess. Added 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close, routed by the runner.** The **frame range** takes one of two forms and never a third — the user's parsed integers, or the explicit statement *the file's own*, the second composed against the reading in force at the attempt ([Render Settings](Render%20Settings.md#93-a-job-submitted-with-no-read) Y9). The **scene** field carries, beside the name, that name's **anchor state** — anchored, with the identity of the reading its bytes came from, or to-anchor with its provenance ([Render Settings](Render%20Settings.md#94-anchoring-at-the-attempt) Y12) — which is what lets a name-shaped entry be validated against the reading in force before the render instruction. The **build path** is B13's matching rule at submission: **path beside version**, because two builds of one version answer the same version when asked, so a version alone cannot identify a build at launch. The **per-job reporting settings** are `Telegram` V1's, fixed at submission and travelling with the submission; the manifest is the vehicle because it is the one per-job material that is written once and never rewritten, which is the requirement V1 states. Routed from [Render Settings](Render%20Settings.md#13-unresolved) §13 item 15, [Server Setup](Server%20Setup.md#13-unresolved) §13 item 9, [Rendering](Rendering.md#9-unresolved) §9 item 14 and [Telegram](Telegram.md#21-unresolved) §21 item 12. **The `Render Settings` §13 item 15 citation is right for the first two fields and wrong for the reporting one — corrected 2026-08-31.** That item is the frame-range form and the scene field's anchor state and says nothing about reporting. **Who composes the reporting field is [Rendering](Rendering.md#4-submitting) S5a**, written that day because until then nobody did. **And `Render Settings` does now carry the control that supplies it: [Y16](Render%20Settings.md#95-the-per-job-reporting-choice-which-is-carried-here-and-is-not-a-payload-entry), added 2026-08-31 out of the same walk, which puts the choice on that page's submit strip and hands it to this manifest's own field rather than into the settings payload — so the reporting field has a composer and a control, and neither is here.** *(Corrected 2026-09-02: this sentence read that `Render Settings` carries no clause about the reporting choice anywhere, which was true when it was written and stopped being true on the day it was written.)*

**M1b. The card make's form, and why the field is here rather than read on the machine. Added 2026-09-20, out of a Build seam: [Rendering](Rendering.md#19-what-survives-smoozy-not-being-there) L29 already said the manifest carries the cards and M1's list had no card field, so the render worker had nothing to look the technology up from and every cards job ended at L23's loud failure before the render.**

**The form.** One value, and it is the make alone — never a copy of the hardware reading, never a card's identity, its bus location or its memory, and never the technology itself. It takes one of five states and never a sixth: **the makes the reading's cards carried**, as the keyable values [Servers](Servers.md#82-what-the-reading-is-made-of-and-every-part-of-it-is-a-trap-closed) H7 requires and never a wording to be parsed, with each distinct make once; or one of the four stated absences — **no card**, **cards present and unreadable**, **the cards could not be established** ([Servers](Servers.md#82-what-the-reading-is-made-of-and-every-part-of-it-is-a-trap-closed) H11, H12), and **no reading stood for this machine at submission**, which is [Servers](Servers.md#81-three-states-and-the-cheap-implementation-has-two) H1's *never read* and is an ordinary case rather than a gap. **The technology is not composed here and never travels**: L23's table is `Rendering`'s and is read on the machine, so the make is the whole of what crosses.

**Why the field and not a reading on the machine.** The make comes from the hardware reading `Servers` holds, which is a desktop reading taken once per primary connection over the pre-package surface ([Servers](Servers.md#83-where-the-reading-is-taken-and-why-no-blender-is-in-it) H14, H15) — so a machine asked at render time would need a second reading mechanism of its own, with H11's and H12's whole table in it, and the value the user was shown on the Render Settings page and the value the render named would be two readings rather than one object. **That is the fault this document already refuses for the map** (R7) and it is refused here for the same reason. It is also what L29 and P2 require: everything the launch needs is durable on the machine before the job exists, and a re-run reads the manifest rather than taking a reading.

**Why M3 is not broken by it, argued rather than asserted.** The make is an observed machine value, so the case has to be made. **It is material of the same kind as the four-value file identity**: a snapshot deliberately taken at submission, fixed for the life of one immutable record, on the machine it was taken of. **And a stale or wrong make cannot produce a wrong render, only a loud refusal** — a make with no row ends the attempt before the render instruction naming the make it could not serve (L23), and a make whose technology no hardware answers to is Blender's own `Error: Found no Cycles device of the specified type`, exit 1, nothing written (L24). **That is the same shape the manifest's Blender version already has** and the version confirmation of L14 is its answer. What D4 forbids is a copy that drifts and is believed; this one cannot be believed past the point where it is wrong.

**M2. Immutability is what `Rendering`'s Row 15 needs.** That row requires that none of what a render must have can be something Smoozy applies by hand each time a render begins, because a re-run happens with nobody connected. **A re-run reads the manifest; it never recomposes one.**

**M3. The manifest carries no observed value the machine still holds and no state.** It is material. [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#3-desired-state-and-observed-state) §3's D4 forbids a durable copy of an observed machine state, and nothing in M1 is one: the four-value file identity is a snapshot deliberately taken and compared against, which is [4.4](#44-the-file-identity-and-when-a-recorded-reading-goes-stale)'s whole subject. **Two fields are snapshots of that kind and not two rules — corrected 2026-09-20 with M1b, which adds the second.** The **card make** is the other, and M1b argues it at length rather than claiming it: it is taken once at submission, it is never compared against and never believed past a refusal, and both ways it can be wrong end the attempt loudly with nothing written. **What stays forbidden is unchanged**: no machine state, no figure the machine still holds and would answer differently about, and nothing a reader could take for current.

### 3.2 Why it does not sit in the job output folder

**M4. The manifest and everything derived from it sit in the job record directory, outside the job output folder**, for L7's three reasons: it would otherwise be a file the inventory did not expect, a file `Download` brought home, and a file inside the tree [Security and licence](Security%20and%20licence.md#10-what-smoozy-leaves-on-a-rented-machine) §10 requires the package area to be outside of.

### 3.3 It is published as one object

**M5. A job's whole submission material is staged in one directory and published by a single rename of that directory onto the job record directory's path.** `Rendering`'s Row 2 promises that a job *"either reaches the machine and joins the line, or it does not exist at all"*, which [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#92-three-tests-applied-in-order-and-the-answer-is-declared-rather-than-inferred) §9.2 test 1 makes atomic entire — and test 2 forbids reaching that by ordering or by care. **Fact 78's two-file half is answered and negative** — *"no single operation updates two independent files atomically"* — so several files cannot be published one at a time and called atomic. One directory rename is one operation.

**M6. That publication needs no extension, because a job identity is never reused.** The base protocol's rename *"refuses to replace an existing target"* and only the POSIX-rename extension overwrites ([Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step) §7.5 S16). **The target here never exists**, so the base operation is enough and [Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step) §7.5 S17's declared degradation does not reach this unit. It reaches the derivatives of [8.4](#84-the-derivatives-one-set-made-once-on-the-machine), which do replace.

**M7. The job output folder is created before the record is published, and an output folder whose record was never published is abandoned material — that case exactly, and never a folder whose record was published and later erased.** *(Narrowed 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 R9: as first written this clause keyed abandonment on the absence of a published record — which `Queue & History` X2's machine-side erase also produces — and instructed a removal the very sweep shape it cites refuses, S28's words being that a path whose record is gone is an orphan and is not automatically removed.)* It is removed by a record-driven sweep of the shape [Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends) §7.8 S28 defines, bounded to the job area: the sweep begins from the desktop's own record of the submission that stopped before M5's publication — `Rendering` S4's ordered unit names the step that stopped it, and T1 makes that job identity never retried — and removes the folder at the path the desktop itself composed at submission (M1), **because that record says the publication never happened, and never because of anything found by enumerating the job area** (S28, S29). A submission whose outcome is not yet settled is not in that state and its folder is left alone. **A folder whose job's record was published is outside this sweep for good, whether or not that record still stands**: X2's erase enumerates what it removes and the job output folder is not among it, and what remains after the erase is the user's renders under [Section 11](#11-cleanup-and-the-lifetimes-this-document-applies) K1's own row — the spec's *deleting history destroys the record, not the renders* — reached by no sweep, ever. The order is that way round so `Queue & History`'s *show outputs* has a folder to open on a job that has written nothing yet. **From 2026-10-04, this holds where the folder's path is known at submission** — the file was read before submitting ([6.6](#66-where-the-job-output-folder-sits) P21). **Where it was not, the render worker makes the folder at render start**, so a submission that stopped before publication made none and this sweep has nothing to remove for it. **The sweep removes the job output folder alone**: the run's folders P20 made on the way to it are where the file points, and stay. *So do the folders P20 made on the way to a chosen fallback folder (P23; added later on 2026-10-04); they are where the user pointed. And the sweep's bound above — "bounded to the job area" — is from that day the one path the desktop composed at submission, wherever 6.6 put it: where the file points, the chosen fallback folder or the job area.*

### 3.4 The attempt records beside it

**M8. Each render attempt writes one attempt record in the job record directory, durably, before the render starts.** It holds the attempt's number, the **frame list handed to the render in the order it was written**, the **map in force for that attempt** ([4.3](#43-the-rule-runs-once-per-job-and-the-side-that-ran-it-writes-the-map)), and the **file identity read at that moment**. **Two further fields, added 2026-09-04 out of a code round, and both are what N9a's telling reads rather than anything the render uses**: **the statement that the file changed before this attempt** — R13 already required it to be written here and this clause did not list it — and **the statement of whether this attempt kept any frame an earlier one had written** ([10.3](#103-the-zero-byte-rule-and-the-limit-it-does-not-cover) C12a). Each is a plain yes or no with no count beside it. It is written once and never rewritten; a later attempt writes a new one and no attempt record is ever removed while the job's record exists. The ordering in this clause is worth nothing without the durability, since a record still in a cache when the machine stops never existed.

*Durably* on this route is **three of the four parts** [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#13-rules-that-bind-every-store) §1.3 rule 6 names, not all four. *(Corrected 2026-09-03 at the code round; this clause cited rule 6 entire, and the fourth part was never performed and has no route.)* **What a write over the file-transfer connection keeps**: the bytes are written; the file itself is flushed to the storage device before the write is reported done; and where the write is published by a name, the rename follows that flush. **What it does not keep is rule 6's last clause** — the flush of the directory that afterwards holds the published name is **not performed**, neither for the job-record area a job's material is published into (M5) nor for the job record directory an attempt record is published into — **and nothing has established that it can be**: the file-transfer subsystem's flush takes an open file handle, that subsystem exposes no operation that opens a directory and hands one back, and whether a server would answer a flush against such a handle has been read nowhere in this project, so no route is written here from remembered behaviour. **What that costs**: a job record or an attempt record published and reported durable **can be absent after a power cut** — the third state `Rendering`'s Row 2 says does not exist — and an attempt record lost that way leaves the reckoning nothing to read back, so [C20](#10-completeness-already-there-the-last-frame-the-exit-status-and-the-stop) covers the whole range and the job is re-rendered whole at the user's expense.

**Where the connection never announced that flush at all, no job is placed on that machine.** *(Ruled 2026-09-03 at the code round under the Creator's standing delegation of 2026-08-31.)* The flush is an optional capability and a server that does not announce it is refusing nothing — but M8's requirement governs on this route, because [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#92-three-tests-applied-in-order-and-the-answer-is-declared-rather-than-inferred) §9.2 test 2 forbids reaching a promise by ordering or by care and writing without the flush would be exactly that. So the submission stops and the machine can take no job for its whole life. **That cost is paid with the cause stated rather than in silence**: what reaches the caller is *this connection never offered the operation*, established by Smoozy before the machine is asked, and never the machine's own refusal.

**M9. Attempt records are what makes Row 134 answerable at all** — the list has to survive an interruption, a reboot and Smoozy being closed, and it is durable on the machine before the render begins. **And they are what keeps Row 137's two arrangements separable**: where the file changed and the map moved, both maps are on the machine, each named by the attempt that used it.

---

## 4. One reading of the file, and everything reads it

**This section exists because five separate promises read the same file and must not disagree about what they found**: the folders shown, the count, the main-output walk, the preview's candidates, and the re-run's already-there test. They are not five readings. **There is one reading per job, recorded once, and every one of them reads that record.**

### 4.1 What the reading is

**R1. One invocation of the Blender the manifest names, headless, over the command channel, returning one document between sentinels.** Observed on 2026-08-17 in Blender 4.5.9, 5.0.1 and 5.2.0: one SSH command, a named Blender already on the machine, no window and no display server, **all three payloads out of one read**, returned as one JSON document between sentinels because Blender's own chatter shares that stream ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §1). **About 3.2 to 3.8 seconds end to end including a fresh connection; the reading itself 0.09 to 0.12 seconds; 15 to 26 KB back** (same, §1.5).

**R2. The reading is taken by the version that will render.** Four separate things move across the version boundary and each changes the answer rather than its wording ([What a file can be asked before it renders](../Blender%20Research/Across%20topics/What%20a%20file%20can%20be%20asked%20before%20it%20renders.md)). Observed in the same sitting: the second scene's engine read back `BLENDER_EEVEE_NEXT` in 4.5.9 and `BLENDER_EEVEE` in 5.0.1 and 5.2.0.

**R3. Where the reading runs is decided by whether the file has been read before submitting**, and by nothing else. At pre-load it runs from the desktop over the connection; at render start it runs on the machine as a step of the render worker before the render is launched. **The invocation and the payload are the same on both sides**, except that at pre-load, where no job scene is named, the payload holds every scene's half (R4b). *(Changed 2026-10-10 on the Creator's seventh notes, note 1.)*

### 4.2 What the reading produces

**R4. One recorded reading, and it is the only thing anything downstream reads.** It holds, per version-correct evaluation:

1. **The gating conditions.** The Compositing switch; whether a compositing tree is present; whether that tree holds a live final output node — the **Composite** node in 4.5 and the **Group Output** node in 5.0, 5.1 and 5.2, which have no Composite node at all; whether that node is muted, which stops the compositor's result reaching the file in 4.5 and makes no difference in the other three; **4.5's old Use Nodes switch**, which decides nothing from 5.0; and, from **5.2 alone**, the Save Output switch. **Only the Compositing switch reaches a file output node**: a tree whose final output node is missing or muted still writes its nodes. **One further condition, added 2026-09-01 out of the repaired Blender research: a scene whose render goes out through its own sequencer holding a Scene strip writes nothing from its file output nodes at all**, even though it is the scene being rendered ([Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 13). **That is a second thing reaching a file output node and this clause said there was only one.** Observed in the same sitting, with 4.5.9 differing exactly as the research records ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §1.4).
2. **The destination set.** Two kinds, named apart inside the set because two of [9.1](#91-the-three-conditions-and-where-each-is-established)'s conditions read only the first. **The render's own output destinations**: the regular output path where it writes, and for each file output node that will run, one destination for each connected output — or **one destination for the whole node** where it is set to gather its outputs into a single multi-layer file. A muted node contributes none and does not even make its folders; an output with nothing connected contributes none; a node inside a node group contributes once wherever it sits. **And every further write place item 6's walk reaches** — the bake output path, the point cache, the geometry-nodes bake folder and the sequencer proxy folder — ~~one destination each~~, per [5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) D20. **Corrected 2026-10-02: for each of D20's added write places the set holds one destination for each distinct location the file records for that place, and one for the carriers that record none ([5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) D21c). Each carrier is set to the folder of its own recorded location.** One destination per place mapped every bake of a kind to one folder, so a second sent folder had no place to be copied to and every attempt failed before the render — observed at the desk on 2026-10-02, a file with geometry-nodes bakes in four folders, all four sent (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/Does the redirect cut the render off from bakes — what was seen.md`, R2). Each such destination is an ordinary one at [6.3](#63-the-rule) to [6.5](#65-the-invariant-the-rule-never-merges-two-destinations-that-were-distinct), and D21f reads each one's folder as it reads a place's.
3. **Every scene a sequencer strip reaches, read the same way.** A scene reached through a **Render Layers node** contributes nothing — its compositor does no work and its own nodes stay silent, whatever its own compositing switch says. A scene reached through a **strip in the video sequencer** contributes its own destinations, read against **its own** gating conditions rather than the job's. Observed in the same sitting: a scene pulling in another read out as `{"via_render_layers_node": [], "via_sequencer_strip": [{"strip":"to_second","scene":"Second","use_sequencer":true}]}`. **A reading that stops at the job's own scene is short in five places at once**, which is Row 125 entire.
4. **Whether the job's compositing tree is shared with another scene**, which arises from 5.0 on and never in 4.5, and which scene is named inside it.
5. **The per-frame set.** Every gating condition and every view layer's renderability is evaluated **at every frame the job covers**, not once for the job. Observed: the per-frame view-layer set came out of the same single read — `'10': ['VL_Always','VL_Sometimes'] … '22': ['VL_Always'] … '50': ['VL_Always']` — and the keyframes read out as data as well, so it works either by stepping frames or by reading the curve (same, §1.3). **That is also what covers 4.5's one way of gating a node frame by frame**, the old Use Nodes switch taking a keyframe: the same stepping already required for view layers reads it, so the count is worked out per frame in all four versions rather than being firm from 5.0 and known-wrong in 4.5. **Narrowed 2026-10-03, out of the Creator's notes on 0.1.8 (note 4): a scene is moved to the frames of its span only where something on it can change from frame to frame** — the scene carries animation data (keys, drivers or strips, which is where a view layer's switch, a render setting and 4.5's Use Nodes are animated); its compositing tree, or any group tree inside it, carries animation data; or one of its timeline markers names a camera. **Otherwise the values read once, at the frame the file was saved on, stand for every frame of its span.** The job's own scene follows the same rule; its output path at each frame is asked with the frame given, and needs no move. Label: **observed in a test** — 214 files, each read by each of the four versions both ways, gave byte-identical readings before and after, apart from one file's per-run temporary folder name ([Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 22a). A value changed at each frame by something the condition does not look at — a script run on a frame change, where scripts may run — would be read as still; that case is `Render Settings` Q3's to carry. **The job's own choices apply before anything reads this set. Added 2026-10-04, out of the Creator's note 14 on the test copy.** The per-frame view-layer set is what the file says; **before the map, the inventory, the holds and the six things are composed from it, each view layer the job pins takes its pin at every frame — a layer pinned on renders at every frame the job covers, a layer pinned off at none** — the rule [Render Settings](Render%20Settings.md#42-the-pin-and-the-mechanism-that-holds-it) V4 to V6 already make true at the render and `Queue & History` B1 already applies to the plan total. **One rule in one place serves all of them**, so the count, the folders and the plan cannot disagree about which layers render. Where the file was read before submitting, the pins are the page's at the moment of submission; where the reading runs at render start, they are the manifest's. **On the desktop after Submit, the box a queue entry opens counts with the pins the job was submitted with, read off the kept setup on the local job record** — the very payload the manifest carries, composed once at submission and written with the record in the same act (`Rendering` A1, `Queue & History` N19's two-copy shape) — **so no second copy of the pins is kept, and nothing reads the file's own switches in their place.** A record holding no kept setup has no pins and counts the layers the file switches on, as before; nothing is made up for it. *Added 2026-10-04, the Creator's second notes, piece 2b: the queue's box said 4 PNG where the job wrote 2. No auditor has seen it.* The recorded reading itself stays what the file says, so a re-run that reads the file again applies the same pins again. **What it cost before:** a job that switched two layers off owed files for both, auto-download waited for files that were never coming, and the count read 5 of 7 for good.
6. **The recorded locations**, taken by **a walk over Blender's own marks on its settings**. That is Row 119's second bite and it is settled by observation rather than by argument: the flat list and the one-pass map **reach an ocean modifier's cache path and nothing else recorded for writing** — not the render output path, not a compositor output node's folder, not a point cache, not a geometry-nodes bake folder — while the walk over Blender's own path marks reached all twelve **including every write location**, in all three versions (same, §1.2). **Narrowed 2026-09-01**, out of the repaired Blender research: this read *miss every write location*, flat, and [What has to travel with a file](../Blender%20Research/Across%20topics/What%20has%20to%20travel%20with%20a%20file.md) now carries the one exception (*Dependencies 17*), **with what separates the ocean cache from the other four not established.** **The decision is unmoved and is now better supported** — one write place out of six is not a route to the other five — but the exception is carried rather than dropped, because a later reader meeting an ocean cache moved by a bulk command would otherwise find this document saying it cannot happen. **So the reading that produces the folders shown is never the file's own list of outside files.**
7. **The destination map**, the collision record, the predicted names, the count, the beauty walk's outcome and the two picks — [Section 6](#6-bringing-a-destination-under-the-jobs-folder), [Section 7](#7-the-expected-output-inventory-the-count-and-the-collision-record) and [Section 8](#8-the-main-output-the-preview-source-and-the-derivatives).
8. **Whether the job's scene holds a simulation that is not baked. Added 2026-10-02 for `Rendering` L10b (the Creator's decision A, Creator-stated).** Yes where any of three holds: **a geometry-nodes simulation zone that is not baked**; **a point cache that is not baked**, on any carrier the walk reaches, the rigid-body world's included; **a fluid domain whose cache type is Replay**, which is never baked. The baked state is read through R18's record, as the file had it, and never as the handler left it. **Where the names come from.** A point cache's `is_baked` was read off 4.5.12 and 5.2.2 at the desk and the reading already reads it (D21b-i); the Replay names are D20's fifth place's, read off all four. **No plan holds the names for a simulation zone or for whether it is baked**, nor `is_baked` in 5.0.1 and 5.1.2: **the builder reads them off the four Blenders at the desk before building this item**, recorded the way the added write places' lookup was, and an unsourced name stops the composition rather than being guessed. A fluid domain of another cache type that is not baked is not in this item; what it renders is not established. **A recorded reading that carries no item 8 — taken before it existed — reads as no.** **Built and corrected 2026-10-02 by the machine side of decisions A and B** ([Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 38g). The names were read off all four Blenders and are the same in each: a simulation zone is a bake of a nodes modifier whose `node` is a `GeometryNodeSimulationOutput`; `is_baked` is on a point cache in 5.0.1 and 5.1.2 as in the other two. **No Blender names whether a geometry-nodes bake holds data** — `NodesModifierBake` carries no such property in any of the four. **So every simulation zone counts, baked or not**: stepping a baked one rendered the same frame as not stepping it (observed, 4.5.12 and 5.2.2), so a baked zone pays the stepping time and nothing else. **A particle system of hair with no hair dynamics is left out**: it simulates nothing, and its cache is never baked. The walk is the job's own scene's objects, and that scene's rigid-body world.

**R4c. A first-frame check of a job whose only output is a video is read as a picture. Added 2026-10-27, Creator-stated (the nineteenth notes, notes 3 and 9; the [Rendering spec](../Specs/Rendering.md), *The first-frame check*). No auditor has seen it.** Where the job's payload carries `Render Settings` Y1b's entry, the render-start reading is told so, and it asks one question of the job's scene before it reads anything else: **does the scene write only a video?** Yes where the scene's regular output path writes a video, where in 5.2 and later the scene's own *write frames* switch is on, and where no file output node stands unmuted in the job's scene or in any scene a sequencer strip reaches. Where yes, the reading sets the scene's output to a still picture — from 5.0 the media type to *picture* first and then the format, in 4.5 the format alone; the format is PNG, the scene's own starting format in every version (the Blender research's *File output nodes*, its hand test on the rented Linux machine) — and only then reads items 1 to 8, so every destination, every frame's name and the map are a picture's. It says in the reading that it did, and `Rendering` L10c makes the same switch in the render's own material. **Where no**, nothing is switched and the reading is what it always was. **The pre-load reading never carries the entry**, because no job exists yet. **Nothing is saved**: the file keeps its video settings for the second job. A file output node in a compositor that would not run counts as unmuted here, so such a job keeps its check as it is; that is the price of asking before the gating is weighed (`Rendering` L10c says what it costs).

**R4a. A recorded location crosses as bytes, and no reading of one may raise, substitute or skip. Added 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close, routed by the runner from [Dependency Upload & Remap](Dependency%20Upload%20&%20Remap.md#12-unresolved) §12 item 18.** A location is exempt from the rule that a file's text is all written one way — *"reading a location out can fail where reading anything else out would not, because a location is exempt from the rule that a file's text is all written one way — real disks hold names that are not"* (*Dependencies 9*). **So item 6's locations, and every location inside item 2's destination set, are carried out of the reading as the bytes the file holds** — never decoded, never replaced by a substitute character, never dropped from the payload, and never the cause of the reading failing. This is N15's discipline for listings applied to the payload rather than to the transport. **Both failures it prevents are silent**: a reading that raises makes a file Blender opens perfectly well *could not be read*, with the version named, permanently; a reading that substitutes or skips keys the wrong record, compares the wrong bytes, mirrors a wrong spelling and matches no pair at the remap, so the asset is missing at a render that reports success. The one document between sentinels of R1 carries them so, and every reader downstream compares them as bytes.

**R4b. At pre-load, where no job scene is named, the one reading also holds items 1 to 5, 7 and 8 for every scene in the file, each keyed by the scene's name and composed exactly as for a named scene, so whichever scene is ticked has its own; the render-start reading is unchanged. Added 2026-10-10 on the Creator's seventh notes, note 1.** The Specs already promise it — *Pre-load reads every scene in the file*, and Smoozy states what the file will write ([Render Settings](../Specs/Render%20Settings.md)) — and R4 delivered it only for the scene the file was saved showing. **It is the same single opening of the file; no second Blender starts.** Each scene's per-frame set is recorded inside the frame walk that already runs over every scene, so nothing is stepped twice.
