The desktop keeps one such half per scene for each (server, file) and reads the ticked scene's; where several are ticked, what is offered to download is every format any of them writes. **A reading from a machine program older than this clause holds one scene, and is read as before.** That one scene's half, read beside every other's in one opening, is the same as a reading naming that scene alone is **not established**: [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 30.

**R5. *Does this file have file output nodes* is not asked anywhere.** What is recorded per node is whether it **will run**, which is items 1 and 2 together.

**R6. The reading was checked against a real render rather than trusted.** It predicted five files per frame and 105 over 21 frames; the render produced 42 in the first node's folder, 42 in the second's and 21 at the regular output — **105 exactly, with no folder at all for the muted node**, and the unconnected output wrote nothing (same, §1.4).

### 4.3 The rule runs once per job, and the side that ran it writes the map

**R7. The destination map is computed once per job, on whichever side ran the reading, and the render applies it rather than deriving one.** This is the whole of Row 122's answer and it is a structural one: the folders the user was shown and the folders the render writes into are **the same object**, not two computations that have to agree.

- **Where the file was read before submitting**, the desktop composes the recorded reading and the map, and both go into the manifest at submission. The render applies the map. Nothing is recomputed.
- **Where it was not**, the manifest says so and carries no map. The render worker takes the reading after the load and before the render, composes the map, and writes it into the attempt record and the job record. The queue entry then carries the six things ([9.3](#93-what-the-machine-produces-for-a-person-who-is-not-there)).

**R8. That is consistent with `Rendering`'s Row 7 rather than against it.** That row settles that the redirect *"happens when the render starts, not when the job is submitted"*, because the rule looks at every destination at once and a job can be submitted without the file having been read. **Both halves hold**: the redirect is applied on the machine at render start in every case, and what is applied is a map computed on whichever side had the reading. The lever is always the machine's; the arithmetic is done once.

**R9. Nothing anywhere recomputes a map that exists.** A builder that finds a second evaluation of the shared-run rule has found the fault Row 122 exists to prevent — *"the user checks where their work is going, agrees, and it goes somewhere else."*

### 4.4 The file identity, and when a recorded reading goes stale

**R10. A live job holds a snapshot, not a mutable file.** The manifest is immutable and carries the `.blend`'s **four-value file identity** as it was when the reading was taken: **size, inode, modification time and change time together**. Fact 21 is answered and it is exactly this tool — a file on the server *"can be told not to have changed from size, inode, modification time and change time together"*, a save-by-rename changes the inode, and *"a hand-restored modification time still moves the change time, which cannot be set back"*; posed live, a byte changed mid-fetch and *"the copy returned 0 and delivered a different file"*, with only the timestamps revealing it ([Six machines](Six%20machines%20—%20the%20container,%20the%20webhook%20and%20the%20ready%20action.md), Fact 21).

**R11. This is not a reading of state and it is the same mechanism another closed document already uses.** [Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#74-resume-never-trusts-a-partial-it-has-not-verified) §7.4 S13 records all four values when a download is arranged and compares all four before a resume. One shape rather than two.

**R12. A fresh check is required at exactly two moments and at no others.** At the start of every render attempt, before the map is applied; and when the reading is taken. **Not** on a reconnection, **not** on a listing, **not** on an elapsed period, and **not** because a transfer touched the folder.

**R12a. R12 is scoped to the job pipeline's use of the recorded reading, and a display-only comparison is outside it. Added 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close, routed by the runner from [Render Settings](Render%20Settings.md#13-unresolved) §13 item 14.** *At exactly two moments and at no others* fixes when a **fresh check is required of the pipeline** — what a manifest, a map, an inventory or an attempt may rest on. A comparison taken for a page's own display, touching no manifest, triggering nothing in the pipeline and writing nothing durable, is neither required by R12 nor forbidden by it: [Render Settings](Render%20Settings.md#15-noticing-the-file-changed-while-connected) Q13–Q16 is that case and it stands. **What R12 still forbids absolutely is a pipeline moment taking its freshness from such a comparison** — the two moments are the pipeline's own and no display reading substitutes for either.

**R13. A recorded reading is stale when, and only when, the four values differ, or the Blender version it was taken by is not the version the manifest names.** R2 requires the reading to be taken by the version that will render and [Render Settings](../Specs/Render%20Settings.md) permits that version to be changed afterwards, which no file value moves, so the version is one of the staleness tests rather than an assumption around them. **Where a recorded reading is stale at submission the manifest carries no map**, and R7's second bullet already covers what follows. Where they differ at the start of an attempt, the map in the manifest is not applied: the render worker takes a fresh reading after the load, composes a fresh map, and writes both into that attempt's record beside a statement that the file changed. **The previous attempt's map is kept**, so one job's folder holding two arrangements of subfolders is a recorded fact with each arrangement named by the attempt that made it, rather than a layout nobody can account for.

**R14. What a re-run then does for the user was never this stage's, and it is settled at Specs. Re-pointed 2026-09-02; this cited the spec's `[PENDING: what a re-run does when the file changed and the folder layout moved with it]` as naming no stage, and that marker is gone.** [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md) reads *"What Smoozy does then is settled 2026-09-02"*: the re-run goes ahead, and **what was settled is the telling** — the job's entry says the file changed between attempts and says what became of the frames that were already finished. It is said, not asked. **The telling is `Queue & History`'s and this document composes none of it: [`Queue & History` §10](Queue%20&%20History.md#10-the-note-the-figure-and-what-the-read-fills-in) N9a, written 2026-09-02 for Row 516.** [Section 10](#10-completeness-already-there-the-last-frame-the-exit-status-and-the-stop)'s already-there test is made against **the map in force for the attempt being started** and against no other, which is the only reading that is not a guess; [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 9 records it.

**Corrected 2026-09-04, out of a code round, and the correction is the spec's before it is this clause's.** This clause said the entry *"says that frames finished under the earlier arrangement were rendered again rather than reused"*, flat, because the spec said it flat. **That rests on a premise that fails, and fails on the common case.** The map is composed from the resolved destination folders and nothing else ([6.2](#62-resolution-comes-before-the-rule) P4, [6.3](#63-the-rule) P6 to P9), and the predicted names from I2's recipe; **a file edited in a way that moves no destination — a material, a light, the camera — is stale under R13 and yields a map identical to the one before it.** Every predicted path is then the same, [10.2](#102-the-reading-the-reckoning-is-made-against) C7's walk finds the earlier attempt's frames at exactly those paths, and C12 counts them already there: **they are reused, not rendered again.** C12 reads presence and size and nothing about which attempt or which file wrote a frame, which is the whole reason the fresh map's being identical is enough.

**What this document supplies to the telling is therefore two facts and not one:** R13's statement that the file changed, and **C12a's statement of whether any frame an earlier attempt wrote was kept by this one.** Both are written into the attempt record ([3.4](#34-the-attempt-records-beside-it) M9) and reach the entry by `Queue & History` N9's route. **Nothing about the already-there test moves.** Teaching C12 which attempt or which file wrote a frame would change what the user gets and what they pay for, and only the Creator rules on that ([Method](Method.md)); it is raised at [Section 15](#15-moments-that-reached-the-creator) and built nowhere.

**R15. Which files a live job depends on is the manifest's `.blend` and the resolved set `Dependency Upload & Remap` keeps.** This document supplies the first, with its identity and its staleness rule; that module owns the second under its own rows, and `File Browser`'s live-job warning reads both.

### 4.5 What the reading costs, and one thing it is not established not to disturb

**R16. The cost is not a reason to skip it.** R1's figures are an upper bound taken on a container with three testers sharing four cores.

**R17. Whether a read disturbs a running render is NOT answered.** Five reads taken during a 21-frame render all succeeded with an empty error map and the render finished all 21 frames and all 105 files, but the run totalled 155.0 s against a control's 79.8 s while the reads spanned only the first 40 s, and three testers shared four cores — *"the two cannot be separated"* ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §1.6). **What stands is that a read against a running render completed correctly every time; the magnitude is untested.** Scheduling and exclusion between the read worker and a render worker are `Rented-server runtime`'s, which owns arbitration for every worker class that can contend; [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 11.

**R18. A read writes nothing beside the `.blend`. Added 2026-10-02, out of step 10c's desk tests.** **Every Blender Smoozy starts on the user's file to read it — the reading of this section, *Read the file*, and `Dependency Upload & Remap`'s check — carries the same load-time handler as the render ([Rendering](Rendering.md#13-the-window-and-the-order-of-the-line) L8b), pointing every Replay fluid cache and every disk cache at a throwaway folder in that run's own working area**, and the run removes the folder when it ends. **Opening a Replay file wrote frame 1 beside the `.blend`**, before any script ran — Blender opened with nothing else wrote it; observed at the desk, 2026-10-02, in 4.5.12 ([Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 38c). R4 item 5's stepping through the job's frames is **expected** to write a Replay or unbaked disk cache for every frame it steps; not observed. **Nothing the reading reports is taken from those caches**, so its output is expected to be the same, byte for byte, with the handler as without it. **Corrected 2026-10-02 by simulation fix piece 5 — a read does report the settings the handler changes** ([Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 38f): a cache's folder, whether *external* is on, whether it is baked. Read as they stand after the handler, the reading at the render's start and the check both reported the throwaway folder (5.2.2). **So the handler keeps every value it changes, and whether a point cache is baked, as the file had them before it writes anything, and every read of those settings goes through that record**; a list of paths has each throwaway folder told back to the value it replaced. With it, all three reads' documents were the same with the handler as without it, and nothing was written beside the `.blend` — where before, the reading at the render's start and *Read the file* each wrote 96 files beside a Replay smoke's and 48 beside an unbaked cloth's, and the check wrote the smoke's frame 1 (4.5.12 and 5.2.2).

---

## 5. The redirect

### 5.1 The route is launch material, and not a copy

**D1. The redirect is carried out by material handed to Blender at launch, which sets each destination's own value in the running Blender before the render instruction runs. No copy of the artist's `.blend` is made.**

**D1a. Two kinds of place are set as the file loads, and not by the material after it. Added 2026-10-02, out of step 10c's desk tests.** A handler registered by a script placed before the file runs as the file loads ([Rendering](Rendering.md#13-the-window-and-the-order-of-the-line) L8b, which owns the line and the handler). **It sets each Replay fluid cache's folder** (D20's fifth place) **and the geometry-nodes bake folders** to their values in the map. The map, the values and the job-folder copies are this document's; only the moment differs. Observed at the desk, 2026-10-02 ([Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 38c):

- **A geometry-nodes bake folder set after the file has loaded is ignored, and the folder the file recorded is read** — even with the modifier switched off and on, or the object retagged. **Set as the file loads, it is read**: identical to the plain render, with no bake beside the `.blend`. 4.5.12 and 5.2.2.
- **A Replay fluid cache writes frame 1 beside the `.blend` the moment the file opens**, before any script runs. With its folder set after load, frame 1 was still written beside the `.blend`; **set as the file loads, nothing was written there** and all of it went to the job folder. 4.5.12.

**Which geometry-nodes bakes are set is the Creator's to rule** (decision B; [Dependency Upload & Remap](Dependency%20Upload%20&%20Remap.md#67-the-uploaded-cache-and-the-redirect--a-seam-between-two-closed-rulings-ruled-row-312) M22a). The two forms are: **every** geometry-nodes bake folder set to its job-folder value, with every geometry-nodes bake on the machine — sent or uploaded beside the `.blend` — copied there as D21b-i copies a physics cache; or **only** the folders a sent bake was copied for, the rest left as the file has them and read where they are. **The material sets no geometry-nodes bake folder** under either form.

**Ruled by the Creator on 2026-10-02: only the folders a sent bake was copied for** (decision B, Creator-stated). A geometry-nodes bake uploaded beside the `.blend` is read where it is; only a bake sent by *Check dependencies* is copied into the job folder and its folder set there by the handler. **This narrows [Dependency Upload & Remap](Dependency%20Upload%20&%20Remap.md#67-the-uploaded-cache-and-the-redirect--a-seam-between-two-closed-rulings-ruled-row-312) M22a's ruling of 2026-08-22 for geometry-nodes bakes only.** So the handler leaves a geometry-nodes carrier whose recorded location no sent copy names as the file has it, and does not raise for it; the Replay folder still raises where its table has no value. A geometry-nodes simulation never baked writes nothing anywhere (entry 38e), so nothing the render writes is left outside the job's folder by this.

**D2. The choice was open and these are the constraints that closed it.**

1. **The launch lever is not optional and already exists.** `Rendering`'s Row 22 requires Simplify and its three settings to be imposed at launch because **no version offers a command-line argument for any of them**, and the same holds for the sample count, the resolution percentage and every other setting with no argument ([What can be set from outside](../Blender%20Research/Across%20topics/What%20can%20be%20set%20from%20outside.md)). **So the copy route would not remove the launch material; it would add a file beside it.**
2. **A copy has to sit beside the original, and that puts a file Smoozy made into the folder the user browses.** Row 120 states the cost: a `.blend` carrying paths written relative to where the file sits may not find its assets from a copy in another folder. A file appearing beside the artist's own that they did not put there, which `File Browser` would show, `File Browser`'s live-job warning would have to cover, and the user could move or delete, is a cost no spec grants them.
3. **Everything that names the file afterwards speaks of the uploaded file.** History, *show outputs* and the file browser all name the `.blend` the user uploaded. The launch-material route leaves exactly one `.blend` for them to name.
4. **The copy route's save silently rewrites locations nobody asked to change.** Observed on 2026-08-17: **saving remaps paths relative by default** ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §2.4).
5. **Nothing is bought by copying.** Either route has to write each destination's setting individually and per version ([5.3](#53-each-destination-setting-is-written-on-its-own)); the copy route does that in a Blender and saves, the launch route does the same thing in the Blender that is about to render.

**D3. The condition the copy was parked against has gone, and the research says so in the plainest terms.** There is no command-line **argument** for a file output node's place in any version, *"**but a script handed to Blender at launch reaches the node's values, and the render that follows uses them**"* — hand-confirmed in all four versions on the Linux machine (Row 120, citing *Writing 9*).

**D4. Setting values at launch does not change how the render is started, and that clash is already settled.** [What can be set from outside](../Blender%20Research/Across%20topics/What%20can%20be%20set%20from%20outside.md): *"**Setting a value from a script is not the same as starting the render from one.**"* `Rendering`'s Row 11 keeps the reporting route; this document adds nothing to it and takes nothing from it.

**D5. The artist's `.blend` is not written to by anything here.** The redirect sets values in a running Blender and nothing saves the file, and *"a wholesale rewrite of a file's recorded locations shows in the running Blender and never reaches the file on disk unless it is saved"* (*Reading a file 9*). Observed deliberately on 2026-08-17: with the rewrite live in the running Blender, a fresh process reading the same file off disk saw every original path, and the digest was unchanged; and the artist's own file was digested before and after all seven runs and never changed, **including the two runs where nothing was rewritten, which is the control showing the check would have noticed** ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §2.3).

**D6. What is owned here is the material; how it is handed to Blender is `Rendering`'s launch contract.**

### 5.2 What the launch material carries, and it is composed per version

**D7. The material is composed for one named Blender version and names it, and the version is confirmed before Blender is launched.** The render worker confirms that the Blender it is about to launch is the version the manifest names, and where it is not **the attempt fails before the render instruction** and no launch happens. That requirement sits on the launch, which is `Rendering`'s under D6. Where the material is handed to another version anyway it does nothing — which is a second line of defence and not the mechanism, because D17 forbids this document resting on a launch failing loudly. That is the direct answer to Row 118's version clause.

**D8. The two values a file output node keeps are not named the same across the boundary, and the redirect must know which.** Observed on 2026-08-17, per version, on disk, by a separate process: the property carrying a node's folder is **`base_path` in 4.5.9** and **`directory` in 5.0.1 and 5.2.0** ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §2.1). **5.1 was not exercised**; [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 4.

**And in 4.5 that property is not always a folder, which is the half this clause did not carry until 2026-09-01.** Out of the repaired Blender research: *"in 4.5, when the node is gathering its outputs into one multi-layer file, that base path is used as the start of the file name and not only as a folder — without a trailing separator the file lands **beside** the folder rather than in it"*, and *"anything writing a folder into that property in 4.5 should end it with a separator"* ([Where a render's files go](../Blender%20Research/Across%20topics/Where%20a%20render's%20files%20go.md), *Writing 9*, on *File output nodes 6*). Label: **observed in a test**.

**So the redirect writes that property with a trailing separator, always, in 4.5**, whatever the node's gathering mode — the rule is unconditional because the mode is a per-node setting the redirect would otherwise have to read and branch on, and a separator on a property being used as a folder changes nothing. **Without it the redirect produces the exact harm it exists to prevent**: the job's own file written outside the job's folder, one level up and beside it, on a machine where nothing else looks for it. **And I2's recipe then predicts a name that is never written**, so [10.5](#105-nothing-here-reads-an-exit-status-as-everything-was-written)'s already-there test and C27's completeness statement both report the frame absent — the redirect failing and the reckoning calling it a missing frame rather than a misplaced one. **5.0, 5.1 and 5.2 name the property `directory` and nothing in the research puts them in this shape; the separator is written in every version regardless**, because a rule with a version branch is a rule with a version bug.

**Corrected 2026-10-04, by a controlled test at the desk, on the build runner's ruling that what lands is what the artist named** ([Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 75a). **The separator turned the artist's file name into a folder.** A 4.5 node gathering into one file, its property `//../Render/EXR/### - Hero - Box`, wrote `<job>/EXR/### - Hero - Box/0001.exr` through the redirect, where the file left alone writes `EXR/001 - Hero - Box.exr`. **So in 4.5 that node's property is split as the regular output path is (P4): the folder before the last separator, the front of the file name after it.** The redirect writes the mapped folder, a separator, and the front exactly as the artist wrote it, and I2's recipe takes the front where 5.0 takes the base file name. Through the redirect the node then wrote `<job>/EXR/001 - Hero - Box.exr` in 4.5.12, and 5.0.1, 5.1.2 and 5.2.2 wrote what they wrote before. **The harm above stays closed**: the value always opens with the mapped folder and a separator, so the file lands in the job's folder; a property the artist ended with a separator has no front and takes the separator-ended folder. **A node writing one file per output, and every node from 5.0, keeps the separator.** The sentences above calling the rule unconditional no longer hold for the gathering node, and the reason they gave goes: the reading already takes the node's mode (I7), so the redirect reads nothing new.

**D9. The material carries exactly this, and nothing that could be an argument instead.**

- the **destination map** from the recorded reading — one new folder per destination, keyed to the destination the reading found, **except [5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) D20's four added write places, which are keyed to the named setting and are in the map whether or not the walk read a value back (D21b)**; **and where a place takes more than a folder to be set, the further values that place requires go with its folder in the material and are applied with it — D21b-i's ~~four~~ values for the physics point cache included** *(corrected 2026-10-02: three, the disk-cache switch never written)*;
- the **remap material** `Dependency Upload & Remap` owns, applied by the same lever at the same moment, because both apply to the same file at the same moment and the spec says whatever route serves one serves the other;
- the **marker** of [5.4](#54-the-marker-because-a-launch-script-does-not-fail-loudly);
- the **version it was composed for**, per D7.

**D9a. *Nothing that could be an argument instead* is a rule about route, not a ceiling on the list. Added 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close, routed by the runner from [Rendering](Rendering.md#9-unresolved) §9 item 18.** A value that **has** a command-line argument in the version the job runs is delivered by that argument and never by the material; a value that has none in that version may only be delivered by the material, and D9's rule does not reach it. [Rendering](Rendering.md#14-the-launch-material-and-its-hand-over) L10 composes into the same material, for the version in hand, every settings-payload value with no argument in that version, Simplify's switch with its three settings, and the skip-existing-frames switch — **each inside D9's rule rather than against it**, and a value that acquires an argument in a later version leaves the material by the same rule. The composition is `Rendering`'s and is not re-decided or copied here; what D9 fixes is what **this** document puts in, and that the material never carries a value an argument could have carried.

**D10. The regular output path is settled by its own command-line argument rather than by the material**, because it has one in every version and *"the `.blend` on disk is left byte for byte as it was"* (*Writing 1*). **It is still one of the destinations the map covers** — the map decides the value, the argument delivers it. **And it is the one pair the material does not set, so the material reads it back**: running inside the same Blender inside the same window, the material reads the value in force for the regular output path at the moment it runs, prints it in D16's marker, and **fails the attempt where it does not match the map's pair**. That is what makes the unit of [Section 12](#12-atomic-or-partial--this-documents-declarations) atomic over a pair no material sets, and it is not the material handling a failure of its own (D18) — it raises one. **The raise ends the run only because [Rendering](Rendering.md#13-the-window-and-the-order-of-the-line) L8a places the failure-to-exit-status argument at the head of the launch line** — observed 2026-08-19 in 4.5.12 and 5.2.0, a launch script that raises otherwise printing its traceback, rendering anyway and exiting 0. *(Sentence added 2026-08-29 at the stage close: the clause declared the raise ended the run and cited nothing for it, which was the assumption `Rendering` L8a names by name and corrects; [Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 28a carries the citation and its limits.)* **The ordering the read-back rests on is established — corrected 2026-08-31; this said nobody had established it.** *Writing 13* posed it in all four versions on two machines: with the file, then the setting, then the script — which is [Rendering](Rendering.md#13-the-window-and-the-order-of-the-line) L8's own order — the script reads the setting the caller handed in, and a script placed before the setting reads the file's own value instead. **Two things the answer binds this clause to.** The value the material reads back is the **raw text handed in**, template marks and all, so the comparison here is against the map's pair as text and never against a resolved file name. And an imposed output path reaches the **active scene alone**, which is [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 19a's residue: where the named scene is not the one the path reached, this read-back is what stops the attempt. [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 19 carries the answer and its limits.

**D11. Anything delivered on the command line sits after the file, before the render instruction, and before any bare double dash.** Outside that window it is silently ignored, with no message and no failure ([What can be set from outside](../Blender%20Research/Across%20topics/What%20can%20be%20set%20from%20outside.md)). `Rendering` owns the line; this document states the window because the redirect's own argument sits in it. **The third edge was added 2026-09-01** out of the repaired Blender research: **after a bare double dash the line is Cycles' and Blender reads nothing of its own there**, so the double dash closes the window as surely as the render instruction does. **The stated window had two edges and the line has three**, and a redirect argument written past the third would be dropped in silence with the render going ahead at exit 0 — which is `Rendering` L24a's finding from the device argument's side, and the same failure with the job's whole output at stake. `Rendering` L8 carries the same three edges.

**D12. From 5.2 a switch inside the file stops the scene's own frames being written even when a path is handed in on the command line, while a node in the same tree keeps writing.** The reading records that switch ([4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) item 1) and the count and the inventory are composed from what it says, so the redirect never promises a file that switch will prevent.

### 5.3 Each destination setting is written on its own

**D13. The redirect reaches each destination's own setting individually, and no bulk route is used for any part of it.** This is Row 119's first bite and it is now observed twice over. The bulk commands that make every path relative or absolute, and the file's own list of outside files, **reach an ocean modifier's cache path and nothing else a file records for writing** — narrowed 2026-09-01 from *do not reach the places a file records for writing*, out of the repaired Blender research (*Dependencies 17*, [What has to travel with a file](../Blender%20Research/Across%20topics/What%20has%20to%20travel%20with%20a%20file.md)); what separates that one from the other five is not established, and **the clause's decision is unaffected: one place out of six is not a bulk route and no part of the redirect is given to one**; and the one-pass route that visits every recorded path **is absent in 4.5.9 altogether**, while in 5.0.1 and 5.2.0 it rewrote the four images, the text and the library — all reaching disk — and **silently left every write location alone**, with nothing raised, nothing printed and the save succeeding ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §2.2).

**D14. That is the negative the question named and it is why no route is trusted without checking what it reached.** *"A redirect built on that route alone would appear to work and leave every place the job writes pointing at the artist's own machine"* (same). The per-setting route was proved to reach every recorded location in the fixture, write locations included, in all three versions, read back off disk by a separate process (same, §2.1).

**D15. One trap the material must avoid, recorded for Build.** From 5.0, **adding an output to a file output node rebuilds the node's socket list**, so a held socket reference goes stale and links land on the trailing extend socket with no error (same, §1.1 and §2.4). Nothing in the redirect adds an output; the trap is recorded because anything that composes a node's values in the same pass could.

### 5.4 The marker, because a launch script does not fail loudly

**D16. The material's first act is to print a marker on the render's own output, naming the job identity, each destination it set, and the value it read back for the regular output path** (D10). The streams are captured and retained by `Rented-server runtime` under Row 286.

**D16a. The marker also carries the fields `Dependency Upload & Remap` writes onto it, per applied pair, and this clause records them where the marker is defined rather than deciding them. Added 2026-09-02, routed from that document's M9d.** The remap material reports every pair in this same marker (`Dependency Upload & Remap` M8), so D16's list is short by two fields without them; both are settled in that document and neither is re-decided here.

- **Per pair, the outcome** — applied, and to how many data-blocks of the pair's kind, or **not applied** with the value found (M8). *Not applied* means zero data-blocks of the kind matched, which is a countable fact rather than an unidentified miss.
- **Per pair that was applied, whether assignment alone is *established* for that pair's kind** (M9d) — **established** for the image kind, and for the library kind with its reload; **not established for every other kind by construction**, so an unposed kind reads *not established* without anyone having to remember it. The field is composed from the pair's kind alone.
- **What that second field claims, and what it does not.** It says the assignment was made and that its sufficiency for that kind was never observed. It says nothing about whether the render read the wrong file and it declares no fault: an applied pair of an unestablished kind may well have worked.

**Neither field opens a second reporting channel.** Both ride the marker this clause defines, on the render's own captured stream, retained by `Rented-server runtime` under Row 286 and readable afterwards with nobody connected — and where a kind is established nothing extra is said at all. **D17 stands over both**: the marker is a positive signal whose absence is readable, and nothing here rests on a launch failing loudly.

**D17. This is forced rather than tidy.** [Open Threads](../Groundwork/Open%20Threads.md) records, observed on 2026-08-16, that a run whose launch script sat in a folder the login could not read printed `Permission denied`, **rendered the factory scene anyway** at the wrong size on the wrong engine, wrote a file, and **exited 0** — and states outright that *"until it is answered, no architecture document may rest on a launch script failing loudly."* **So nothing here rests on it.** The marker is a positive signal whose absence is readable; it does not make the failure loud and is not offered as doing so.

**D18. The material contains no failure handling of its own.** *"A failure raised inside a script that catches it leaves a successful exit status behind"* (Row 135, on the spec's own observation), so anything the material catches becomes a job that reports success over a render that did not do what was asked.

**D19. The placement of the material is confirmed by reading it back under the login the render will run as** (L12), which closes the one shape of D17's hazard that is Smoozy's own doing. **Under L13's one login the read-back is a check against a placement fault** — material that did not land, or landed somewhere else — **and never against a permission difference between two logins**, of which there are none. It does not close the hazard; [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 8.

### 5.5 What the redirect reaches, and the one residue it does not

**D20. The redirect covers the regular output path, each file output node's folder, and every further write place the walk over Blender's own marks on its settings reaches** — the bake output path, the point cache, the geometry-nodes bake folder and the sequencer proxy folder. **Label: Creator-stated (2026-08-18)**, ruled on the finding this document's own scoping raised; [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md) names the covered places and [Dependency Upload & Remap](../Specs/Dependency%20Upload%20&%20Remap.md)'s `[PENDING: whether the places a file writes to fall under the job's-folder redirect]` is answered by that naming. **Each added place is an ordinary destination from here on**: it is in the destination set of [4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) item 2, ~~it takes part in working out the shared run of [6.3](#63-the-rule)~~, it is in the map, it lands under the job's folder, ~~it is in the folders the user is shown~~, and it comes home. What it is not in is the count ([7.3](#73-the-count) I14). **Corrected 2026-10-04, out of the Creator's notes 14 and 18 on the test copy: an added place takes no part in working out the shared run, and always takes its own named folder under the job's folder, value or none ([6.3](#63-the-rule) P6, P7a, P9); and it is not among the folders the user is shown (P11).** **The reason the first is struck:** a bake output path left at `//`, the `.blend`'s own folder — which a freshly read file records — made the shared run one folder shorter on every job, so every folder the render wrote gained a level, and the `.blend`'s own folder was listed as a place a stills job writes into. **The reason the second is struck:** the list is where the user checks where their work goes, and four or five cache folders on every job, written to or not, said it went somewhere it did not.

**A fifth added write place: a fluid domain's cache folder, where the domain's cache type is Replay. Added 2026-10-02, on the build runner's ruling under the Creator's standing delegation; [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md)'s own rule — every place the job would write lands under the job's folder — already covers it.** Its setting is `domain_settings.cache_directory`, where `domain_settings.cache_type` is `REPLAY`. **Replay is the only fluid cache type seen writing during a render**: one set of files per frame, beside the `.blend`, outside the job's folder, never brought home, and read by the next job of the same file. A baked fluid of another cache type is not this place and is left as the file has it. Label: **observed at the desk**, 2026-10-02 ([Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 38c). **It is set as the file loads and not by the material** ([5.1](#51-the-route-is-launch-material-and-not-a-copy) D1a). **Added 2026-10-02 by simulation fix piece 4 — what a render writes there through Smoozy's own line, which names its frames as a list** ([Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 38e): with the folder set as the file loads to an empty folder, a Replay smoke and a Replay liquid rendered 1 to 24 **wrote frame 1's files alone, and every frame after the first was the same picture** — the simulation did not move — in 4.5.12 and 5.2.2; a plain Blender given the same list does the same, while the same files rendered as one animation (start, end and the animation switch) wrote every frame's files. **Until that piece, Smoozy's frames of a Replay file moved, and not because the render wrote them**: the reading at the render's start (R4 item 5) had stepped every frame of its span and written a whole cache beside the `.blend`, and the render read it. **Which picture a user is owed for a Replay file rendered from a list of frames is not this clause's**; it is the shape of the Creator's open question about an unbaked geometry-nodes simulation, which renders still for the same reason. **Everywhere this document says *D20's four*, it was written before the fifth**: D21b, D21b-ii, D21f, D21g, P7a, P10 and P14 read the fifth as one more added write place; D21b-i is the point cache's own, D21d's three are the three it names, and L7a places no copy at it.

**D21. One residue is not reached and it is not disclosed either, because it is never found.** A path held in a **custom property** is reached by no route this stage has — the flat list and the relative sweep miss it, and the walk over Blender's own path marks misses it too ([4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) item 6). **So it is not *found and not mapped*; it is not found at all.** It cannot be recorded, cannot be shown, and cannot hold a job, and this document says so rather than claiming a disclosure it cannot deliver ([Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 12). **What the reading does find is disclosed**: a destination the map records as **not mappable** is carried in the recorded reading with its reason and reaches `Queue & History` as a field of it ([Section 13](#13-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document)), so nothing the reading found is hidden.

**D21b. The four added places are named settings, not found values, and that is what Row 313 turns on. Added 2026-08-21.** D20's four — the bake output path, the point cache, the geometry-nodes bake folder and the sequencer proxy folder — are a **fixed, known set**, unlike the arbitrary destinations the flat list and the relative sweep turn up. **So each of them enters the destination set of [4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) item 2 whether or not the walk read a value back from it, and the redirect sets each one under the job's folder unconditionally** — for the point cache, on every cache whose disk cache the file already has on, and never on a rigid-body world's (D21b-i; *added 2026-10-02*); **for the geometry-nodes bake folder, only where a sent bake was copied** (D1a; *the Creator's ruling of 2026-10-02*). Smoozy does not need to have found a value to know the setting is there; it names the setting. **This is the opposite of D21's custom-property case**, where there is no setting to name and nothing to assign to.

**D21b-i. Setting a place is not the same as assigning its path, and for the physics point cache it is four values together. Corrected 2026-08-22, when it was run.** [Fact 116](Facts.md), observed on a rented machine in Blender 4.5.12 and 5.2.0 ([The multi-provider batch](The%20multi-provider%20batch%20—%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md)). The redirect writes, for the point cache:

```
point_cache.use_external   = True
point_cache.filepath       = <this cache's folder under the job's folder>
point_cache.name           = <this cache's own file stem>
```

**and the folder is created before the render is started**, not left to Blender.

**Corrected 2026-10-02, out of step 10c's desk tests: the values are set only on a point cache whose disk cache the file already has on, and the disk-cache switch itself is never written.** The block above began with `point_cache.use_disk_cache = True` until that date, and named one stem for every cache. Every Blender fact in this paragraph is **observed at the desk, 2026-10-02**, in 4.5.12 and 5.2.2 unless it says otherwise ([Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entries 38a and 38b).

- **Writing the switch on a cache where it is already on deletes every file that cache holds.** 48 baked files beside the `.blend` became none at that step, and 3 hair files became 1.
- **A cache the file keeps in memory writes nothing to disk, and is left as the file has it.** Turning its disk cache on dropped a cloth's in-file bake — frame 8 alone came out wrong — and wrote 60 files beside the `.blend`.
- **A rigid-body world's cache is never set.** Switching its disk cache on ended Blender with exit 139, on a demonstration file's rigid-body chain and on a fresh rigid-body scene never baked.
- **The values are written in the order above**: *external* on, the folder, the name. No file beside the `.blend` moved at any step in that order.
- **The name is the cache's own file stem** — its name where it has one, else the stem Blender gives it from its object — and never one stem for every cache. Blender's own stem for one cloth, `ClothSheet`, was `436C6F74685368656574`, the object's name in hexadecimal; that the rule holds beyond that one object is **not established**. **Narrowed 2026-10-02, out of step 10c's simulation fix piece 3:** Blender's stem is the object's name written as two capital hexadecimal digits for each byte of its UTF-8, seen for a cloth on each of two objects — one named `Ткань`, whose stem was `D0A2D0BAD0B0D0BDD18C` — and for a particle system, in 4.5.12 and 5.2.2 ([Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 38d). **A cache's name holds at most 63 bytes**, so where the object's name is longer than 31 bytes its stem cannot be written to the name and a baked cache copied into the job folder is not read — the render finishes with the object still. ~~That is unresolved and waits on a ruling; it is not papered over here.~~ **Resolved 2026-10-02, on the build runner's ruling under the Creator's standing delegation (simulation fix piece 3c):** as a baked cache is copied into the job folder — found on the machine or sent by the check — every file named `<stem>_<frame>_<index>.bphys` whose stem is longer than 63 bytes is copied under a stem the name can hold: the stem's first 46 bytes, an underscore, and the 64-bit FNV-1a of the whole stem in 16 capital hexadecimal digits. The material writes that same stem to the cache's name. Every other file keeps its name. At the desk, a cloth on an object named with 40 Latin characters and one named with 20 Cyrillic letters then rendered all 24 frames like the plain render, uploaded beside and sent, in 4.5.12 and 5.2.2 (entry 38d). This keeps the ruling of 2026-08-22, and costs the user nothing. **A baked cache copied into the job folder is read under its own stem and not under another**: frame 24 came out identical to the plain render, and under the stem `smoozy` it was wrong on 35% of pixels, with Blender's cache line reading *"No valid data to read!"*.
- **An unbaked disk cache still comes under the job's folder with these three values**: 24 files in the job folder, nothing beside the `.blend`, frame 24 identical to the plain render (4.5.12 only).

**A baked physics cache whose files are on the machine where the file records them — sent by the check, or uploaded beside the `.blend` — is copied to its job-folder value before the render, as a sent bake is ([Dependency Upload & Remap](Dependency%20Upload%20&%20Remap.md#67-the-uploaded-cache-and-the-redirect--a-seam-between-two-closed-rulings-ruled-row-312) M22c), and recorded the same way (M22f).** Where its files sit is its recorded folder where *external* is on, else the default folder beside the `.blend` (D21c). A baked cache whose files are not on the machine is not copied. *(Added 2026-10-02, on the build runner's ruling under the Creator's standing delegation, which keeps M22a's ruling of 2026-08-22. A cache marked baked whose files are not where its setting looks gives the object as it starts, unmoving, on every frame, rendered alone or in order; so a baked cache left beside the `.blend` while its setting names the job folder is never read.)*

**`use_external` is the value that decides it, and without it the assignment is a silent no-op.** Assigning `filepath` alone with `use_disk_cache = True` — the obvious form, and the form this clause used to specify — was *"read back exactly as assigned"*, the bake ran, **the render finished and reported success**, and the cache was written to `blendcache_<stem>/` beside the `.blend`: 250 files, outside the job's folder, uncounted, destroyed with the machine. **That is D22's failure produced by the redirect itself.** With the four values set, both versions wrote `pointcache/<name>_000001_00.bphys` onward under the job's folder and nothing beside the `.blend`. *(The four were set on a fresh cache in that run. Since 2026-10-02 the switch is the file's own and is never written — the correction above.)*

**D21b-ii. A read-back is never evidence that a redirect took, and this is a standing rule over all four added places. Added 2026-08-31, promoted out of D21b-i, whose observation is its whole ground.** **A location assigned from outside can be stored, returned on read, and ignored at write time** — observed on the physics point cache in 4.5.12 and 5.2.0, where `filepath` assigned with `use_disk_cache = True` alone read back exactly as assigned, the render finished and reported success, and the cache was written beside the `.blend` regardless (D21b-i). **So no clause in this group may treat a read-back that matches what was assigned as evidence that the assignment took**, for the point cache or for any of the other three, and none does: the three assignments recorded at D21d all read back and none of them is counted as an answer. **What stands in place of a read-back is D21f**, which reads the folder after the render rather than the setting before it.

**D21c. That is what closes the door Row 313 found, and it is stated as a construction rather than as care.** *Dependencies 10* documents, identically in all four versions, that a physics cache with no location given lands in a folder named for the file **beside the `.blend`** — which on a rented machine is inside Smoozy's asset area, outside the job's folder, uncounted, and destroyed with the machine. **An implementation that only redirected destinations the walk returned a value for would leave the ordinary artist's file — the common case, not a corner — on exactly that path, silently.** The fixture behind *Reading a file 9*, which is where the four were established as findable at all, was built *"with every one written out in full from the drive root"*, so it established them for a file where each had been given an explicit path by hand.

**D21d. What this rests on is [Fact 116](Facts.md), and it is now observed for one of the four and unobserved for three. Corrected 2026-08-22.** The question is whether each of the four accepts an assignment from outside the file where nothing was recorded, and whether the render then honours it. **For the physics point cache it is answered, and the first answer was the negative** — D21b-i. **For the other three it is still posed by nothing this project has run**, and the attempts are recorded rather than the conclusions: the **geometry-nodes bake folder** took `bake_target='DISK'` and `bake_directory` and read both back, `geometry_node_bake_single` returned without raising, and **no bake folder appeared anywhere** — the fixture's simulation zone passed geometry through unchanged and produced nothing to bake, so the assignment was never tested against a real bake; the **sequencer proxy folder** took `proxy_storage='PROJECT'` and `proxy_dir` and read both back, and **no proxy was ever built** because the fixture has no movie strip; the **bake output path** was assigned to `render.bake.filepath` and read back, and **no image bake was run**. **A read-back is not evidence, on the point cache's own showing.** **What holds without it:** the promise `What a job writes` makes is *not left on a machine that is about to be destroyed*, and that is kept exactly when the redirect reaches — a render that wrote no cache at all leaves nothing behind, so the promise is not kept vacuously in that case but kept truly. **What breaks with it:** where an assignment does not take, the cache lands beside the `.blend` and D22 applies — silently, with the render reporting success, which is now an observed mode rather than a feared one. [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 12's neighbour. **And it is not going to be established, so the silence is removed instead of waited out**: [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 22 records why the three are unresolved permanently, and D21f is what stands in place of the missing answer.

**The geometry-nodes bake folder was run at the desk on 2026-10-02, against real bakes** (D1a): set after the file has loaded, it is ignored; set as the file loads, it is read. That settles it for a folder that held a bake; ~~a geometry-nodes simulation set to bake to disk and never baked was not tried~~ **and a geometry-nodes simulation set to bake to disk and never baked writes nothing anywhere** — rendered plainly from a list of frames and as one animation, and stepped frame by frame from a script, with the bake folder left empty and with one named, in 4.5.12 and 5.2.2: nothing new beside the `.blend`, in the run's folder, in the temporary folders or in the home folder, and every frame the first (simulation fix piece 4, [Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 38e). The sequencer proxy folder and the bake output path stay as above.

**D21e. Nothing here claims a cache was written.** The completeness statement counts inventory entries, and [7.3](#73-the-count) I14 gives an added write place a folder with no per-file entries, so no count ever vouches for a bake or a cache. **A redirect that took and a render that wrote nothing to it are not distinguishable by anything this stage has**, and nothing above pretends otherwise: what comes home is whatever is under the folder, and an empty one is reported as empty rather than as a loss.

**D21f. An added write place that produced nothing under the job's folder is disclosed rather than passed over. Added 2026-08-31, with Row 313 closed unresolved and Fact 116 established for one of the four.** **What it covers:** D20's four added write places, each of which D21b assigns under the job's folder whether or not the walk read a value back — the bake output path, the point cache, the geometry-nodes bake folder and the sequencer proxy folder, and nothing else; the destinations the reading found are already covered by D21 and by [6.4](#64-when-a-destination-is-not-mappable-and-the-case-that-is-not-that) P12. **When it runs:** at the ending walk — the one complete walk of the job output folder taken once the attempt has ended ([Download](Download.md#7-what-ends-an-arrangement-row-220) §7 step 2), which is the same folder the completeness statement of [10.6](#106-the-completeness-reading-and-the-one-signal-a-lost-write-leaves) is made against. **It costs no reading of its own**: that walk already returns every entry under the job output folder and already reaches an added write place's mapped folder ([Download](Download.md#44-the-files-with-no-frame) J8), and [10.2](#102-the-reading-the-reckoning-is-made-against) C8 is why a walk is what this document reads for anything of the kind. **What it looks for:** per place, whether that walk returned any entry at all under that place's mapped folder ([6.3](#63-the-rule)) — presence under the folder, never a read-back of the setting (D21b-ii) and never a size or a name. **What it does where the folder is not the place's own: nothing, and it says so. Added 2026-09-05, out of a code round.** A place the walk read a value back from goes through the ordinary shared-run rule of [6.3](#63-the-rule), which can land it at the job output folder itself (P7's *nothing left*) or in a folder it shares with a render destination (P17). In the first shape the reading is a fixed **no** whatever the render wrote; in the second a fixed **yes** where the cache wrote nothing. **So the reading is withheld, and stated as withheld, wherever the place's mapped folder is the job output folder itself or is shared with any other destination in the map** — the completeness statement then carries *not readable for this place* rather than an answer nobody read. **Nothing downstream branches on it**, by D21g, so this moves no file and changes no path; what it stops is a field stating an answer it did not take. **What it does where nothing was returned:** the place is named on the completeness statement as having produced nothing under the job's folder ([10.6](#106-the-completeness-reading-and-the-one-signal-a-lost-write-leaves) C27a), which reaches `Queue & History` as a field of that statement ([Section 13](#13-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document)) and is readable afterwards with nobody connected, exactly as D21's *not mappable* disclosure reaches that document as a field of the recorded reading. **No second reporting channel is opened**: this is that statement and D16's marker and nothing else, and where the walk returned something under the place's folder nothing is said at all.

**D21g. What the disclosure does not claim, and the boundary is the point of it.** **Nothing anywhere asserts that a bake or a cache was written.** D21e already states that a redirect that took and a render that wrote nothing to the place are not distinguishable by anything this stage has, and [7.3](#73-the-count) I14 gives an added write place a folder with no per-file entries, so no count ever vouched for one and none is short here. **A place that produced nothing is therefore not by itself a fault.** A file with no simulation to cache, no bake to write, no proxy to build and no image bake asked for produces nothing at all four, and that is the ordinary file rather than a failure. **So the statement says what was observed and no more** — nothing was produced under the job's folder for that place — and it says nothing about why, holds no job ([9.1](#91-the-three-conditions-and-where-each-is-established) H1's list is closed and is not widened by this), moves no count (I14), and is not an error anywhere. **What it removes is the silence.** Where an assignment does not take, D21b-i's observed mode leaves the cache beside the `.blend` with the render reporting success, and without this the two cases a reader could otherwise never separate — the place that had nothing to write, and the place whose redirect was ignored at write time — look identical and both look like an ordinary success. This makes the second one readable; it does not decide which of the two it was, and nothing downstream may present it as deciding. **The wording is `Queue & History`'s and Design's**, as every other field of a reading this document supplies is.

**D22. A location the redirect did not reach does not fail loudly either.** *"A whole foreign folder tree shaped for the artist's own computer is built at the top of the rented machine's disk and written into, silently, and the render reports success"* ([What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md); *Dependencies 10*). Observed again on 2026-08-17: the rewritten copy wrote ten files all under the new folder with `ls: cannot access '/home/artist': No such file or directory`, **while the unrewritten fixture built that foreign tree silently** ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §2.3).

---

## 6. Bringing a destination under the job's folder

### 6.1 The suggested mechanism is kept, and this is why

**P1. The mechanism the spec offered is taken as it stands: take the folder every destination writes into, throw away the run of folders they all share, and keep what is left under the job's folder.** Row 121 permits replacing it outright provided both promises survive; it is kept, and the reason is that no replacement considered keeps the second promise as well.

**P2. What was weighed and declined.** Giving every destination a folder named for its position in the set keeps everything under the job's folder and **loses the artist's arrangement entirely**, which is the second promise. Keeping the whole of every path under the job's folder keeps both promises but buries the ordinary case: a file with one output path and no compositor would get a chain of the artist's own folders to walk down before reaching its frames, where the shared-run rule gives *"a folder of frames, in a folder named for the job that made them."* **The shared-run rule is the only one weighed that gives the ordinary file a flat folder and still keeps two different places different.**

**P3. What the mechanism gains that is new here is the failure it did not have.** [2.3](#23-the-length-budget-in-bytes-checked-before-a-path-is-used) is that failure, and [6.4](#64-when-a-destination-is-not-mappable-and-the-case-that-is-not-that) is what is done about it. The rule *"deepens paths by construction"* and the machine refuses a name it will not take for its length **at open, before any byte is sent**, so the check has to be Smoozy's own and has to happen before the render.

**And the added write places of [5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) are part of what deepens them.** The shared run is worked out over a larger set, so it can be shorter, so what is left under the job's folder is deeper. **So [2.3](#23-the-length-budget-in-bytes-checked-before-a-path-is-used)'s budget bites more often and [9.1](#91-the-three-conditions-and-where-each-is-established)'s first condition fires more often** than either would over the render's own destinations alone. [6.5](#65-the-invariant-the-rule-never-merges-two-destinations-that-were-distinct) P16 is unaffected by the larger set: only a prefix identical to every destination is ever removed. **No longer so from 2026-10-04**: the added write places take no part in the run (P9 as corrected), so they no longer shorten it; each takes a named folder of one segment (P7a).

### 6.2 Resolution comes before the rule

**P4. Every destination's folder is resolved to a single absolute form before the rule sees it, and the rule works on nothing else.**

- **A path written relative to the `.blend`** is resolved against the folder the `.blend` sits in **on the rented machine**, and is an ordinary path from then on. It may climb above that folder and the resolution settles that too.
- **A path written on another kind of computer** is kept exactly as written and resolves to nothing on Linux — Blender *"does not translate it, does not rewrite it and does not refuse it"*, and a drive letter is an ordinary place name that happens to hold a colon. Observed on 2026-08-17: a Windows path went in with backslashes and read back with forward slashes ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §1.2). Such a path is taken as its own tree, sharing at most the topmost folder and often nothing at all, and it keeps the whole of itself beneath.
- **The regular output path is one piece of text holding the folder and the front of the name together**, and Blender itself splits it at the last separator. **The rule works on the part before that separator.** A path ending in a separator has no name front at all. **In 4.5 a file output node gathering into one file holds its folder and the front of its file name the same way, and is split the same way** (D8 as corrected 2026-10-04). **A separator inside the blend-file shorthand is not the last separator**: `//f_` is `f_` in the `.blend`'s own folder, never at the machine's root *(added 2026-10-04; it had split as `/` and `f_`)*.

**P5. After resolution no destination folder climbs, and the rule never has to reason about one.** That is why P4 comes first.

### 6.3 The rule

**P6. The shared run is the longest run of **whole folder names** common to every resolved destination folder in the set, measured in whole names and never in letters.** `/home/a/out` and `/home/a/output2` share `/home/a`. **The set is the render's own output destinations alone, and D20's added write places take no part in it, with or without a value. Changed 2026-10-04 with D20 and P9.**

**P7. Each destination's new folder is the job output folder joined to what is left of that destination's folder after the shared run is removed.** A destination with nothing left lands at the job output folder itself.

**P7a. A destination with no resolved folder takes no part in working out the shared run, and its new folder is the job output folder joined to a name for the setting itself. Added 2026-09-04, out of Build, where two builders read this section two different ways.** **Only one kind of destination can reach this**: one of D20's four added write places that the walk read no value back from, which D21b puts in the destination set and under the job's folder whether or not a value was found, and which therefore offers P6 nothing to measure and P7 nothing to take a run from. **P6 already reads only the *resolved* destination folders, so such a place shortens no run.** Giving it the machine's root as its folder instead would leave no run common to it and the render's own destinations, and **P2's declined outcome — the whole of the artist's own path under the job's folder — would then arrive on the ordinary file rather than on a corner**, D21c's own case being the artist who recorded no path for any of the four. **The four names differ from each other**, because D21f reads whether the ending walk returned anything under *that place's* mapped folder and four places sharing one folder would make that reading say the same thing about all four; so P7's *nothing left* case is not what such a place takes. **The names themselves are Build's**, like every other name this document does not fix. **Nothing else here moves**: the place is an ordinary destination at P12 and P14, its pair is keyed to its named setting at P10, and its folder is among the folders the user is shown at P11.

**Narrowed 2026-10-02 with [4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) R4 item 2.** A place now has one destination for each distinct location the file records for it, and those resolve and take P6 and P7 like any other destination, distinct by P16. **What this clause names is the one destination for the place's carriers that record no location.** Its name stays distinct from every other folder in the map, per place and per recorded location.

**Widened 2026-10-04 with D20: every destination of an added write place now takes this clause's named folder under the job output folder, whether or not a location was recorded for it**, distinct per place and per recorded location as above, and none takes P6 and P7 any longer — the narrowing's *"those resolve and take P6 and P7 like any other destination"* no longer holds for the added places. P10's further values, D21b-i's stems and L7a's placed copies are unchanged; what moved is only the folder each one is set to.

**P8. The regular output path takes part in working out the shared run alongside the nodes, and is not treated as the root of anything.**

**P9. The set the rule runs over is the destination set of [4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) item 2 entire** — including every scene a sequencer strip reaches, and including the added write places of [5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not). A shared run computed over the job's own scene alone, or over the render's own output destinations alone, is a different answer.

**Corrected 2026-10-04, out of the Creator's notes 14 and 18 on the test copy: the shared run is computed over the render's own output destinations alone** — every scene a sequencer strip reaches still included — **and the added write places are mapped by P7a, each to its own named folder, and take no part in it.** Everything in the set is still in the map and still lands under the job's folder; what changed is which destinations decide the run. **Why:** a bake output path left at `//`, the `.blend`'s own folder — which a freshly read file records — sat in the set on every job, so the run came out one folder shorter, every folder the render wrote gained a level, and the `.blend`'s own folder was shown as a place a stills job writes into. The sentence above calling a run over the render's own destinations *a different answer* is the answer now.

**P10. The map is a record of pairs — the destination the reading found, and the new folder — and the render applies pairs.** It is not an instruction to recompute. [4.3](#43-the-rule-runs-once-per-job-and-the-side-that-ran-it-writes-the-map) is why. **Two things about the added write places of [5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not), so a pair is not read as the whole of what they take.** A pair for one of D20's four is keyed to the **named setting** rather than to a value the reading found, and it is in the map whether or not the walk read a value back (D21b). **And a pair is not always a folder alone**: where the place takes more than a folder to be set, the further values it requires are recorded with the pair and applied with it — for the physics point cache that is D21b-i's ~~four values together~~ values together *(corrected 2026-10-02: three, the disk-cache switch never written)*, and setting the folder alone is the observed silent no-op.

**P11. What the user is shown as *the folders the job will write into* is the set of new folders in that map, relative to the job output folder** — the added write places of [5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) among them, since they are in the map. That is Row 122's second clause and it is the same object the render applies.

**Corrected 2026-10-04, out of the Creator's note 14 on the test copy: the folders shown are the new folders of the render's own output destinations — the destinations the render asked for, which are the inventory's own folders — and the added write places are not among them.** They are still in the map, still redirected and still come home; listing them put four or five cache folders on every job whether or not anything wrote there ([What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md), *What the user is told before submitting*). **Beside the folders, the user is shown where the job output folder sits** (6.6) — its run, and P19's case where the fallback is taken. It is still the same object the render applies.

### 6.4 When a destination is not mappable, and the case that is not that

**P12. A destination the rule covers is recorded in the map as *not mappable*, with the reason, in exactly two cases. There is no third, and the reading names which it is.**

1. **Its predicted deepest file path exceeds the whole-path budget, or one of its segments exceeds the name budget** ([2.3](#23-the-length-budget-in-bytes-checked-before-a-path-is-used)).
2. **In Blender 4.5, an output's name climbs above the node's folder.** Moving the folder does not move that output with it, and **Smoozy does not touch output names**, so the redirect cannot carry it. In 5.0 every separator in a name becomes an underscore and every file lands flat in the node's folder, and in 5.1 and 5.2 a name cannot climb out — so this case is 4.5's alone.

**P13. A job with any destination recorded as not mappable is a job whose paths Smoozy cannot bring under its folder**, and it is held under [9.1](#91-the-three-conditions-and-where-each-is-established). **There is no partial redirect that proceeds quietly** over a destination the rule covers: the whole point of the promise is that `Download` is told it has one folder to watch.

**P14. The added write places of [5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) are ordinary destinations here, and there is no class of write place the rule covers but does not map.** A bake output path, a point cache, a geometry-nodes bake folder and a sequencer proxy folder are mapped like any other destination, and **where one of them is not mappable it is P12's case 1** — a length that will not fit — **and it holds the job under [9.1](#91-the-three-conditions-and-where-each-is-established), exactly as any other destination does**. The one write place that is not here at all is D21's custom-property residue, which the reading never finds, so there is nothing to record and nothing to hold on.

**P15. Every other form of path is mappable, which is what lets the folders be stated firmly.** [6.2](#62-resolution-comes-before-the-rule) covers the relative and the foreign forms, and P7 covers the case where nothing is left.

### 6.5 The invariant: the rule never merges two destinations that were distinct

**P16. Two destinations that resolved to different folders always land in different folders under the job's folder**, because only a prefix identical to every destination is removed and the remainders therefore still differ. **So the collision set after redirecting is exactly the collision set before it**, which is what the spec means by *"After redirecting they still collide"* and what makes the collision record of [7.4](#74-the-collision-record) computable before the render.

**P17. And two destinations that were the same place stay the same place**, which P7's *nothing left* case covers without inventing anything to tell them apart with.

### 6.6 Where the job output folder sits

*Added 2026-10-04, out of the Creator's note 18 on the test copy. The Creator chose that the job's outputs go where the file points, in one folder per job, with a path from another computer or a folder Smoozy cannot write falling back to Smoozy's own job folder and the job saying so — Creator-stated, recorded word for word at the end of `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The Creator's tests of the test copy/Second notes, 2026-10-04.md`, and written at [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md) and [Queue & History](../Specs/Queue%20&%20History.md). Until this date the job output folder sat under the job area of [1.2](#12-the-areas-and-what-may-never-sit-under-a-job-folder) L6 for every job. No auditor has seen this section.*

**P18. The job output folder sits inside the shared run of the render's own output destinations, and is named by N3.** The run is P6's, over the render's own output destinations of [4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) item 2 — every scene a sequencer strip reaches included, D20's added write places excluded (P9) — after P4's resolution on the server. The job output folder is that run joined to N3's name, unchanged: the identity keeps two jobs of one file apart in one run, and nothing parses a name. P7 then puts each destination's remainder under it. **The Creator's file** — regular output `//../Render/Animation`, nodes at `//../Render/PNG` and `//../Render/EXR`, the `.blend` in `/home/ubuntu/rendering` — gets `/home/ubuntu/Render/<N3 name>/`, with `PNG/` and `EXR/` beneath and the video at its top. **A job with no render destination at all** has no run and takes P19's place without a reason recorded: nothing will land in it, and [9.1](#91-the-three-conditions-and-where-each-is-established) H1's writes-nothing statement already speaks for that job.

**P18a. A first-frame check writes into its range job's output folder, and every file it writes is marked. Added 2026-10-29, Creator-stated, the twenty-first notes, note 6 (`Specs/Rendering.md`, *The check's files are marked*); the mark `check_` is a recommendation awaiting the Creator's choice. No auditor has seen it.**

- **The folder.** A check job's payload names its range job (`Rendering` S13). Its job output folder is the one P18 names for the range job — the same run, joined to the range job's N3 name — and P7 puts each destination's remainder under it as for any job. P19 to P23's fallback is decided once, for the range job, and the check takes the same answer. The folder is made by whichever of the two renders first, which is the check.
- **The mark.** The step-4 reading that composes the map (R4c) puts `check_` at the start of the **last name component** of every render destination's file name, after P7 and before anything reads a name — the regular output, each File Output node's slot and L10c's still picture alike. Folders in a remainder are not marked. So the map, the inventory, the count, the completeness statement, the preview's candidates, Telegram's pictures and the download's matching all read marked names by the ordinary rules, and none carries a case of its own. The range job carries no mark and composes exactly as it would without a check.
- **What it may not touch.** Removing the check — X1a's deletion of the pair, History's clearing, a cancelled range, the check's own failure — removes only files the check's own inventory names, never the folder and never a file of the range job's. Where removing a job would remove its output folder, a check never does.
- ~~**What is not yet established.** Where the last name component sits for a File Output node's slot in each supported Blender, and what Blender writes for a name that ends before its frame digits, are Blender facts. **Nothing in the Blender research has been read for them in this pass**; they go to research before this clause is built, and the mark at the start was chosen because it does not need the second.~~
- **What is established, 2026-10-31 — observed in a test** (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/A desk test — where a name's start lands.md`, a controlled test in 4.5, 5.0, 5.1, 5.2, 5.3 beta and 5.4 alpha). The mark goes at the start of the last piece of the setting that starts each written file's name: the scene output's path in every version; in 4.5 each File Output input's own path, or the node's folder path for a multilayer node; from 5.0 the node's file name — except, from 5.1, for an input whose name holds a subfolder, whose name's last piece starts the file. A path ending in a slash takes the mark as a new last piece, so the frame-only name `0001.png` becomes `check_0001.png`. The second fact is no longer needed: the mark sits before the name, never after the digits. The Creator approved the `check_` pictures. **The range job finds the folder already standing when it renders and takes it**: its name holds the range job's identity, which is never reused, so only its check can have made it (N10 is unchanged for every other folder). Recorded 2026-10-31 by the note 6 builder. No auditor has seen it.

**P19. The fallback is the job area of L6, exactly as every job used before this section, in two cases, and the case is recorded with the path.** *Corrected later on 2026-10-04: the fallback's place is P23's — the server's chosen fallback folder where one is set and passes these same conditions, and the job area otherwise. The two cases and their recording are unchanged.*

1. **Another computer's.** A render destination is in P4's foreign form, or the run would need a folder made directly under the machine's own root — which is what a path from the artist's own Mac (`/Users/…`, `/Volumes/…`) or a run of `/` alone comes to on a rented Linux machine. **What this does not tell apart, stated:** a path from another Linux computer that the server can make without a folder at the top of its disk — `/home/alex/…`, on a login allowed to make `/home/alex` — is taken as this server's and made as the file says. Nothing in a path says which computer wrote it, and that is where the file points.
2. **A folder Smoozy cannot use.** The run lies under the root of L1 or the package area's location of L4a, where nothing of a job may sit (L7, and [Security and licence](Security%20and%20licence.md#10-what-smoozy-leaves-on-a-rented-machine) §10, which rests on those areas being outside every path a render writes under); or under a shared temporary area, which L3 already keeps everything of Smoozy's out of because it is wiped at every boot, so a re-run's reckoning and a download would find nothing; or making a missing folder of the run, or the job output folder itself, is refused (P20); or a destination's deepest predicted file path at that place exceeds 2.3's budget (N8) — where it exceeds it at the fallback as well, P12 case 1 holds the job as it always did.

**The job is not held for either case.** Everything still lands in one folder, still comes home and is still counted; the spec grants the fallback as an answer, not as a question.

**P20. Making it: each missing folder of the run, found missing by a stat, is made with N9's plain form one segment at a time, as N11 makes an added write place's parents, and then the job output folder with the plain form.** A refusal anywhere on the way is P19 case 2, and the folders already made on the way are left: they are where the file points, and Blender would have made them itself.
