At the fallback a refusal is N10's fault, as it always was. *Corrected later on 2026-10-04: at a chosen fallback folder a refusal fails that folder and the job area is used (P23); only a refusal in the job area is N10's fault.*

**P21. The choice is made once per job, on the side that composes the job's first map (R7), and never again.**

- **Where the file was read before submitting**, the desktop chooses at submission from the recorded reading and the server path of the `.blend`, makes the folder before the record's publication (M7), and the manifest carries the path and its case (M1).
- **Where it was not**, the manifest carries the statement that the folder is chosen at render start — *and, from later on 2026-10-04, beside it the server's chosen fallback folder as the row held it at submission, or that none was set (P23)*. The render worker chooses at [Rendering](Rendering.md#2-the-attempt-pipeline) P1 step 4, with the map, makes the folder before step 5, and writes the path and its case into the job record directory beside the recorded reading and the map *(and, from later on 2026-10-04, whether the chosen fallback folder was passed over — P23)*. They reach the entry by `Queue & History` N9's route with the reading, and the desktop holds the path on the job's local record as it holds one composed at submission, so `Download` walks it like any other. **Until then the job has no output folder**, and *show outputs* on it is withheld (`Queue & History` Y8).
- **A later attempt never chooses again.** A re-run whose file changed composes a fresh map (R13) under the same job output folder, which is what keeps `Queue & History` Y5's *a re-run writes where its first attempt did*.

*Added later on 2026-10-04 at Build (the Creator's second notes, piece 4): **while the desktop writes no recorded reading into any manifest** (R7's second bullet; the build runner's ruling of 2026-09-29), **every job is this clause's second case** — the desktop never chooses at submission, and the one choice is the render worker's, recorded in the job record directory once and read by every later attempt. Render Settings' line asks that same choice with nothing made, so it says only what is known before submitting (a folder the machine lacks at its top, or a refused make, is found at render start and said on the entry).*

**P22. What this costs, stated rather than left to be found.** **The outputs land on whatever mount the file's folder is on**, so L2's largest-available choice and L4a's separation of the package area from the render's outputs govern only P19's fallback; a file placed on a small mount fills that mount, and the full disk is met as it is met anywhere ([10.5](#105-nothing-here-reads-an-exit-status-as-everything-was-written)). **Nothing else of Section 1 moves**: the job output folder is still its own folder, holding nothing but what the render writes and L7a's placed copies (L7); nothing outside it is counted, listed, watched or brought home (L8) — the artist's own files beside it in the run included; and the job record directory, the manifest and every other area stay where L6 puts them. **Corrected later on 2026-10-04 with P23: L2 and L4a govern only a job output folder in the job area**; one in the chosen fallback folder lands on that folder's mount, exactly as one where the file points does.

**P23. The fallback's place is the server's chosen fallback folder where the user set one and it passes P19's conditions, and the job area of L6 otherwise. Added later on 2026-10-04, on the Creator's want of that date** — *"I want to choose that fallback folder myself. Smoozy's own folder stays the default, and each fallback job still gets its own job folder inside whatever I choose"* — written at [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md) and [Servers](../Specs/Servers.md), with the build runner's rulings in `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the plan pass for the chosen fallback folder.md`. No auditor has seen this clause.

- **Where it is held: one field of the server row** ([Servers](Servers.md#61-one-durable-store-and-one-row-per-server) D3), the path the user typed with surrounding white space removed, or empty. **Why there**: a folder is a place on one machine, the row is the one durable thing per machine, and it already holds that machine's root (L1) — so no new store, and the removal and its enumeration reach it with nothing added (`Servers` D6, D17). **It is the user's own instruction and not a machine-derived value**, like the remembered setup bit (`Servers` B8): nothing is read from a machine to produce it, it carries no installation identity, and no branch of [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#23-the-machines-installation-identity) §2.3 disqualifies it. One value per row, no history of earlier values, no default per provider.
- **Empty means the job area**, exactly as P19 was first written.
- **The value in force is the one the row held at submission, and nothing later moves a job.** Where the desktop chooses (P21's first case) it reads the row at that moment. Where the render worker chooses (P21's second case), **the manifest carries the value as it stood at submission, or the statement that none was set** (M1), and the worker reads it from there, never from anything later. A change to the row touches only jobs submitted after it; a re-run never chooses again (P21).
- **It governs P19's fallback and nothing else.** A job whose run passes P19 sits where its file points (P18), whatever the row holds. **A job with no render destination at all** (P18's last sentence) takes the fallback's place, so this clause's.
- **The chosen folder meets the conditions the run meets, and fails where the run would.** It fails where it is not an absolute path; where it would need a folder made directly under the machine's own root (P19 case 1); where it lies under the root of L1, the package area's location of L4a, or a shared temporary area (P19 case 2); where making one of its missing folders is refused — they are made by P20, one segment at a time, and the folders already made on the way are left; or where a destination's deepest predicted path does not fit N8's budget with the chosen folder as the prefix (N7). Where it passes, the job output folder is the chosen folder joined to N3's name.
- **Where it fails, the job output folder goes in the job area, and nothing new is recorded or said.** The case recorded is P19's — why the file's own place was not used — and the path recorded shows where the folder went; `Queue & History` Y8's telling of that case stays true, so no third case exists and no new wording is owed (the runner's ruling 4). **Where it fails in the job area too**, P12 case 1 and N10 govern as they always did. **Reversed later on 2026-10-04 by the build runner, under Rule 8** — the user chose that folder, so the job does not change place without a word: **beside P19's case, the job records one more value, that the server's chosen fallback folder was passed over**, yes or no. It is recorded where the case is — in the manifest's path field where the desktop chose (M1), in the job record directory where the render worker chose (P21) — and it reaches the entry by the same routes and is said there (`Queue & History` Y8). Which of the conditions failed is not recorded: the telling does not name it. P19's two cases are unchanged, and this is not a third: the case still says why the file's own place was not used.

**What this costs, stated.** ~~A chosen folder that cannot be used is not reported as such: the user reads the job area's path where they expected theirs, beside the reason the file's own place was not used.~~ *(Struck later on 2026-10-04 with the reversal above: it is reported, in one sentence, though not with its reason.)* And a chosen folder on a small mount fills that mount, as P22 says of a folder the file points at.

**And a path that is not absolute never reaches the row. Added later on 2026-10-04 by the build runner's ruling:** the Edit dialog refuses it where it is typed (`Servers` D3), so P23's absolute-path condition above guards only against a value that reached the row by some other route, and is kept. *Added later on 2026-10-04 at Build (piece 4): a chosen folder holding a `.` or `..` step fails as a relative one does, for L3's own reason — a comparison of text would walk round it.*

---

## 7. The expected-output inventory, the count and the collision record

### 7.1 Names: asked of Blender for one route, and built from the recipe for the other

**I1. The split is forced rather than chosen, and the reading records which of the two produced each name** so a mismatch found later says where to look.

- **For the regular output path, the name is asked of the Blender that will render.** Asked where a frame will land, the file's answer matches disk *"for a frame render and for an animation, templates and frame-number padding and all"*, and the one exception — a still driven through the script route — is one every job avoids because **every job renders as an animation** (`Rendering`'s Row 14). **And the asking doubles as a check**: a broken template in the regular output path raises the same complaint before any render begins that stops the render at a failing status, so a job that could never write is stopped before it costs anything.
- **For a file output node's outputs, the name is built from the version's own recipe**, because there is no route to ask. From 5.0 a node displays one assembled name for the whole node before anything renders, and **no way was found in any version for a script to read that assembled name** (*Writing 8*, *Reading a file 4*), so the displayed name is not a route.

**I2. The node recipe differs across the version boundary and the reading holds both.** 4.5 joins the node's folder, the output's own piece of path, the frame number and the ending; 5.0, 5.1 and 5.2 put **the node's own base file name** between the folder and the output's name. Observed on disk on 2026-08-17: 4.5.9 wrote `beauty0010.png` and **5.2.0 wrote `fixture_52beauty0010.png`** ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §1.4). **5.1 was not exercised**; [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 4. **Both halves of this recipe are the node's folder joined to something, and in 4.5's gathering mode the folder is not a folder — D8's separator rule is what makes this recipe predict the name that is actually written**, and without it the prediction and the file part company by one path level with nothing raised. **Corrected 2026-10-04 with D8: in 4.5's gathering mode the property's part after its last separator is the front of the file name, the redirect keeps it, and the recipe takes it where 5.0 takes the base file name.** ~~**How wide the frame number is was never established, and the recipe rests on the two names above being four digits at frame 10; [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 27 carries it, permanently unresolved. Added 2026-09-05, out of a code round** — I3's padding rule is written for the regular output path, which is asked of Blender rather than built, so nothing carries it across to a node.~~ *(Struck 2026-10-04: the width is now observed, below, and item 27 is narrowed.)* *(2026-10-18: the base file name is joined with its curly-brace expressions replaced, and an output's own name holding one is unresolved — **A curly-brace expression in a node's names**, below.)*

**Where the frame number goes. Added 2026-10-04, out of the Creator's note 14 on the test copy; observed in a test at the desk in 4.5.12, 5.0.1, 5.1.2 and 5.2.2, all four alike** ([Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 42a). The name is read from its last separator on — the base file name and the output's own name together. **The last run of hash marks there takes the frame number, zero-padded to the run's length and written whole where the number is wider, and nothing is added at the end**: `### - name` writes `001 - name` at frame 1 and `1000 - name` at frame 1000. An earlier run stays as written (`##_##` writes `##_01`), and so does a run in a folder part (`d##/x` writes `d##/x0001`). With no run there, four digits go on the end, a floor (`beauty0001`, `beauty1000`). The same holds for a node that gathers into one file. **Before this the recipe ignored hash marks**, and the Creator's job predicted `### - … Mattress0001.png` where Blender wrote `001 - … Mattress.png`.

**A curly-brace expression in a node's names. Added 2026-10-18, the Creator's thirteenth notes, notes 5, 9 and 10; no auditor has seen it.** A node's base file name — in 4.5's gathering mode, the folder property's name front (D8) — may hold the curly-brace expressions `Dependency Upload & Remap` G9 allows in the File Output node (*Dependencies 5*). Blender replaces each one before it writes, so the recipe joins the replaced text and never the raw text, and the values are those of the file Blender opens on the machine. From 5.0 the node holds a folder, a file name and a name per input (*Writing 2*), and in 5.3 a node made fresh by a script starts with its file name at `{blend_name}` (*Writing 2*), so the case is not rare. Observed: base file name `{blend_name} - suzy` with the output `Image` wrote `suzanne - suzyImage0001.png` at frame 1 from `suzanne.blend` (5.3 beta at the desk, and the Creator's downloads from 5.2.2 and 5.3, 2026-10-18; [Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 42b). Read as raw text, both of that job's node files were predicted under names Blender never writes: the frame was never counted as there, no preview was made, and the finishing message said 0 frames.

**What the research says, and what the reading does with each variable.** *Dependencies 5*: an expression is *"substituted at the moment the path is used so that the braces stay in the field, with a format specifier allowed after a colon and a literal brace written doubled"*. Every version's manual takes templates in the render output path and *"the compositor File Output node's output paths"*, and names none of the node's own strings. **The base file name taking them is observed (above). Whether an output's own name takes them — 4.5's slot path, from 5.0 an input's name — is unresolved**, so an output's own name holding a brace is left unresolved whole. Label: documented, except where a row says observed.

| Expression | Offered in (*Dependencies 5*) | What the research says it stands for | The reading |
|---|---|---|---|
| `{blend_name}` | 4.5, 5.0, 5.1, 5.2, 5.3 | the blend-file's name; observed as the name without its `.blend` ending (entry 42b) | **replaced**: the open file's name without its `.blend` ending |
| `{fps}`, `{resolution_x}`, `{resolution_y}` | 4.5 to 5.3 | named only | unresolved |
| `{blend_dir}` | 5.0 to 5.3 | *"the blend-file's whole folder as a full path"*, observed on Linux | unresolved: its exact spelling, a closing separator included, is not recorded |
| `{blend_name_lib}`, `{blend_dir_lib}` | 5.0 to 5.3 | *"the name and folder of the library a linked data-block came from rather than of the open file"* | unresolved: what they give for a node in the open file is not recorded |
| `{scene_name}`, `{camera_name}` | 5.0 to 5.3 | named only; restricted to render output paths | unresolved |
| `{node_name}` | 5.0 to 5.3 | named only; restricted to a path property on a node | unresolved |
| `{project_root}` and a project's own variables | 5.3, by its release notes | refused as unknown with no project active | unresolved |
| `{{` and `}}` | every version | one literal brace | **replaced** by `{` and `}` |
| any expression with a format specifier after a colon | every version | allowed; what it does to a value is not recorded | unresolved |

**How Smoozy gets the replaced text.** The reading runs inside the Blender that opened the machine's copy, and records, beside each node's raw base file name and each output's raw name, the text with every *replaced* row above put in. Each value is taken from what the reading itself reads in that Blender — `{blend_name}` from the open file's own path, which the reading already reads for a baked cache's default folder (D21b-i) — and **never from a call that claims to work out templates**. A name holding any other expression, an expression the running version does not offer, or a brace that does not close is **unresolved**, and the reading records it so, with the expression named. `the_name_owed_at` joins the replaced text, and the frame number goes in after the replacement, as above. A reading recorded before this carries no replaced text and is taken as its raw text.

**What an unresolved name does.** The output stays in the reading with its reason, and gets no inventory entry, so it adds nothing to the count (I13). The already-there test, C27's completeness statement and the preview's candidate list never wait for it, and its absence is never read as a missing frame: a frame counts as there when its other owed files are. The file Blender writes for it matches no entry and comes down at the ending walk (`Download` J8). Where that leaves a frame owing no file the reading can name, the frame set is recorded as **not predictable, with the reason named** (I9a's discipline), never as frames that write nothing (I10). An unresolved variable becomes replaced only when the Blender research records what it stands for in that version.

**A node's folder is read the same way. Added 2026-10-18 by the build runner, from the plan pass's finding; no auditor has seen it.** A node's folder may hold the same expressions, and the redirect (§6) would otherwise hand Blender a mapped folder still holding them, which Blender replaces as it writes, so the files land where the inventory does not look. So the folder is replaced by the same rule before the redirect maps it — `{blend_name}` and a doubled brace — and the mapped folder Blender is handed holds no expression. A folder holding any other expression leaves every output of that node unresolved, as above, and the redirect hands it on unchanged.

**The regular output path's folder is read the same way. Added 2026-10-18 by the build runner, from piece P's account; no auditor has seen it.** I1 asks Blender for the regular output path's *name*, but its folder is mapped from the recorded text, so `//../Render/{blend_name}/x_` would be handed to Blender with the braces in and predicted under the raw folder. Its folder is replaced by the same rule before the redirect maps it; any other expression in it leaves the regular output path's entries unresolved, as above.

**I3. The regular output path's recipe is the same in all four versions** — the frame number lands at the hash marks, padded to as many marks as there are or added on the end four digits wide when there are none, the padding a floor and not a ceiling, and the format's ending last unless the artist switched it off. It is asked of Blender all the same, per I1, because asking is version-correct by construction and costs nothing extra inside a reading that is already running. **A node's name follows the same padding, built rather than asked, with which run takes the number stated at I2** (added 2026-10-04, observed; entry 42a).

**I4. For a node in the job's own scene, the frame number is present in every version, because every job renders as an animation.** From 5.0 a node stamps the frame number only on an animation render; 4.5 stamps it every time. **That is the shape the observation covers**: I2's two names on disk, `beauty0010.png` in 4.5.9 and `fixture_52beauty0010.png` in 5.2.0, both carry it. **The one observation of a scene reached through a sequencer strip goes the other way**, in the same sitting: 4.5.9 wrote `sec0001.png` **with** a number while 5.2.0 wrote `fixture_52sec.png` with **none**. **That observation is carried with its source's own limit and with no gloss on it** — *"stated narrowly — one fixture, one scene reached through a strip, and the general rule was not established"* ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §1.4) — so **the sentence above is stated for the job's own scene and is not extended to a sequencer-reached one**, and this document neither reads the strip observation as a general rule nor denies it. Whether a node in a scene reached through a sequencer strip stamps the frame number from 5.0 is a question owed to Blender Research; [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 18.

**I5. The two together were checked against a real render and were right.** R6's 105 predicted against 105 written, on one fixture, in two versions.

### 7.2 What one frame owes

**I6. For each frame the job covers, the file is asked six things and the frame's owed files follow from them**: which view layers render at that frame; **how many views the scene writes** (I9a); what the regular output path writes; which file output nodes will run and what is connected to each; **whether the job's scene renders through its own sequencer holding a Scene strip, in which case its file output nodes write nothing at all** ([Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 13); and where two of those land on one name. **It was four until 2026-09-01**, when the repaired Blender research settled the two that were missing; both additions **take files out of the count**, and a count that is too high reads as a job with missing frames for ever. **The first question is answered with the job's pins applied** ([4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) item 5, *added 2026-10-04*): a layer the job switched off renders at no frame and a node input fed only by it owes nothing (I8). **The frame's owed files are the regular output's one file, plus what each running node owes, less what a collision swallows** — *collision* here being the count's own test of [7.4](#74-the-collision-record) and not the narrower collision record.

**I7. What each running node owes.** **One file for the whole node** where it is set to gather its outputs into a single multi-layer file, whatever the number of outputs — **the one mode in which 4.5 treats the node's folder property as the start of a file name, which D8 covers** *(as corrected 2026-10-04: the redirect keeps that start as the file name rather than ending it with a separator)*; **otherwise one file for each output that has something connected to it.** The mode follows the format setting in 4.5 and a separate media setting from 5.0, and the reading takes the mode rather than the count of outputs.

**I8. A view layer that does not render at a frame writes nothing at that frame, so a node input fed only by it owes no file.** That is the count's own settled clause and it is why [4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) item 5 evaluates per frame.

**I9. View layers never multiply the count.** A frame with several active view layers writes **one** set of files from the regular output path, never one per view layer; no view layer's name goes into a file name; and only a multi-layer file carries them all, **inside the one file**.

**I9a. Views are not view layers, they do multiply the files, and they change the names — the condition every counting rule in this document was measured under, and it was never stated. Added 2026-09-01, out of the repaired Blender research.** *"Every one of those answers, and every Overwrite test on this sheet, was taken on a scene with one view. With two views switched on, no file with that name is written at all — the files are `img_0001_L.png` and `img_0001_R.png`"* (*Frames*, on *Writing 8* and *Images 5*); with the settings as they come every version writes **two** files per frame, one per view, and told to put both views in one file every version writes **one** ([Where a render's files go](../Blender%20Research/Across%20topics/Where%20a%20render's%20files%20go.md)). Label: **observed in a test**, the multi-view half on one rented Linux machine and on no Mac.

- **So the reading establishes how many views the job's scene writes, and it is one of I6's six questions rather than an afterthought.** A one-view scene is the case every rule here is written for and nothing changes for it.
- **What breaks on a two-view scene is not one frame but the whole job, and it breaks silently.** The predicted name is never written, so **C12 to C15's already-there test finds every frame absent, C27's completeness statement reports every frame missing, and C17's carry-on re-renders the entire range** — over a job that rendered correctly and wrote both files for every frame. **The user pays twice and is told the job is incomplete both times.**
- **The render itself is fine and is not interfered with. What fails is only the prediction, and that is where this is answered.** Where the reading finds the scene writing more than one view, **the expected-output inventory is not composed for that job**: the reckoning records the frame set as **not predictable, with the reason named**, and C27's completeness statement says so in those terms. **Nothing then reads absence as evidence** — no frame is called missing, no carry-on re-renders anything, and the count is withheld rather than being wrong. That is the same discipline C23 already applies to a status that cannot be trusted, applied to a name that cannot be predicted.
- **This document composes no per-view names, deliberately.** The suffix form was observed for stills on one rented Linux machine and on no Mac, and for a stereo movie the name differs **by version** — `stereo_0007-0009_L.mp4` in 4.5 and 5.0, `stereo_0007-0009.mp4` in 5.1 and 5.2 (*Images*). A recipe built from that would be assembled out of several questions, which this stage does not do.
- **Whether Smoozy should carry such a job at all, and what the user is told, is not this document's and is not decided here.** No spec mentions views anywhere, so there is no promise to read; it is routed as a hole at [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 25. **What is closed here is the silent harm**, and it is closed whichever way that goes.
- **What a stop landing between the two files leaves has never been tried**, in any version. It is [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 24, and the refusal above is what keeps it off the path.

**I10. Frames that produce nothing are counted as nothing.** A frame with no view layer active on it renders nothing and writes nothing, and a job can carry a stretch of those mid-range. A count that multiplied frames by outputs would promise files that are never coming.

**I11. What a node writes need not be the render's own picture, and the count is unchanged by that.** It is still one file per frame.

### 7.3 The count

**I12. The job's count is the owed files summed over its frames, and it is never a multiplication.** It is a constraint on the count rather than on the method, and the method above satisfies it because the reading is already per frame.

**I13. The count is of the files the job is expected to leave on disk, not of the writes it asks for.** A collision is two writes and one file, so it is counted once. `Download` measures arrivals against this number, so a number that can never be reached would leave a job short of done for good.

**I14. The expected-output inventory is the count's own working, kept rather than thrown away.** It is what everything downstream reads and it holds **one entry per expected file**: the predicted path, the frame it belongs to, which route produced the name, and the destination that aims at it with its mapped folder — **or both destinations, where two of them aimed at that one path** ([7.4](#74-the-collision-record)). **The count is the number of those entries.** Nothing derives a count a second way, and nothing keys the inventory per destination, which would count a collision twice against I13.

**The added write places of [5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) are carried in the same inventory as folders** — each with its mapped folder, no predicted per-file entries, and no contribution to the count. A bake or a cache writes files this stage has no recipe to name and no method to count, and I13 states the harm exactly: *"a number that can never be reached would leave a job short of done for good."* Overshooting a count leaves a job readable as done; an unreachable one never resolves. **So `Download` still watches one folder and brings it home whole, and what it measures arrivals against is the count of the render's own predicted files.**

**I14a. The per-destination figure and the split by format are counts over this inventory, and the format is the destination's rather than a field of an entry. Added 2026-08-21, per Row 375.** [Design](../Design/Render%20Settings.md) states, for a file that has been read, each destination with how many files and which format it writes, and then the total split by format; I14's entry carries no format and nothing anywhere produced a per-format count.

- **The format is a field of the destination in the recorded reading** ([4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) item 2), one per destination, per version-correct evaluation, taken by the same reading that already takes I7's single-file mode and never by a second act. It is the field `Download` [N5](Download.md#63-matching-an-arriving-file-row-298) matches an arrangement's chosen formats against, and the one I1 and I3's recipes take a name's ending from. **No format is copied onto an inventory entry**: an entry already names the destination aiming at it, and a second copy would be a second mapping for N5's to disagree with.
- **The split by format is a count of I14's entries, grouped by the format the destination each entry names carries.** Every entry falls in exactly one group and the groups sum to the count (I12, I13), so a collision is counted once in the split exactly as it is counted once in the total.
- **The per-destination figure is the number of entries naming that destination, and it is not the total's working.** An entry naming two destinations is one file and appears in both figures, so **the per-destination figures may sum above the count, and the count is never derived by adding them** — which is I14's own *nothing keys the inventory per destination*. The two figures are different objects and how a screen reconciles them is Design's.
- **A colliding entry whose two destinations carry different formats is grouped under the format of the first destination the entry records**, deterministically, and which format the file left on disk actually carries is not decidable before the render — I16: what survives is the picture of the node built first, silently. [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 20.
- **A video destination's entry counts one, in that destination's own format, and carries [7.5](#75-the-video-shape)'s *may grow* mark into the figure with it** (I19–I20). The added write places of [5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) are in no count and therefore in no figure and no group.

### 7.4 The collision record

**I15. Two things are called a collision here and they are not the same set. Both are found by comparing the predicted names, and the reading records both.**

- **The collision record** — what the user is warned about, and what holds a job under [9.1](#91-the-three-conditions-and-where-each-is-established) — is the spec's own case: two destinations **in different nodes** aimed at the same name in the same place. Two outputs on one node cannot collide, because Blender adds a number to a repeated name as it is typed; a node group used twice in one tree is the same case as two nodes. **What the reading calls the two instances of a file output node inside a group used twice, and whether it produces two destinations for them at all, was never established; [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 29 carries it, permanently unresolved. Added 2026-09-05, out of a code round** — this bullet says where the case belongs and nothing anywhere says whether the reading can put it there.
- **The count's test** is wider: **any two destinations in the map predicting one path in one folder**, which is I6's *less what a collision swallows*. It takes in the one case the record does not: **a node destination landing on the regular output path's own predicted name.** Concrete in 4.5, where I2's node recipe is the node's folder, the output's own piece of path, the frame number and the ending — a regular output path of `…/out/beauty` and a node folder resolving to `…/out/` with an output named `beauty` both predict `beauty0010.png`, and P17 keeps two destinations that were the same place in the same place.

**The two sets can differ, and where they do the count is right and no warning is raised.** [9.1](#91-the-three-conditions-and-where-each-is-established)'s third condition reads **the collision record** and never the count's test, because the spec's warning is node-to-node.

**I16. What a collision leaves is one file, holding the picture of the node built first, silently.** So a pair contributes one file to the count — I13's *counted once*, applied to the count's test — and where the pair is in the collision record both members are recorded, because the warning names two destinations.

**I16a. Each member of a colliding pair carries where it comes from, by name, and the origin is a field of the destination in the recorded reading. Added 2026-08-25, per Row 493.** [Design](../Design/Render%20Settings.md) draws the pair's two lines naming each destination's origin — *the scene's output path*, *File Output node Beauty* — because the path alone is the same string twice, and the naming is what sends the artist to the right two places in their own file.

- **The origin is per destination, per version-correct evaluation, taken by the same one reading and never by a second act** — I14a's shape, one field further along. For the **regular output path** it is the route I14 already records; for a **file output node's destination it is the node's own name as the file holds it**, which no clause recorded before this one: I14's *which route produced the name* separates the two routes without naming the node, and R5's narrowing is about the will-run question, not a ceiling on a destination's fields — I14a already put the format on the destination by the same reading.
- **Nothing new is reached for.** The map already keys its pairs to the destination the reading found ([6.3](#63-the-rule) P10, [5.2](#52-what-the-launch-material-carries-and-it-is-composed-per-version) D9), so the reading already holds each node's destinations apart; this clause records the name it holds them apart by.
- **A node's name is the artist's own text and crosses as bytes under R4a** — never decoded, never substituted, never dropped — and every reader downstream compares and draws it as bytes.
- **Where the pair is in the collision record, both members' origins are recorded with them** (I16), so the warning names two origins beside two paths. What the two lines say and where they are drawn is Design's; the field reaches the pages through [Section 13](#13-what-this-document-supplies-and-to-which-document)'s standing supplies and no new route.

**I17. The collision record is computed once, over the map's new folders**, and [6.5](#65-the-invariant-the-rule-never-merges-two-destinations-that-were-distinct) is why that is the same answer as computing it over the original folders.

**I18. The collision record is one of the three conditions that hold a job** ([9.1](#91-the-three-conditions-and-where-each-is-established)); the count's wider test holds nothing and warns nobody.

### 7.5 The video shape

**I19. The shape is per destination and not per job.** No version's node can write video, and one render's own output cannot be a video and a set of images at once — the setting holds one value. **So a video destination can only ever be the regular output path, and a job can carry a video destination and frame-shaped node destinations at once.**

**I20. A video destination's inventory entry is one file, marked as *may grow*, and carries no per-frame entries.** The file is named for the whole range before Blender knows the render will finish; where the artist has Blender's own splitting on, a second file appears under a name of its own. **So Smoozy states one and says plainly that a long job may leave more, rather than promising a number it will have to break.** **Its name is asked of Blender (I1) with the scene's start and end set to the job's first and last frame**, for that one question and put back at once, so nothing else the reading reports changes; a job of the file's own range is asked at the file's own. *Added 2026-10-31, observed in a test* (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/A desk test — the name of a video over part of its range.md`, a controlled test in 4.5, 5.0, 5.1, 5.2, 5.3 beta and 5.4 alpha): for a video Blender answers one name built from the scene's start and end at the moment it is asked, whatever the frame, and a render of part of the range writes the name of the part (`vid_0001-0002.mp4` for frames 1 to 2 of a file saved at 1 to 12). Asked at the file's own range, a 2-frame job was predicted under a name never written and its finished video was reported missing (the live Telegram pass's fault 4).

**I21. Nothing judges a video destination's progress by watching the file.** Its size *"does not move while the render runs — it appears within moments at a token size and stays there, reaching its real size only when the file is closed."* **A size that does not move is not the same as nothing being written**, and neither reading is taken: [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#13-rules-that-bind-every-store) §1.3 rule 5 forbids it anyway.

**I22. Where any of a job's destinations is video-shaped, the already-there machinery is switched off for the whole job.** A re-run renders the whole range again, because there is no frame-shaped thing on disk to skip and a partly written video is not something a second run can carry on from. **Running the job again replaces what was left rather than adding to it**, so nothing has to be cleared first.

**I23. The branch is taken before submitting and the warning is worth nothing afterwards**, which is why the shape is in the recorded reading and therefore in the manifest or in the first attempt's record.

**I24. The finding under all of this rests on one container and one codec**, apart from a survey of what each container leaves after an interruption which came out differently for every one of them, and the spec says outright that none of it should be read as a fact about video output in general. Nothing above keys on a container, a codec or a size.

---

## 8. The main output, the preview source, and the derivatives

### 8.1 The walk, and its three declared outcomes

**B1. The main output is found by following the beauty pass forward from where the render enters the compositor.** The beauty pass is called **Combined** in every version — in a written file and in a multi-layer file's list of layers — and the render enters on a **Render Layers node**, which names the scene and the view layer it reads, so the entry point can be picked out. **The socket the beauty leaves that node on is called `Image`**, which is a different name from the pass's own and is the name the walk starts at. **Nothing marks the picture once it is travelling, in any version**, so the beauty is followed by wiring and never recognised further along. **Which view layer the walk enters at. Added 2026-10-04, out of the Creator's note 14 on the test copy:** the walk starts at the Render Layers node reading **the first view layer that renders at the job's first frame, with the job's pins applied** ([4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) item 5) — never at a layer the job switched off. No clause said which layer, and the build took the job's first pinned layer whatever its pin; that is expected, not seen, to be why the Creator's job named its own folder as its main output.

**CORRECTED 2026-09-20, out of a Build reading, and this is the third of three places rather than all three.** This clause said the pass is called *Combined* *"in every version — in a written file, in a multi-layer file's list of layers, **and on the socket it comes out of**"*. **The third is not true.** Every Render Layers node in 4.5.12, 5.0.1, 5.1.2 and 5.2.2 LTS was read at the desk, under Cycles and under the built-in engine alike: **the socket the beauty leaves on is called `Image`, and no socket on the node is called `Combined` at all.** Label: **observed in a test** (2026-09-20, `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Piece 13 — what landed.md`); the citation is [Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 46.

**What it had cost, and it was not the render.** A walk matching the socket by `Combined` matches nothing on any real file, so **every walk comes back *no entry*** and B8's outcome falls on every job rather than on a failed one: *show outputs* opens the job's own folder, the preview falls to the last image, and B9's duplicate-picture warning never fires. **No render is stopped** — the map, the inventory, the frames and the launch are all composed without the walk.

**The other two places are untouched and stay so.** The name inside a written file and the name in a multi-layer file's list of layers were not read against and nothing here contradicts them; **[B19](#83-the-previews-candidate-list) rests on the second and does not move.** **Nothing substitutes a name to make something work**: `Image` is what the four Blenders printed, and a name with no source would be written down as an absence instead.

**B2. The walk reads back out of a saved file with no window at all, complete, in every version**, which is what lets it run inside the reading of [Section 4](#4-one-reading-of-the-file-and-everything-reads-it) rather than needing a render.

**B3. The four limits are the answer's whole difficulty and each is handled by the walk stopping rather than guessing.**

- **A link cannot be walked across the boundary of a node group**, so the walk steps into the group's own tree.
- **A splitting node puts its first input on the right in 4.5 and on the left in 5.0, 5.1 and 5.2**, so which side carries the beauty is version-dependent and the walk is composed per version like everything else here. **Which side the file's own socket order puts first was never established, and this limit settles only the version's half of it; [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 28 carries the other, permanently unresolved. Added 2026-09-05, out of a code round.**
- **Where a two-picture node's factor sits between its ends, both inputs contribute and nothing says which contributed what.** The walk stops.
- **Which input came out can be told for five named kinds of two-picture node and for no others.** At any other kind, the walk stops. A freshly added mixing node starts at a different factor in 5.2 from the other three, which is a reason the factor is read rather than assumed.

**B4. The walk records one of three declared outcomes, and that is how failure is detected.** It is a stated result of the reading, never an empty answer inferred from a missing one.

| Outcome | What it means | What follows |
|---|---|---|
| **reached** | the walk arrived at one or more destinations, and at the final output node or not | those destinations are the main-output candidates; the regular output path is among them exactly when the walk reached the final output node **and the scene's own frames are written**, which is B5's last paragraph |
| **blocked** | the walk met a node it cannot pass, naming that node | no main output |
| **no entry** | no Render Layers node for the job's scene and view layer, or the compositor does not run and the case below applies instead | no main output, unless B5 |

**B5. Where the compositor does not run at all, the raw render lands at the regular output path and no walk is needed** — the beauty reaches it by definition, and that destination is the main output. That is the *No file output nodes* and *nodes carrying only utility passes* case in one rule.

**B5 narrowed, 2026-10-18, by the build runner, from piece P of the Creator's thirteenth notes (notes 5, 9 and 10); no auditor has seen it. A compositor with no Group Output still runs where a live File Output node is in it**, so *does not run at all* means neither a live final output node nor a live File Output node. Observed: `suzanne.blend` and a stand-in, each with two Render Layers nodes into two File Output nodes and no Group Output, wrote both node files in 5.3 beta and 5.1.2 at the desk and in 5.2.2 and 5.3 on a rented server (2026-10-18; [Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 42b). Where such a node is reached by the beauty, **B7 applies and that destination is the main output**, so the preview leads with the layer's own picture and not the regular output path's, which on the Creator's file was a fully transparent picture. The regular output path stays an output the job writes and is counted as I13 counts it. **So B5's *nodes carrying only utility passes* case holds only where a Group Output is in the tree**: with none, such nodes still make the compositor run, the walk from the beauty reaches nothing, and the job has no main output (B8) rather than the regular output path's picture. *(Added the same day, from piece P2's account.)*

**B5 and B4's table both hold only where the scene's own frames are written, and the regular output path is a main-output candidate nowhere else. Added 2026-09-05, out of a code round; this section never read D12's switch and [§7](#73-the-count) always did, so one switch was being read two ways.** From 5.2 that switch stops the scene's own frames being written even with a path handed in on the command line, and the inventory already returns nothing for the regular output path at every frame where it is off. **Where the switch is off, B5's shortcut does not apply and the regular output path is no candidate**, so a job whose beauty reaches no file output node falls to B8's *no main output*. **This is the plan catching up with the spec above it and not a choice about the product**: [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md) already says a job can have no main output at all *"as well as in Blender 5.2 with the regular output path switched off"*, and calls that *"an ordinary, working setup, not a fault."* **What it changes for the user**: on such a job *show outputs* opens the job's own folder rather than one that stays empty for the job's life, and B9's duplicate-picture warning stops counting a place nothing is written to.

**B6. Where a sequencer strip produces the output, the scene's own compositor result never reaches the scene's own file**, so the walk does not deliver the regular output path as a destination the beauty reaches ([Where a render's files go](../Blender%20Research/Across%20topics/Where%20a%20render's%20files%20go.md), *The compositor 7*). Observed in the same shape on 2026-08-17, as data out of the reader: `sequencer_takes_over: true, compositor_runs: false`.

**B7. Where the beauty reaches a destination inside a file output node, that destination wins over the regular output path**, whatever it is also writing. That is the Creator's decision about which output matters and it is applied, not re-derived.

**B8. No main output means *show outputs* opens the job's own folder and the preview falls to the last image and nothing better.** It is not a safety net; it is what every job whose walk did not get through gets, which is why B4 makes the outcome explicit.

**B9. The duplicate-picture warning is read off this same walk and off nothing else**, so Smoozy warns only where the beauty really does reach more than one destination.

**B9 narrowed, 2026-10-12, on the Creator's eighth notes, note 4, and their answer that day** — [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md)' fourth bullet carries it. **The regular output path is not counted where its destination's format is video-shaped** (I19: only that destination can be). The count is the file output node destinations the walk reached, plus the final output node only where the regular output path writes images. **A second fact is read off the same walk**: the walk reached the final output node, the regular output path writes a video, and at least one file output node destination was reached. That is told as two outputs named plainly, not as the warning. Both are composed with H8's six things off the recorded reading, so the settings page and the queue entry read one answer, and H8 still lists six: this is the fourth told another way. *(B9 above is unchanged in its words; until this note a video at the regular output path counted as a second destination of the same picture. No auditor has seen it.)*

**A muted file output node is no destination of the walk: the walk ends there and counts nothing (R4 item 2).** Found on the Creator's twelfth notes, note 6: a file whose only output was a video was said to write frames from a File Output node that was muted. *Added 2026-10-18; no auditor has seen it.*

**B10. Thirteen of the seventeen terminal tests behind the walk settle Linux and are untested on macOS**, which is that question's own recorded limit and not a reading taken here — *"The Mac half of the other thirteen of the seventeen terminal tests has simply not been run."* ([The compositor](../Blender%20Research/Comparison/The%20compositor.md), question 8, which [What a job writes](../Specs/Sources/What%20a%20job%20writes.sources.md) names for the walk). Every walk here runs on the rented machine, which is Linux, so the untested half is not on the path this document takes.

### 8.2 The two picks, made once and never recomputed

**B11. Two picks can be arbitrary and both must be stable for the life of the job**: which candidate is the main output where two or more are left in the same format class, and which file the preview reads where two are left after every other rule.

**B12. The split between working and viewing formats is applied and not built for.** Anything that is not PNG or JPEG is a working format; where only those two are present PNG is the working one; where only one format is present it is both. That is the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-13 in the spec, and Row 297 closed there.

**B13. Each pick is made once, at the moment the recorded reading is composed, and is written into the job record on the machine as part of that reading.**

**B14. The desktop takes both picks into the job's history record the first time it learns them, and after that the history record is authoritative and nothing re-derives them.** That is step 4 of [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#7-reconciliation-on-the-desktop) §7 — filling in what the local record does not hold from the machine's own notes — and it needs no mechanism of its own. **The history record is user-held** ([State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#111-five-classes-and-the-reason-there-is-no-sixth) §11.1), so the pick lives as long as the entry and survives the machine being destroyed, which is the ordinary end of a job.

**B15. Nothing anywhere recomputes a pick.** A pick recomputed at each opening satisfies every other sentence in the spec's section and breaks this one, and nothing would show it but a user noticing the preview changed. **Where the desktop never learned a pick and the machine is gone, the job has no main output** and falls to B8, rather than being re-derived into a possibly different answer.

### 8.3 The preview's candidate list

**B16. The candidate list is the expected-output inventory filtered to the files that are present, and it is not the main output's folder.** The preview reads across everything the job wrote.

**The same list is where the moving picture and the contact sheet get their source**, which [Telegram](../Specs/Telegram.md) already makes this document's — *"which file each picture comes from is [What a job writes]'s"*. Their source is the job's **frame-shaped candidates at the destination the rungs of B17 and B18 chose**, in the frame order the inventory holds; a job with no frame-shaped candidate has neither. **Producing them is the media worker's** under `Rented-server runtime`, and how many of that source a picture draws on is `Telegram`'s and Design's; this document names the source and nothing further.

**B17. The rungs, in order, applied over that list.** The **beauty pass**; failing that, the **diffuse colour pass**, which is `DiffCol` in 4.5 and `Diffuse Color` from 5.0 — so the rung needs to know which Blender wrote the file, which the manifest names; failing that, the latest image, whatever pass it turns out to be.

**B18. Within one rank the viewing format is preferred**, wherever in the job's folder it sits. **Where two files are left after that, the one in the main output's folder is read**, which is the walk of [8.1](#81-the-walk-and-its-three-declared-outcomes) being read a second time rather than a second walk. Where the job has no main output, or neither file sits there, B13's pick decides.

**B19. Inside a multi-layer file the picture is chosen by name, which is the same reading as choosing the file.** The beauty is `Combined` under the name of the view layer it came from; a picture written there by a file output node takes the name of the output that fed it.

**B19a. A picture inside a multi-layer file is found by its name and never by its position, and nothing this document composes or reads picks one by position. Added 2026-09-02, out of [Open Threads](../Groundwork/Open%20Threads.md)'s *Settings 19* item, which asked for a reading rather than for a research question.** [Where a render's files go](../Blender%20Research/Across%20topics/Where%20a%20render's%20files%20go.md): *"Find a pass by its name and never by its position"* — with the denoising feature passes stored, **how many of them there are and what order they sit in both change between the versions**: 5.2 stores five where 4.5, 5.0 and 5.1 store three, and 5.1 orders its three differently from 4.5 and 5.0. Label: **observed in a test** (*Settings 19*).

**The reading was taken across this document and the answer is that nothing here picks by position.** B19's rungs name `Combined`, `DiffCol` and `Diffuse Color`, which are names; the name recipes of [7.1](#71-names-asked-of-blender-for-one-route-and-built-from-the-recipe-for-the-other) produce file names and never channels; the walk of [10.2](#102-the-reading-the-reckoning-is-made-against) C7 reads a path, a kind and a size and never opens a file; and B26's two routes hand a whole file to Blender's own image handling or to the bundled library, neither of which is given an index. **So no clause moves**, and the rule is written down so that the next clause reaching inside a multi-layer file inherits it rather than re-deriving it. **What breaks where it is broken:** a version-dependent wrong picture — a preview showing a denoising feature pass in place of the beauty, on one version only, silently, with nothing downstream able to tell.

**B20. A file Smoozy cannot treat as finished is not a candidate at any rung, and the exclusion is exactly the two it can see.** An **empty file** is never a candidate ([10.3](#103-the-zero-byte-rule-and-the-limit-it-does-not-cover)); and where the render was interrupted, **the last frame asked for that is present** is not a candidate either, for the same reason [10.4](#104-the-last-frame-asked-for) re-renders it. **The third case is not excludable and is not pretended to be**: a file left by a write that ran out of room carries the name the finished frame would have carried and Smoozy does not tell it from a finished one. That is the spec's own standing and [10.3](#103-the-zero-byte-rule-and-the-limit-it-does-not-cover) states the same limit from the other side, so the two do not disagree.

**Separately from that, a video-shaped destination is not a preview candidate at any rung.** The rungs read an image, a video file is not one, and B26 has no route for it. **A job whose only destination is video-shaped falls to B21** — **except its first-frame check**, which is read as a picture (R4c) and so has a frame-shaped destination and a preview by these rules *(added 2026-10-27, Creator-stated; no auditor has seen it)*; a mixed job keeps its preview from its frame-shaped destinations ([7.5](#75-the-video-shape) I19).

**B21. Where the job has written no image at all there is no preview**, which covers a job that writes nothing and a running job before its first frame lands.

### 8.4 The derivatives: one set, made once, on the machine

**B22. The still copies are produced once on the rented machine and both readers read the same set.** `Queue & History` fetches and holds copies and produces none; `Telegram` reads the same derivatives. That is [The shape](The%20shape.md#each-rented-server)'s and it settles the question [Open Threads](../Groundwork/Open%20Threads.md) parked about whether the machine-side copies and the app's copies are one piece of work or two: **one.** **The moving picture and the contact sheet are not in that set and are not defined here** — [The shape](The%20shape.md#each-rented-server) gives them to the media worker, and what this document owns of them is the source file named at [8.3](#83-the-previews-candidate-list). [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 17 records what that leaves open.

**B23. A derivative is produced on the machine, by the worker driving the Blender the manifest names, headless and needing no connection, and by nothing else, because there is nothing else.** The desktop produces none — R3 splits the two sides in the same shape, and the ordinary case for the failure is [Telegram](../Specs/Telegram.md)'s: *"the bot sends and receives from the machine itself, and Smoozy need not be running or even installed on a computer that is switched on."* Observed on 2026-08-17: **outside Blender the machine carries no image tool of any kind, and the system Python carries no imaging library**; installing one plans six further packages and nothing was installed ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §6.4).

**B24. The cost is about 0.8 seconds idle and about 2.4 seconds with all four cores taken by a render, with the output byte-identical between the two and the render still progressing.** Blender's own startup dominates; the work itself is about 0.2 seconds (same, §6.1). **Arbitration between a preview or media worker and a render worker is `Rented-server runtime`'s**, which owns scheduling and exclusion for every worker class that can contend; what this document declares for it is that a derivative is **low priority, cancellable, and never blocks a render**.

**B24a. The fifth declaration for the preview kind: nothing declared, which A1 reads as unlimited. Added 2026-08-29 at the stage-close gate repair, from `Rented-server runtime`'s own seam check of A1 against its owning documents ([Rented-server runtime](Rented-server%20runtime.md#16-unresolved) §16 item 28).** [A1](Rented-server%20runtime.md#71-what-each-owning-document-declares) took four things until 2026-08-22 and now takes five; the fifth is **machine-level exclusion of the kind's own kind** — whether the machine admits more than one operation of that kind at a time. **B24 was written against the four and names no exclusion, so this states the one it does not carry; B24 itself is unchanged.** **This kind declares nothing at the fifth, which A1 reads as unlimited**: [Section 12](#12-atomic-or-partial--this-documents-declarations) makes a job's derivatives **partial across derivatives, atomic within one** on §9.2 test 1 — nothing promises an outcome about the set — so no promise here wants the machine held to one derivative at a time, and a declaration of one would serialise per-frame work against that. **What keeps derivatives out of a render's way is the class and not a count**, which is B24's *low priority, cancellable, never blocking a render*; the rule that acts on the fifth declaration is `Rented-server runtime` A5a and it is not reached by this kind. **The media kind's declarations are not this document's** and are `Telegram` §12 M5's (B22, and `Rented-server runtime` W1).

**B25. The colour treatment is the job's own view transform, read out of the `.blend` in the reading of [Section 4](#4-one-reading-of-the-file-and-everything-reads-it) and carried in the manifest.** The file records no treatment, so something has to choose, and the choice is the whole difficulty. Observed on 2026-08-17, same frame under three choices: the job's own transform blew out **none** of the picture, a plain choice blew out **23.7%**, and every preview was fetched and looked at — all three read plainly as the render with textures visible, **and a copy with no treatment chosen does not**, shading gone and surfaces flat clipped colour (same, §6.2). **That is the failure mode and it is obvious rather than subtle.**

**B26. The route is chosen by the source file's kind, and the split is forced by what was observed rather than preferred.**

- **A single-picture working format** — single-layer OpenEXR, Radiance HDR — is loaded, scaled and saved by Blender's own image handling, which behaved identically in all three versions.
- **A multi-layer file** takes the image library bundled inside Blender's own Python, **pointed at Blender's own colour configuration**. Loading a multilayer OpenEXR as an image gives no pixels in all three versions and **the scaling call then silently succeeds and changes nothing**, while the saving call writes at full size; the compositor route produced the small copy in 4.5.9 and **did not in 5.0.1 or 5.2.0** (same, §6.3). **The bundled library is the one route observed to work for multilayer in all three**, so the gap in that sitting does not bite.
- **The multi-layer route's names, and how it finds the picture. Added 2026-10-03 in the Build stage, out of a desk lookup on 4.5.12, 5.0.1, 5.1.2 and 5.2.2 LTS; label: observed in a test ([sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 52a). No auditor has seen this text.** Until that day none of this route's names was on record and a multi-layer source had nothing made. **Every name below was enumerated off each of the four Blenders, run, and is the same in all four.** The library is the module `OpenImageIO` in each Blender's bundled Python; Blender's own colour configuration is the file `config.ocio` in the folder `bpy.utils.system_resource('DATAFILES', path='colormanagement')` returns. The copy is made in four steps:
  1. **The picture is found by name, part by part** (B19a): every part of the file is opened with `OpenImageIO.ImageBuf(path, part, 0)`, `part` running over `nsubimages`, and the picture is the part's channels named `<view layer>.Combined.R`, `.G`, `.B` and, where present, `.A` (`spec().channelnames`), taken out in that order with `OpenImageIO.ImageBufAlgo.channels`. **The channel names are those in every shape a file comes in**: 4.5 writes one part holding every view layer, ordered by name rather than as the scene lists them; 5.0 onward one part per pass per view layer, each part also named `<view layer>.<pass>`; and 5.0 onward with *Interleave* (`image_settings.use_exr_interleave`) one part again. A file holding no such channels has nothing made. **The view layer is the first that feeds the destination** (`the_view_layers_that_feed_it`, read at §4.2) **and renders at the frame**, else the first that renders at the frame, and that is the name B31a's record carries. **The frame's layers are item 5's set read with the job's choices** — the pins the render applied, the manifest's own, by R4 item 5's one rule. *(Added 2026-10-04, on the build runner's ruling: a regular output path is fed by every layer the file switches on, so a layer the job pinned off — absent from the file — could be the one picked.)*
  2. **It is scaled to B26a's target** with `OpenImageIO.ImageBufAlgo.resize`, the size given as `OpenImageIO.ROI(0, width, 0, height)` — the order `xbegin, xend, ybegin, yend`, read back off a made value, since the library's own signature names its arguments only by position.
  3. **It is given the job's own view transform** (B25) with `OpenImageIO.ImageBufAlgo.ociodisplay(picture, display, view, fromspace=…, colorconfig=…)`: the display is the one a factory scene starts with in the running Blender (`scene.display_settings.display_device`, `sRGB` in all four), which is the display Blender's own route uses for a single-picture file; the source space is the configuration's `scene_linear` role (`OpenImageIO.ColorConfig(path).getColorSpaceNameByRole("scene_linear")`, `Linear Rec.709` in all four); and `colorconfig` is the file above (B27). **The configuration is always named** — 5.1 and 5.2 also set it in the process's surroundings and 4.5 and 5.0 do not.
  4. **It is written as an eight-bit PNG** (B28) with `write(path, OpenImageIO.TypeUInt8, "png")`, the format named because the staging path's ending is Smoozy's own.

  **What the copy came to: Blender's own PNG of the same frame, to a mean difference of 0.0012 and never more than one level in 255, in all four** — the same agreement as the single-picture route. A transparent frame's copy matched to 0.0004 with its transparency exact; the same file given the plain view transform was 0.0156 off; with two view layers the first layer's copy matched that layer's own PNG and was 0.046 off the other's; a 9000×3000 frame was written at 7500×2500 and 208 KB; one copy took 0.5 to 1.4 seconds. **A multi-layer file a file output node gathers is not made by this route**: its pictures carry the node's own entry names (B19), and the reading records the node and not the entry the beauty reached, so such a frame has nothing made and its record says why. **The regular output path's multi-layer files are told apart by the recorded format in every version** — from 5.0, `image_settings.file_format` reads `OPEN_EXR_MULTILAYER` once the media setting is `MULTI_LAYER_IMAGE`, as it does in 4.5. **Bound**: every reading was taken on the Creator's Mac; the configuration's folder on Linux is the same call's answer and was not watched there.

**B26a. The derivative's scaling has a target rather than only a direction, and the target is the tightest cap any consumer places on it. Added 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close, routed by the runner from [Telegram](Telegram.md#21-unresolved) §21 item 18.** B26 said *scaled* and named no dimension and no byte size, and one consumer's caps are exact. **Two of the three are published**, in the sending service's own words on its own page: *"The photo's width and height must not exceed 10000 in total. Width and height ratio must be at most 20."* **The third is this project's own observed cap** — **10,485,760 bytes** — which the same page publishes as *"The photo must be at most 10 MB in size."* and which `Telegram` I3 reconciles as *"ten mebibytes, which is that page's *10 MB* said precisely"*. (`Telegram` I3, reading `https://core.telegram.org/bots/api` on **2026-08-20**.) **So a derivative is produced to sit under all three**, and that is a requirement on the production rather than a check at the point of sending. **This matters because a full-size derivative is demonstrated rather than hypothetical**: on the multi-layer route B26 names, the scaling call silently succeeds and changes nothing while the saving call writes at full size — so without a stated target an 8K multi-layer job produces a derivative that no consumer can take, for every frame. **Where the source cannot be brought under the caps at all** — a ratio beyond 20 is the one shape scaling cannot fix — the derivative is produced as small as it can be made and the fact is recorded with it, so a consumer refuses knowingly rather than being surprised. **Nothing here changes which route produces the copy**, which is B26's, or what it is written in, which is B28's.

**B27. The bundled library is never pointed at anything but Blender's own colour configuration.** Observed: pointed at nothing it fails or guesses, and the obvious guess a general tool makes is plausible and is **not** what the job was made under; only the route through Blender's own configuration reproduces it (same, §6.4).

**B28. The derivative is written in a viewing format, and that is what removes a question this document cannot answer.** The fetched copy is opened on the user's own computer, and **Windows cannot be posed at all today**. A viewing-format picture is the one thing all three systems open without Smoozy carrying an image library, so nothing about reading a working format ever crosses to the user's end. **Where the picture carries transparency, it is laid over a checkerboard of light grey and white squares, each a 24th of the copy's shorter side, before it is written; the copy itself carries none.** *Added 2026-10-17, Creator-stated, the Creator's tenth notes, note 7: Queue, History and Telegram show transparency as the checkerboard, and the phone draws no backdrop of its own, so the copy carries it and every consumer — the contact sheet and the moving picture included — shows it unchanged. An opaque picture is unchanged. B26's observation that the multi-layer route keeps a transparent frame's transparency exactly stands; this lays that picture over the checkerboard after it. **Two facts it rests on are not on record**: whether the single-picture route's copy keeps the picture's transparency at all, and the bundled library's names for filling a checkerboard and laying a picture over it in each Blender. ~~Label: **unresolved**, to be settled by a desk lookup on real Blenders before it is built.~~ **Settled the same day by a controlled test in Blender 4.5.12, 5.0.1, 5.1.2, 5.2.2 and 5.3 beta, on a Mac** (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/Note 7's lookup — transparency in a preview copy.md`): the single-picture route's copy keeps the picture's transparency, scaled or not, from a PNG or a single-layer EXR; the bundled image library lays a picture over a checkerboard with `ImageBufAlgo.checker`, `ImageBufAlgo.over(picture, board)` and `ImageBufAlgo.channels(…, (0, 1, 2))` to drop the alpha, **the board needing an alpha channel of its own** or `over` refuses. Label: **observed** (controlled test, 2026-10-17). No auditor has seen it.*

**B29. A derivative is produced into a staging path and published by a rename, under [Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step) §7.5.** Blender writes straight to the final name and grows in place, so a derivative produced directly at its final name can be fetched half-written and shown to the user as the render. **This is the one unit in this document that publishes over an existing name** — M6 removes the need for the extension from the job-material unit entirely, because a job identity is never reused — so S17's declared degradation applies here and nowhere else in this document, and [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 1 states what breaks.

**B30. Derivatives sit in the job record directory and never in the job output folder** (L7, L8), so no derivative is ever counted by the inventory, watched by `Download`, or brought home as an output.

**B31. What a derivative is made of is one named source file**, chosen by [8.3](#83-the-previews-candidate-list). A running job's preview follows the newest candidate the rungs allow; when that is refreshed and how it is shown is `Queue & History`'s.

**B31a. A derivative's record carries the name of the view layer its picture came from, where its choosing establishes one, and only where the file holds more than one view layer. Added 2026-08-25, per Row 496 — the machine half; the entry half is `Queue & History` P10a's.**

- **The name is established exactly where the picture's own choosing names a view layer.** Inside a multi-layer file the beauty is chosen as `Combined` under the name of the view layer it came from (B19), and that name is the record's; a picture a file output node fed is chosen by the name of the output that fed it (B19), which names no view layer, and no name is recorded — **except where exactly one view layer feeds that destination, whose name is then recorded** (the last bullet; *changed 2026-10-12, Creator-stated, the Creator's ninth notes (2026-10-11), note 2*). For the **regular output path's ordinary image** the name is the file's **first** view layer, the only layer such a file holds (I9; `Queue & History` P10's own ground). *(From 2026-10-17, where the file holds more than one view layer, that image names no view layer and records its source instead, by the last bullet: it holds the first layer's picture or whichever the compositor was set to read (`Specs/What a job writes`, *A frame with several active view layers*, observed), so no layer's name can honestly be given. The Creator's tenth notes, note 2. No auditor has seen it.)* Absence is a recorded state of the derivative's record, never a gap.
- **Where the file holds one view layer no name is recorded** — there is nothing to tell apart, which is the design's own drawing condition kept as a rule about the record rather than a second reading at the draw. Whether it holds more than one is read off the reading's own evaluation, which already enumerates every view layer per frame ([4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) item 5, I6) — **with the job's choices applied, as B26 reads it** (added 2026-10-04).
- **A view layer name is the artist's own text and is carried as bytes under R4a's discipline.**
- **The name is written with the production and journalled with it**, so the desktop learns it by the event it already fetches on (`Queue & History` P3), holds it beside the fetched copy on the job record, and step 4's fill-in carries it where a connection learns the job late — no new route and no third one. Nothing re-opens B15: the pick, and with it the name, is made once and never recomputed, and a derivative produced before this rule carries none, drawn as none.
- **One copy per View Layer, each naming its own. Added 2026-10-10, from the Creator's seventh notes, note 4; no auditor has seen it.** Where B26's multi-layer route makes a frame's copy, `Queue & History` P1 has it also make one for each other View Layer in the frame's set whose `<view layer>.Combined` the same file holds, by the same four steps with that layer's name in step 1; each copy's record carries that layer's name by this clause's rule, whether it is the frame's main copy (B26 step 1's pick, unchanged), and its place in the frame's set. The key those copies are held under is `Queue & History` P4's: (job, frame, View Layer).
- **Where the frame's copy is not made by the multi-layer route, each other view layer gets its copy from its own destination. Added 2026-10-12, Creator-stated, the Creator's ninth notes (2026-10-11), note 2; no auditor has seen it.** Each other view layer in the frame's set gets a copy from the destination B19's rungs choose among those whose only feeding view layer it is — `the_view_layers_that_feed_it`, read at §4.2, holding that layer and no other — and its record names that layer, carries the main-copy mark unset and its place in the frame's set, under P4's key. A destination fed by more than one view layer is no other layer's source: it can give the frame's main copy and nothing else, which covers the regular output path (fed by every view layer the file switches on) and a node gathering several layers into one file, whose picture names are not known (B19). A destination fed by no view layer is no layer's source either. **A main copy made from a file a file output node fed with exactly one view layer names that layer**, and that layer gets no second copy. A view layer with no destination of its own has no copy, and nothing is made or said for it. Each copy is made by B26's four steps with the chosen file in step 1, and B25, B15 and P1a/P1b's owed reading apply to it as to the main copy. Only where more than one view layer renders at the frame is any of this done, by the second bullet's condition.
- **A copy whose record names no View Layer, in a frame of more than one, records its source: the regular output path, or the File Output node by name.** *Added 2026-10-17, the Creator's tenth notes, note 2; no auditor has seen it.* The desktop words it as the duplicate-picture and collision lines already do — *Output path*, *File Output node Beauty* (`Design/Render Settings`, `Design/Queue & History`) — so a frame's main copy is never unnamed beside named ones. A node's name is the artist's own text and is carried as bytes under R4a's discipline, like a view layer name. It is written with the production and journalled with it, by the fourth bullet's route.

---

## 9. The three holds

### 9.1 The three conditions, and where each is established

**H1. Three conditions hold a job before its render starts, and the list is closed.**

| Condition | Established by |
|---|---|
| **a destination Smoozy cannot bring under the job's folder** | the map, [6.4](#64-when-a-destination-is-not-mappable-and-the-case-that-is-not-that) — a length that will not fit, or a 4.5 output name that climbs out of its node's folder. **It reads the whole destination set** of [4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) item 2, the added write places of [5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) included, because the spec grants this hold unconditionally for any path Smoozy cannot bring under the job's folder |
| **the job will write nothing at all** | the **expected-output inventory** of [7.3](#73-the-count) holding no entry — the count of I12–I14 at **zero** — and never the added write places of [5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not), which are in no count (I14), because the spec's case is a job that writes nothing the user asked the render for and a cache folder is not that. *(Widened 2026-08-25, per Row 495 — this cell read the destination set alone, and H1a states what that missed and why the count is the condition now.)* Count zero is produced two ways and both hold the job: the **render's own output destinations** in [4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) item 2 empty across every frame — in every version a tree whose nodes are all muted, unconnected, or reached by a compositing switch that is off, and from **5.2** the Save Output switch off with no node writing anything — and destinations standing while **no frame the job covers has an active view layer**, every frame owing nothing (I6, I8, I10). **The count is read as *the job will write nothing at all* only where every destination the rule covers is mappable and every frame's regular output name was answered; where either fails the inventory is not composed at all and this condition is not established. Added 2026-09-05, out of a code round** — a count of zero can be the arithmetic of an inventory that silently dropped what it could not predict for, and this condition read that as a job aiming at nothing |
| **two destinations aimed at one name in one place** | the **collision record** of [7.4](#74-the-collision-record), which is the node-to-node set and not the count's wider test |

**H1a. A job that renders nothing at all is the writes-nothing condition and not a fourth, and the condition consumes the count rather than the destination set.
