Added 2026-08-25, per Row 495.** As first written the second condition read [4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) item 2's destination set alone, and a job whose every frame has no active view layer has destinations that are not empty — so the condition never fired for it, while I10 counted its every frame as nothing and C14 called the same job complete: the reading already produced the fact and no condition consumed it. The count is where it is consumed now, and the count is a field of the recorded reading ([4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) item 7), so H2 stands unchanged. **The reason is named inside the one statement and is read off the reading's standing fields, never a new one**: item 2's destination set says whether nothing was aimed anywhere, item 5's per-frame set says whether nothing renders at any frame, and the words are Design's. **Item 5's set is read with the job's choices** — the pins applied by R4 item 5's one rule, the same set the count was composed from — and the reason is composed with the count and travels with it; the recorded reading stays the file's. *(Added 2026-10-04, on the build runner's ruling: read off the recorded reading alone, a job whose every layer is pinned off was told nothing was aimed anywhere.)* **No seventh statement exists** — the six are [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md)'s, settled there, and this document adds none — and H5's two triggers scope unchanged: where the file was read before submitting, the page stated the same count-zero fact and the user answered it by submitting. A video destination's entry counts one (I20), so a video-shaped job is not held by this condition, which is the outcome the destination-set reading gave it too.

**H2. All three are read off the recorded reading and none needs a second evaluation.** That is why they are here and not in three places.

**H3. This document names the three conditions; `Rendering` owns the mechanism.** Its Row 23 quotes the spec saying *"This module carries that out and decides none of it"*, and the release of a held job is `Rented-server runtime`'s machine-held answer-release path, which `Rendering`'s Row 4 already needs for the first-frame check. **Nothing here invents a second waiting mechanism.**

### 9.2 Where the hold is placed, and what releases it

**H4. The hold is placed on the machine, after the file is loaded and before the render begins.** It has to be: the answer may arrive from a phone with nobody connected and Smoozy closed. **And from 5.2 it has to be before Blender is asked to render at all** — with no live node at all that version refuses the render outright before any frame is started, so a writes-nothing job left to the run's own complaint would never reach a hold. That is a reason for where the hold sits and not a clause of the condition it holds on.

**H5. The two triggers differ and the difference is the spec's, not this document's.**

- **The cannot-bring-under condition holds the job whichever side computed the map**, because the spec states it unconditionally. Where the map came from the manifest, the queue entry has carried it since submission and the hold is where the user's answer is taken.
- **The writes-nothing and colliding-pair conditions hold the job only where the map was computed on the machine**, because where the file was read before submitting the user was already shown those two and answered by submitting. The spec's own wording is *"Where that read finds…"*, and *that read* is the one at job start.

**H6. The user is not asked the same question twice**, which is the whole reason H5 is written as two triggers rather than one.

**H7. What the answer does is release or stop, and neither writes anything this document owns beyond the attempt record.** `Rendering` and `Rented-server runtime` own the release; `Queue & History` owns the entry.

### 9.3 What the machine produces for a person who is not there

**H8. Where the file was not read before submitting, the machine produces the six things and they reach a queue entry that may be read much later, from a different computer, with nobody connected at the moment they were worked out.** The six are the folders it is writing into, that it is writing nothing where that is so, that two of its destinations collide where they do, that the same picture is being written to more than one place, that its compositor is shared with another scene, and that its output is a single video file. **All six are fields of the recorded reading** — [6.3](#63-the-rule) P11, [9.1](#91-the-three-conditions-and-where-each-is-established), [7.4](#74-the-collision-record), [8.1](#81-the-walk-and-its-three-declared-outcomes) B9, [4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) item 4, and [7.5](#75-the-video-shape).

**H9. They reach the desktop by the two routes that already exist and by no third one.** The recorded reading is journalled as an event when it is written, so a reader learns of it by reading forward from its own durable cursor ([State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#4-the-event-journal-and-acknowledgement) §4); and it is in the job record for step 4 of [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#7-reconciliation-on-the-desktop) §7 to fill in. **Nothing polls and nothing is pushed.**

**H10. The queue entry is `Queue & History`'s and what it says here is this document's**, which is the spec's own division and is kept.

---

## 10. Completeness: already there, the last frame, the exit status, and the stop

**Everything in this section sits inside [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#13-rules-that-bind-every-store) §1.3 rule 5's one carve-out, and inside its limit: it is a rule about what to render, never a reading of state.**

### 10.1 The reckoning runs on the machine

**C1. The reckoning is a step of the render worker, before the render is launched, and it runs on the machine.** The spec settles that *Smoozy* does the reckoning and hands Blender the frames it wants; on the machine the render worker is Smoozy. **It needs no connection**, which is what makes the automatic re-run after a machine comes back with nobody connected and Smoozy closed possible at all.

**C2. Blender's own way of skipping frames is not used and cannot be.** It looks at one path only — the one the regular output path would write — it judges by bare existence so an empty file counts as done, and **it does not reach file output nodes at all**. Handing over a list of frames instead works in every version, and the list may mix single frames and stretches.

**C3. The list handed over is composed from the reckoning's own integers and never from text.** A malformed frame list **renders nothing at all and still reports success** (*Frames 7*), so the list is never assembled from anything a user typed or a document parsed.

**C4. The desktop may perform the same reckoning while it is connected, and what it produces is a reading it shows and never acts on.** It reads the same inventory by the same rule. **The machine's reckoning is the one the render uses.** `Rendering`'s carry-on figure is that module's and is shown against this reading.

**C5. The result of the reckoning is never written as state.** What is written is the attempt record's frame list, which is a record Smoozy wrote and is therefore a state fact under [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#13-rules-that-bind-every-store) §1.3 rule 5 rather than an exception to it.

**C6. Where any destination is video-shaped the reckoning does not run at all** and the attempt covers the whole range (I22).

### 10.2 The reading the reckoning is made against

**C7. One walk of the job output folder and everything under it, taken locally by the render worker, returning each entry's path relative to the job output folder, its kind and its size, compared against the expected-output inventory.** It is a walk and not a listing because [Section 6](#6-bringing-a-destination-under-the-jobs-folder) deepens paths by construction (P3, P7), so the inventory's predicted paths sit in subfolders for every job with more than one destination, and a single directory's entries would miss them. The inventory is the map in force for **this** attempt ([4.4](#44-the-file-identity-and-when-a-recorded-reading-goes-stale) R14), so a job whose file changed is reckoned against the arrangement it is about to write into and not against one it wrote into before.

**C8. Size comes with the walk and costs nothing extra, which is what makes the rule buildable.** Observed on 2026-08-17 at protocol level with the server's own logging: a 15-entry listing cost **zero per-file stats**, and **5010 entries cost 52 round trips rather than 5010** — one per file would have been 651 seconds against the 18.22 measured — while the command-channel walk returned kind, size, time and name for 5010 entries in **about a quarter of a second** ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md) §5.4). **So the reading the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-12 rests on costs what the ruling assumed it costs.**

**C9. A reading that did not complete is not a reading.** Observed: **partial output is delivered before a connection dies**, and a truncated listing and a genuinely short one were both four lines, *"separable only by exit status"* (same, §5.6). **So the reckoning proceeds only on a reading that ended successfully**, and a reading that did not is a failed attempt rather than a folder that was found empty.

**C10. Nothing here reads a file's presence or size for any purpose but this rule.** The completeness statement of [10.6](#106-the-completeness-reading-and-the-one-signal-a-lost-write-leaves) is derived from the same one reading and is not a second one.

**C11. Nothing compares a file's size against an expectation of what a whole frame should weigh.** Smoozy has no idea what a whole frame of a given scene should weigh, and *"opening a frame proves nothing"* — a part-written file **opens** and can report the full picture size.

### 10.3 The zero-byte rule, and the limit it does not cover

**C12. A frame counts as already there only when every file it should have produced is present and none of them is empty.** *Should have produced* is **the inventory's entries for that frame, one per expected file** (I14), which is I6's per-frame reckoning kept — and it is why the count is worked out frame by frame rather than multiplied.

**C12a. Beside its frame list the reckoning says one further thing: whether any frame it did not hand over was one an earlier attempt had written. Added 2026-09-04, out of a code round, for the telling [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md) settles and R14 routes.** It is **a frame in the job's range that the reckoning did not hand over and that owed at least one file** (I14). Such a frame counts as already there only because its files were found present and not empty (C12), and nothing but an earlier attempt writes into the job output folder — **so it is a frame an earlier attempt wrote and this one keeps.** The *owed at least one file* half is what leaves C14's produce-nothing frames out: nothing was ever rendered for one of those, so keeping it is not reuse. **This reads nothing new.** It is derived from the list the reckoning composed and the inventory it composed the list against; it takes no second walk (C10 is untouched), no comparison of the two maps R13 keeps, and no reading of any file's presence or size beyond the one C7 already took. **A change can move part of an arrangement and leave the rest, and this clause answers that without a rule of its own**: it asks whether *any* frame was kept, so a job landing partly in each case reads as one where frames were reused — which is true of the frames that were, and is the half the entry's sentence is about. **It carries no count**, for the reason `Queue & History` N9a's last bullet gives — a number of frames reused under the earlier arrangement would want a walk of that arrangement, which no clause here takes.

**C13. A file of zero bytes is never a finished frame, wherever in the range it sits.** This is not Smoozy judging whether a picture is whole; it is Smoozy refusing to count nothing as something. **Two observed routes put a nought-length file at a frame's own name, and the first of them is not a lost write at all.** A machine restarted mid-render left eleven whole frames and *"Six further files sat beside them at **zero bytes**"* — `f_0012.png` through `f_0017.png`, *"the placeholders Blender writes ahead of itself"*, so *"the output folder said seventeen and the truth was eleven"* ([The rented machine](The%20rented%20machine.md)). **Those six are frames never written rather than writes that failed**, and without this rule all six count as already there. The second is a lost write: an abruptly stopped render left short files that on Linux *"were exactly zero bytes"* (C33). **Not every lost write leaves one**, and this rule does not claim otherwise — on a full disk the file left behind is short and not empty, which is C15's stated limit and C29's, and no zero-byte test reaches it.

**C14. A frame that should produce nothing is already there**, since there is nothing owed and nothing to wait for. **And where every frame of a job produces nothing, the job is complete and the re-run has nothing to do**, which overrides `Queue & History`'s own rule for a job that wrote nothing before it died.

**C15. The limit is stated rather than hidden: a file that is short but not empty still counts as already there.** So a machine that went down can leave a job reporting complete with a hole in it, where the file left behind happens to hold some bytes. **Where that bites, the two signals are the count coming up short and what the machine printed**, which is [10.6](#106-the-completeness-reading-and-the-one-signal-a-lost-write-leaves) and nothing more. **Two unresolved facts decide how often this limit is met, and both are named here rather than only at C29. Added 2026-09-01, scenario replays *The server disk fills up during render* finding N-b and *The provider reboots the machine* finding 1.** **Fact 28** — whether a partway fill of the machine's mount leaves a truncated file at the final name, unposed on Linux and permanently unresolved ([Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 23). **Fact 44** — what reaches the disk when a machine powers down abruptly, unposable on anything this project runs; **a provider's forced hardware maintenance is the ordinary occasion for it rather than a corner case**, which is what makes this limit a routine exposure rather than a rare one. **On both, the harm is the same and it is the one C13's zero-byte rule was written to prevent, arriving by the one route that rule does not cover.**

**C16. A folder existing is not enough, and the reckoning never reads one.** It compares the inventory's predicted paths against the walk's paths (C7). A folder with a file missing from inside it is a frame that is not done.

### 10.4 The last frame asked for

**C17. The last frame Smoozy asked for that counts as already there is re-rendered anyway.** It is the last one **in the order Smoozy asked for**, which Smoozy knows because it wrote the list.

**So the carried-on range opens *at* that frame and never after it, and this is said plainly rather than left to be read off the arithmetic. Stated 2026-09-02, at the runner's ruling on the disagreement with [Rendering](Rendering.md#62-carrying-on-and-the-figure-read-from-the-machine) A4.** That frame is the one the render was part-way through writing when it stopped, so it is the one frame in the range that can hold a file which is present, is not empty, and is not whole — the shape C15 states as its limit and [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 23 states as permanently unresolved. **A frame that may be truncated is never counted as already there.** That is the whole reason this rule exists and it is why the rule re-renders the frame rather than reading it. `Rendering` A4 composes the figure the user sees for *carry on from where this stopped* and follows this clause; **where the two ever read differently this one governs**, because the other ending is a broken picture kept under a finished frame's name for good, which is the one outcome `Whole app`'s third promise does not tolerate.

**C18. The list is the previous attempt's frame list, recorded in that attempt's record before that render started** ([3.4](#34-the-attempt-records-beside-it)). The reckoning walks that list backwards and the first frame in it that counts as already there is the one re-rendered anyway.

**C18a. Where the immediately previous attempt record carries no frame list, the reckoning walks back to the latest record that carries one. Added 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close, routed by the runner from [Rendering](Rendering.md#9-unresolved) §9 item 17.** Two attempts write no list: one held at [Rendering](Rendering.md#2-the-attempt-pipeline) P1 step 5 writes a record with no list, and one failing before P1 step 7 writes no record at all. **C18's *previous attempt's frame list* is therefore read by when a list was written and never by how an attempt ended.** C20's whole range applies only where **no** attempt record carrying a list can be read at all. Without this a builder takes the immediately previous record literally, finds no list, and falls to C20 — re-rendering and re-paying for everything the last attempt already wrote.

**C19. It is not the highest frame number on disk, and the difference is real.** A plain range is walked forward ascending, a stepped range moves by the step and stops at or below the end, and **a typed list of frames is rendered in the order it was written, not sorted** — so the highest-numbered frame present need not be the last one finished.

**C20. Where no previous attempt record can be read, the attempt covers the whole range.** That is the safe reading and it is stated rather than left to be discovered: with no record of what was asked for, there is no last frame to name, and what an outright power loss leaves is not established. **The ground is [Fact 44](Facts.md) and its wording here is now the register's own. Corrected 2026-09-02, reboot replay finding 7; this read *Fact 44 is not posable on any machine this project can reach*, which the register withdrew on 2026-08-22.** No machine this project **runs today** can pose it, and **the blocker is what is installed rather than what exists** — the Creator's Mac has no hypervisor and no container runtime, and installing one is their own act. **That route is open and it is with the Creator** ([What Build inherits](What%20Build%20inherits.md) item 4); a rental could never have posed it, so the no-further-rental ruling does not reach this one. `Queue & History` D8 was brought to the same ground on 2026-08-29 and this clause is now level with it ([Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 7).

**C21. This rule and [10.3](#103-the-zero-byte-rule-and-the-limit-it-does-not-cover) are separate and both are needed.** That one reads the machine; this one reads a record Smoozy made before the render. A zero-byte file at exactly the name the finished frame would carry is *present*, and presence is what the already-there test reads — so without C13 an abruptly stopped job would treat a frame that was never written as finished, and without C17 the frame in flight would pass as done.

### 10.5 Nothing here reads an exit status as *everything was written*

**C22. The exit status is used for exactly one thing in this document: separating *the job never started* from *the job ran*.** A run that could not read the `.blend` at all ends at a failing status. **Nothing else about completeness is read off it.**

**C23. ~~Twelve~~ Twenty endings report success** — ~~*"Twelve separate endings report success, and in most of them the render did not do what was asked."*~~ **Corrected 2026-08-31: the joined answer was repaired on 2026-08-30 and the figure is now twenty, six of them ending at 0 with an empty output folder.** **The six matter more than the count**, and for anything deciding completeness they are the load-bearing fact: a gate reading only the status would call all six finished jobs with their frames written. **Nothing in this clause's own decision moves** — it never rested on the number. ([When a render ends](../Blender%20Research/Across%20topics/When%20a%20render%20ends.md), the joined answer that holds the count) — and this document's own share is exact: a node's write failing for any cause tried — a name taken by a folder, a folder refusing writing, a broken path template, a full disk — leaves the render running to its last frame at status 0 with every other file for that frame written; **a node's folder that cannot be made behaves the same way**, where the regular output path in the same position stops at the first frame and exits 1 with nothing written; and a failure raised inside a script that catches it leaves a successful exit status behind, which is why [5.4](#54-the-marker-because-a-launch-script-does-not-fail-loudly) D18 forbids the material catching anything. [The rented machine](The%20rented%20machine.md) records the same shape three more times from outside Blender — a full disk, a purged driver, a mismatched driver — each finishing at 0.

**C24. Exit 1 is not proof that nothing was written either.** A stray argument taken as a file name ended a run at 1 with the frame already on disk. **So a failing exit status never removes a file, never marks a job's outputs absent, and never skips the reckoning**: the inventory-against-listing comparison runs whatever the status was.

**C25. The rule behind the successful ending is exact and is not what it looks like.** A part-written frame never sets the exit status; what sets it is the **next** write, the one that cannot be opened at all. **So a one-frame job that runs out of room prints its ordinary saved line and ends successfully over a picture that is not whole.** Nothing in this document treats a job's last frame differently for that reason; C17 already re-renders it and C13 already refuses an empty one.

**C26. The words Blender prints for a failed write differ by version and by operating system — four wordings for one failure — so nothing here is decided by reading them.** That is also [Security and licence](Security%20and%20licence.md#13-logging-redaction) §13 rule 5 from the other side: a tool's output is captured whole or not at all and is never mined for a cause.

### 10.6 The completeness reading, and the one signal a lost write leaves

**C27. The completeness statement for a job is the inventory compared against one walk of the job output folder (C7), and it names four sets**: the files expected — **the inventory's entries, one per expected file** (I14) — the files present, the files absent, and the files present at zero bytes. **The count coming up short is that statement, and it is the only completeness signal this document produces.**

**C27a. Beside those four sets the statement carries one further reading, and it is not about files owed. Added 2026-08-31 with [5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) D21f.** For each of D20's four added write places it says whether the walk returned anything under that place's mapped folder. **Such a folder holds no inventory entry** (I14), so it belongs to none of C27's four sets, changes no count, and leaves *the count coming up short* exactly the signal C27 says it is. **D21f states when the reading is taken and D21g states what it does and does not claim**, and neither is restated here.

**C28. It names the absent and empty paths.** That is what makes the spec's *only signal* usable at all: the only signal a lost write leaves anywhere is a line naming the path in what the machine printed, and **the completeness statement is what says which paths to look for.** Whether anything searches the retained streams for them is `Queue & History`'s and `Desktop app and delivery`'s; the streams themselves, their durable retention, and the explicit outcome distinguishing *the render printed nothing* from *nothing was collected* are `Rented-server runtime`'s under Row 286, cited and not restated.

**C29. Counting the files that arrive does not catch a write that ran out of room, and this document does not claim it does.** Such a write leaves a file at the right name, Blender prints its ordinary saved line for it, and Smoozy does not tell it from a finished one. **The completeness statement counts it as present**, which is the same standing C15 already gives it, so the two do not disagree.

**C30. Whether Blender says anything about the disk beside that saved line is not firm**, and nothing here rests on it. The run that wrote OpenEXR printed lines naming it; what the two that wrote PNG printed was not kept. Label: **unresolved** — [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 5.

**C31. Nothing on the node records a failed write and Blender fires no hook for a failed frame write**, though it has one for a failed file save; 5.2 alone exposes its own report log to a script and with no window that log stays empty even for something that certainly errored. **So a program driving the render cannot be told, only read**, and C27 is the whole of what this document reads.

### 10.7 Why the stop must be the interrupt, and the video half that is only here

**C32. A render Smoozy ends is ended with the interrupt and never with the outright kill, and never with the polite request to close.** `Rendering` owns sending it under its Row 17; **what this document owns is why**, and the why is what each ending leaves rather than how quickly it arrives.

**C33. For a set of images.** An **interrupt** has never left a short image — not in one run, by anybody, in any version, on either machine, across hundreds of runs. A **polite request to close** and an **outright kill** each left the last image short in all eighty of their runs when the stop was aimed at the moment the file appeared.

**The zero-byte half is bounded to the batch that produced it. Narrowed 2026-09-02; this clause read *on Linux every short file was exactly zero bytes*, flat, and its own sources file already carried the bound.** That figure is **one batch's** — the largest of three sets of runs — and *Frames 12* says outright that **whether a terminate or a kill leaves a short image, and how often, is not fixed**: three sets answered it three ways and none was chosen. *Frames 13*'s own 160-run block over four image formats found **a spread of sizes on both machines**. **So the zero-byte shape is not a general property of a Linux short file and nothing here uses it as one** ([Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 58). **No further Blender research will settle which batch governs** — the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31 — so this is bounded permanently rather than pending ([Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 26).

**What the bound costs, exactly.** C13 catches a short image only where that image is empty, and on the wider evidence a terminate or a kill can leave one with bytes in it. **Such a file falls to C15's limit** — short but not empty counts as already there — **and what stands between it and being skipped is C17**, because a stop's short image is by construction the last frame asked for that is present. **The decision in this clause does not move:** C32 sends the interrupt, and the interrupt has never left a short image at all, in any run, in any version, on either machine.

**C34. For a video, the difference is total, and this is the half `Rendering`'s row does not carry.** An **interrupt** leaves a file holding the frames that were finished. A **polite request to close** and an **outright kill** each leave the file at its token size **with nothing in it**. So the gentler-sounding of the two endings is the one that loses the whole video, and for a video-shaped destination the choice of ending is the difference between the render and nothing. **There is no already-there machinery to recover it** (I22), which is why this is stated as a constraint on the stop rather than as something the re-run absorbs.

**C35. A file output node writes before the frame's own file, every frame**, so a stop landing between the two leaves the node's file short with nothing at all at the frame's own path. **The already-there test answers that without a rule of its own**: the frame owes a file that is not there, and where the node's file is empty C13 refuses it too.

**The machine bound, stated as I9a and D21b-ii already state theirs. Added 2026-09-02.** The ordering is *Frames 13*'s and it carries a condition this clause did not: it was **run on both machines for Blender 4.5, and on the Creator's Mac alone for 5.0, 5.1 and 5.2**, coming back the same shape in all three ([Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 58). **Smoozy renders on the machine that is not the Mac**, so for three of the four versions the ordering is observed only on a machine Smoozy never uses. **It will not be widened** — the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31 closes the further rental and the further research alike ([Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 26). **What breaks if the order is the other way round on rented Linux in 5.0, 5.1 or 5.2:** nothing decided here. A stop landing between the two files leaves one of the pair absent whichever is written first, the already-there test reads the frame as owing a file that is not there in both directions, and C17 re-renders that frame anyway. **This clause is a reason rather than a mechanism**, and it is narrowed instead of removed so that a later reader does not take it for a four-version, both-machine observation.

**C36. What must be signalled is the render's own process and not a shell around it.** A render had to be started detached to survive the connection at all ([The rented machine](The%20rented%20machine.md)), so the thing that must be reached is by construction not the thing the connection started. **The recorded execution group and its validation are `Rented-server runtime`'s and `State, identity and recovery` §5.2's**, cited here because this document's reason for the interrupt is worthless if it reaches the wrong process.

**C37. Nothing here matches an ending on its number.** The number a polite terminate ends with is recorded as `-15` by one topic and `143` by three others, and the joined answer says anything matching an ending on its number must accept both.

**C38. Where a machine goes down of its own accord nothing is sent at all**, and that case is the one C17 was always written for.

---

## 11. Cleanup, and the lifetimes this document applies

**K1. Two rulings are applied and no third rule is invented.** [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#113-the-two-rulings-those-documents-were-promised) §11.3 has already settled both halves and they are taken as they stand.

| Thing | Cleared when | Never cleared by |
|---|---|---|
| **a fetched derivative**, on the user's computer | its job record is deleted, **or its age ending is reached, whichever comes first** *(corrected 2026-08-22 — the never column below held **an age** and **a sweep** until the Creator's ruling of that date. **The period is thirty days**, `State, identity and recovery` §11.1 R16, ruled 2026-09-02 under the Creator's standing delegation; corrected here the same day, this having said the period was parked at that document's §13 item 12 for the Creator alone, which it no longer is)* | a cap, a schedule, or a machine still being reachable |
| **a machine-side still copy** — the set of [8.4](#84-the-derivatives-one-set-made-once-on-the-machine) B22, and not the media worker's moving picture or contact sheet | once the fetched copy exists, **or** the job's record is gone | anything earlier — the machine is the only place a derivative can be made |
| **a job record directory** | the job's record is gone, or the machine is destroyed | anything else |
| **a job output folder whose record was published** — standing, or since erased by `Queue & History` X2's owed erase, which removes the record and never the folder (M7) | it is the user's outputs and this document does not remove it while the machine lives; the removals are the machine-reaching server removal the specs already define, and the machine's own destruction | any age, any cap, any free-space reading, **any sweep** |
| **a job output folder whose record was never published** — the abandoned submission, and only that (M7) | the abandoned-material sweep reaches it (M7, K4) | — |
| **a staged directory or file this document published from** | its publication, under [Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends) §7.8. **A staged attempt record left by an interrupted publication never reaches that moment, and it stays until the job's record directory goes** *(added 2026-09-03 at the code round; this row gave a staged file its publication as its only clearing moment, and K4's sweep does not reach this one — the job's own record **was** published, which puts the whole submission outside that sweep for good)*. It costs one small file, in a directory the user never opens and which no inventory counts | — |

**K2. A fetched derivative is subject-held on its job record, and on its own age, and is not throwaway on the user's computer.** *(The age half was added 2026-08-22 on the Creator's ruling; this clause held the record alone.)* The shape calls the derivatives throwaway and that is true of the machine-side set only. `Queue & History` promises that *"A finished job keeps its preview"*, the ordinary end of a job is that the machine is destroyed, and once it is gone the copy cannot be made again — observed, *"Everything on the disk went with it … None of it was recoverable and no part of it was offered back"* ([The rented machine](The%20rented%20machine.md)).

**K3. Nothing on the machine is cleared on the strength of a free-space reading alone.** [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#114-on-the-machine) §11.4, and the reason is Fact 28's: the reading is a choice of mount, and a machine whose disk is full is precisely the machine whose records are most needed.

**K4. The abandoned-material sweep is bounded to the two directories this document names, and it is record-driven, never enumeration-driven.** It removes a job output folder whose record was never published, from the desktop's own record of the stopped submission (M7), and a staged directory left by an interrupted publication, under that contract's own S28. **Neither reaches anything the user put there, nor anything a render wrote** — a folder whose record was never published has held no render, since without the published record no job ever joined the line (`Queue & History` Q3) — and neither is triggered by a machine being reachable again ([Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#78-cleanup-and-the-five-ways-a-transfer-ends) §7.8 S30).

**K5. A downloaded output on the user's computer is in no class at all.** It is the user's file; nothing counts it, caps it, ages it or removes it.

---

## 12. Atomic or partial — this document's declarations

**Declared per action against [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#9-the-atomic-or-partial-boundary) §9, with the test that decided each. Nothing below is decided at run time.**

| Action | Partition unit | Test | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|
| **placing a job's material on the machine at submission** | the whole action | §9.2 test 1 — `Rendering`'s Row 2 promises an outcome about the whole job and about nothing smaller | **atomic entire.** M5's single directory rename is the publication; test 2 forbids reaching it by ordering, and Fact 78's negative two-file half forbids doing it file by file |
| **creating the job output folder** | the whole action | §9.2 test 3 — the record's publication is unsafe unless the folder exists, so the ordered pair is one unit | **one unit with the action above.** Where the folder cannot be made, the record is **not attempted**, naming the folder as the unit that stopped it |
| **composing and recording the reading** | the whole reading | §9.2 test 1 — five promises read it and a half-composed reading would be read as a whole one | **atomic.** It is published with the manifest, or written into the job record by a single rename where it is composed on the machine |
| **applying the redirect at launch** | the whole map, **the regular output path's own command-line argument included** (D10) | §9.2 test 1 — the promise is that *everything* a job writes lands under the job's folder | **atomic.** A map applied in part is a job writing to the artist's own machine, which is the failure Row 118 exists to prevent. The material sets every pair it can set, and for the one pair it does not it **reads the value back and fails on a mismatch** (D10) — so every pair is either set or verified, or the attempt fails before the render instruction. **This rests on the ordering of [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 19**: where the argument's value is not yet in force when the material runs, the read-back fails a correct attempt rather than passing a wrong one, so the failure is safe and total |
| **writing an attempt record** | the whole record | §9.2 test 2 — a half-written frame list read by a later re-run would name a last frame that was never asked for | **atomic**, by staging and one publication |
| **producing the machine-side derivatives for a job** | one derivative | §9.2 test 1 — no promise is made about the set, and `Queue & History` promises a preview rather than a number of them | **partial across derivatives, atomic within one.** Each has its own outcome; a failure names that derivative and does not abandon the rest |
| **fetching derivatives to the user's computer** | one derivative | as above, and the transfer contract makes each one atomic | **partial across, atomic within** |
| **clearing a job's machine-side material** | one directory | §9.2 test 1 — nothing promises an outcome about the set | **partial across, atomic within** |

**T1. No unit here is retried inside its action** ([State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#95-no-unit-is-retried-inside-its-action) §9.5). A failed submission is a new submission with a new job identity and a new folder, which is `Rendering`'s.

**T2. Two units above rest on Fact 78 and are written as atomic because [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#96-where-a-unit-must-be-atomic-and-the-ground-for-it-is-not-established) §9.6 says so** — the job's material, and a derivative's publication. **Fact 78 answered the interruption boundaries on 2026-08-18 on ext4, xfs and overlayfs, so these two units now stand on ground that exists rather than on a rule written against a gap**; [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 1 carries the answer and what is still open, which is the rollback boundary and Fact 44.

---

## 13. What this document supplies, and to which document

**To `Rendering` — its launch material and the reason for its stop.** The launch material of [5.2](#52-what-the-launch-material-carries-and-it-is-composed-per-version), composed per Blender version and naming the version it was composed for, **with the requirement that the Blender about to be launched is confirmed to be that version and the attempt fails before the render instruction where it is not** ([5.2](#52-what-the-launch-material-carries-and-it-is-composed-per-version) D7, D6); that the regular output path is delivered by its own command-line argument inside the after-the-file-before-the-render window ([5.2](#52-what-the-launch-material-carries-and-it-is-composed-per-version) D10, D11); that the material contains no failure handling of its own ([5.4](#54-the-marker-because-a-launch-script-does-not-fail-loudly) D18) **and that the launch line carries the failure-to-exit-status argument, without which the material's read-back raise (D10) is a paid wrong render at success ([Rendering](Rendering.md#13-the-window-and-the-order-of-the-line) L8a) — added 2026-08-29 at the stage close, this supply having stated no such requirement** and prints a marker naming the job and the destinations it set ([5.4](#54-the-marker-because-a-launch-script-does-not-fail-loudly) D16); the frame list for an attempt and the requirement that it is composed from integers ([10.1](#101-the-reckoning-runs-on-the-machine) C3); the immutable submission manifest as the thing a re-run reads rather than recomposes ([3.1](#31-what-the-manifest-holds) M2); **the card make L23's table is keyed on, carried on that manifest in M1b's form — added 2026-09-20, this supply having named no such field while `Rendering` L29 said the manifest carried the cards**; **the three conditions that hold a job and where each is established** ([9.1](#91-the-three-conditions-and-where-each-is-established)), the mechanism being that module's and `Rented-server runtime`'s; and **why the stop must be the interrupt, including the video half** ([10.7](#107-why-the-stop-must-be-the-interrupt-and-the-video-half-that-is-only-here)). **Rows 6, 7, 11, 17, 20, 22 and 23 are that document's**; this one supplies what each of them carries out, Row 20's carry-on figure being shown against the reckoning of [10.1](#101-the-reckoning-runs-on-the-machine) C4.

**To `Dependency Upload & Remap` — its asset and remap stores.** The asset area of [1.2](#12-the-areas-and-what-may-never-sit-under-a-job-folder), which is a sibling of the job area and **outside every job folder**, so nothing it places is counted by an inventory or brought home as an output; that the remap material it keeps on the machine sits there too; that the remap is delivered by the same launch material as the redirect and at the same moment ([5.2](#52-what-the-launch-material-carries-and-it-is-composed-per-version) D9); the path and name rules of [Section 2](#2-path-and-name-rules), including the byte budget and the plain create-directory rule; and, as an answer it was owed, that **the write places its own `[PENDING]` asks about are covered by the redirect** — the bake output path, the point cache, the geometry-nodes bake folder and the sequencer proxy folder are mapped, land under the job's folder and come home, on the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-18 ([5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) D20) — and that the one place no route here reaches is a path held in a custom property (D21).

**To `Queue & History` — its output readings.** The job output folder's path and its name, which is the job identity's textual form and, where a label was given, N3's bounded rendering of that label, with the full label authoritative in the record and on the entry ([2.2](#22-what-a-job-folder-is-named-for)); the recorded reading and every field of it a queue entry or a history entry shows — the folders, the count, the collision record with each member's origin (I16a), the duplicate-picture warning, the shared compositor, the video shape, and **a destination the map records as not mappable, with its reason**, which is a field and not a seventh warning, its presentation that module's and Specs' ([Section 4](#4-one-reading-of-the-file-and-everything-reads-it), [Section 7](#7-the-expected-output-inventory-the-count-and-the-collision-record), [5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) D21); **which folder *show outputs* opens**, which is the main output's, or the job's own folder where there is none ([8.1](#81-the-walk-and-its-three-declared-outcomes)); **which file the frame preview reads** and the derivative it reads it through, with the view layer name a derivative's record carries where one is established ([8.3](#83-the-previews-candidate-list), [8.4](#84-the-derivatives-one-set-made-once-on-the-machine) B31a); **that both picks are made once and are never recomputed**, and that the history record is where they live after the desktop learns them ([8.2](#82-the-two-picks-made-once-and-never-recomputed)); **what *already there* means for a re-run** and that a job which was never going to write anything is complete rather than re-run ([10.3](#103-the-zero-byte-rule-and-the-limit-it-does-not-cover) C14); and the completeness statement of [10.6](#106-the-completeness-reading-and-the-one-signal-a-lost-write-leaves), which names the absent and empty paths against which its raw record is worth reading. **Added 2026-08-31 with Row 313's close: that same statement carries, for each of the four added write places, whether anything was produced under its mapped folder** ([10.6](#106-the-completeness-reading-and-the-one-signal-a-lost-write-leaves) C27a, [5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) D21f) — **a field of that statement and not a warning and not a fault** (D21g), read afterwards with nobody connected like every other field of it, its presentation that module's and Design's. **Added 2026-09-04, out of a code round: the two facts N9a's telling is composed from** — R13's statement that the file changed before an attempt, and [10.3](#103-the-zero-byte-rule-and-the-limit-it-does-not-cover) C12a's statement of whether that attempt kept any frame an earlier one had written — **both fields of the attempt record** ([3.4](#34-the-attempt-records-beside-it) M9), read and never re-derived, with **no count of frames either way** (C12a).

**To `Download` — its inventory.** The expected-output inventory of [7.3](#73-the-count) I14, one entry per expected file, with the predicted path, the frame, the mapped folder and the destination or destinations aiming at it; **the count as the number of those entries**, and that the count is of the files expected on disk rather than of the writes asked for; that the added write places of [5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) are carried as folders with no per-file entries and are in no count; **the one folder to watch**, which is the job output folder and holds nothing else ([1.2](#12-the-areas-and-what-may-never-sit-under-a-job-folder) L7); **the video shape**, which is one file with the number stated as possibly growing and no per-frame entries ([7.5](#75-the-video-shape)); **that a file appearing in that folder during a render may not have finished being written**, and that an empty file is never a finished one; and **that the inventory is a statement about the machine and never about the user's computer** ([2.5](#25-the-output-names-are-not-touched-and-the-inventory-is-a-statement-about-the-machine) N14), which is Fact 29's negative arriving at that module's door. **Added 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close, routed by the runner from [Download](Download.md#15-unresolved) §15 item 12: the attempt record's frame list, by name** — the same list supplied to `Rendering` above, read for the order a later attempt renders in ([3.4](#34-the-attempt-records-beside-it), [10.4](#104-the-last-frame-asked-for) C18, C18a) — **and the route for all three of the inventory, the recorded reading and that frame list, which is the desktop-initiated read of the job record directory over the connection.** All three sit in the job record directory of [1.2](#12-the-areas-and-what-may-never-sit-under-a-job-folder), the desktop reads them there, and nothing is composed for that module or pushed to it. **Where one has not arrived, nothing here manufactures a substitute**: that module states what it does in that window and this document supplies no default. **Added 2026-08-31 with Row 313's close: that the ending walk of [Download](Download.md#7-what-ends-an-arrangement-row-220) §7 step 2 is the reading D21f's disclosure is taken from** — no walk of its own and no new request, since that walk already covers the whole job output folder and already reaches an added write place's mapped folder (J8) — **and that what it returns under each of the four mapped folders is what the completeness statement then carries** ([10.6](#106-the-completeness-reading-and-the-one-signal-a-lost-write-leaves) C27a). **Nothing about narrowing, fetching or the count moves**: such a file has no format and no entry and is already outside every count and inside every arrangement (N4, J8, I14).

**To `Render Settings` — what its page states about what the file will write.** Added 2026-08-21 for Row 375. The destination set with each destination's mapped folder ([6.3](#63-the-rule)); **the per-destination figure and the split by format** ([7.3](#73-the-count) I14a), with the per-destination figures not summing to the count where a collision stands; the count as the number of inventory entries and of files expected on disk (I12–I14); and the fields of the recorded reading that page states beside them — the folders, that it is writing nothing where that is so, the collision record with each member's origin (I16a), the duplicate picture, the shared compositor and the video shape ([9.3](#93-what-the-machine-produces-for-a-person-who-is-not-there) H8) — each carrying the limit its own clause carries. **There is no delivery and no route to build**: where the file was read before submitting, the desktop composed the reading, the map and the inventory itself ([4.3](#43-the-rule-runs-once-per-job-and-the-side-that-ran-it-writes-the-map) R7), so the page states figures over the object it made, computed nowhere else (R9) and asked of no machine. **Where no reading stands the page states none of it**, which is that document's job-submitted-with-no-read case and not a gap here.

**To `File Browser` — what its live-job warning is answered from, and the reading rule its listings keep. Added 2026-09-02; [The shape](The%20shape.md#12-file-browser) routes this supply and this section carried no entry for it.** The manifest `.blend`'s path, as the thing a live job depends on, the resolved set beside it being `Dependency Upload & Remap`'s ([4.4](#44-the-file-identity-and-when-a-recorded-reading-goes-stale) R15); **the job output folder's path and its name** ([2.2](#22-what-a-job-folder-is-named-for)), which that document's warning also covers — the spec's own words are *the folder and files a running job is writing to* — and which holds nothing but that job's outputs ([1.2](#12-the-areas-and-what-may-never-sit-under-a-job-folder) L7); and **that no reading of a folder is line-oriented and no rendered line is ever parsed** ([2.5](#25-the-output-names-are-not-touched-and-the-inventory-is-a-statement-about-the-machine) N15), which binds that document identically and which its L2 already takes by name. **What is deliberately not supplied is R10's four-value identity and R13's staleness test**: applying either would mean reading the machine at the moment a destination is named, which that document's W1 forbids for the warnings it answers, and its W5 records the omission as a decision rather than a gap. **Nothing is composed for that document and nothing is pushed to it** — the paths it needs are on the local job record it already holds.

**To `Telegram` — its preview source.** The same machine-side derivatives, in the job record directory, produced once and read rather than remade ([8.4](#84-the-derivatives-one-set-made-once-on-the-machine)); and that a derivative may be cleared only once the fetched copy exists or the job's record is gone ([Section 11](#11-cleanup-and-the-lifetimes-this-document-applies)).

**To `Server Setup`.** The root of [1.1](#11-one-root-one-mount-chosen-once) and the requirement that it is chosen once from a per-path available figure; **the package area's own location, chosen apart per [1.1](#11-one-root-one-mount-chosen-once) L4a where the candidate set offers more than one mount, recorded and handed over exactly as the root is**; **that the whole root is handed over to the login the supervisor runs as before hand-over**, because a rental's second place to write arrives owned by root ([1.3](#13-ownership-and-the-confirmation-that-a-placement-worked)); that the placement is confirmed by a real write of at least one byte at the exact path; and that the five areas of [1.2](#12-the-areas-and-what-may-never-sit-under-a-job-folder) exist before the first job so that no area is ever created deep ([2.4](#24-creating-a-folder-the-plain-form-never-the-make-parents-form) N11).

**To `Rented-server runtime`.** The file rules it reads: the layout of [Section 1](#1-the-server-directory-layout), the path and name rules of [Section 2](#2-path-and-name-rules), and that **no directory's presence is evidence of anything**. The declaration a derivative worker needs — **low priority, cancellable, never blocking a render** ([8.4](#84-the-derivatives-one-set-made-once-on-the-machine) B24) — against its own common arbitration mechanism. That the reckoning of [10.1](#101-the-reckoning-runs-on-the-machine) is a step of the render worker before launch and needs no connection. And that this document reads the retained streams' existence and the explicit collection outcome as that document's under Row 286, and produces the paths those are worth searching for.

**To `Security and licence`.** That the package area is outside every other area of [1.2](#12-the-areas-and-what-may-never-sit-under-a-job-folder) on both arrangements [1.1](#11-one-root-one-mount-chosen-once) L4a permits — a sibling of the job area directly under the root wherever L4a leaves it there, and on a mount of its own, under no shared root at all, where L4a places it apart — and that on either arrangement **no path this document synthesises for a job ever reaches inside it**: the job output folder and the job record directory of [2.2](#22-what-a-job-folder-is-named-for), every path mapped or predicted under them ([Section 6](#6-bringing-a-destination-under-the-jobs-folder), [Section 7](#7-the-expected-output-inventory-the-count-and-the-collision-record)), and every directory the removals of [Section 11](#11-cleanup-and-the-lifetimes-this-document-applies) K1 and K4 reach, which are a job output folder, a job record directory and a staged directory and nothing else. **The one thing this document composes that touches the package area at all is the area's own location** (L4a); what sits inside it is named by the documents L6 gives its contents to, §10's own token file among them. That is what its §10 relies on when it says the token is protected against the user's own browsing and every other Smoozy operation *"because those all run under paths `Job files and outputs` owns"*, and when it says no job cleanup can remove it by accident. **The property is reach and never space**: L4a's second arrangement is placement rather than protection, [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 21 states what it does not prevent, and nothing here is offered as a defence of the token's mount against a render filling a disk.

**To `Desktop app and delivery`.** That a fetched derivative is a viewing-format picture, so nothing about reading a working format crosses to the user's computer ([8.4](#84-the-derivatives-one-set-made-once-on-the-machine) B28); and that the inventory's names are the machine's, which its receiving-filesystem collision judgement under Row 303 is what stands between them and the loss observed on 2026-08-17.

**This document's diary declaration — it writes no act lines and no told lines, and that is its fixed list rather than a silence. Added 2026-08-31 under [Reporting a problem](Reporting%20a%20problem.md#3-what-goes-into-the-diary) J2a and J4a**, which require every feature document to declare in itself the acts it writes to the diary, in a fixed line form with the identities it carries, or to state in one line that it writes none. **It writes none**, and the four candidates are named with the test each fails so that the next reader is not left to re-derive them. Nothing here is new: no act is invented for this document to perform, and every act it takes part in is already promised, and already owed as a declaration, by the module whose spec promises it.

- **Placing a job's material on the machine and creating its job output folder** ([Section 12](#12-atomic-or-partial--this-documents-declarations)) — the act a user could name is *started a job*, which is `Rendering`'s Row 2 and that document's to declare. This document supplies the unit's shape and its atomicity, which is not an act of its own.
- **The reading of the file** ([Section 4](#4-one-reading-of-the-file-and-everything-reads-it)) — fails the second test. No spec names the reading's completion as something the user is told about or can see afterwards: it is a step inside submitting where the render worker takes it (R3), and a step inside a page's own preparation where the desktop takes it, the folders, the count and the six statements being what `Render Settings` and `Queue & History` state off the recorded reading afterwards. The spec's *read a file* is `File Browser`'s under J2's assignment and not this reading.
- **Fetching a derivative to the user's computer, and clearing one** ([Section 11](#11-cleanup-and-the-lifetimes-this-document-applies), [Section 12](#12-atomic-or-partial--this-documents-declarations)) — fails the third test. The unit is one derivative and there is no promise about the set, so a line here would be one per derivative, which is the rate J2a forbids in terms. The preview the user sees afterwards is `Queue & History`'s promise and that module's to declare.
- **The abandoned-material sweep and the cleanups of [Section 11](#11-cleanup-and-the-lifetimes-this-document-applies)** (K1, K4) — fails the second test. Nothing they reach is named as a completion the user is told about or can see: a job output folder whose record was never published is one no user could name, and a staged directory is the transfer contract's residue.

**And no told lines, the one reading that could look like an exception being another document's.** What this document composes on the machine — the recorded reading, the destination map, the expected-output inventory, an attempt record's frame list and the completeness statement — crosses to the desktop as material read out of the job record directory, or learned by reading forward from the event journal (H9), for `Queue & History`, `Download` and `Render Settings` to state. J4a rules that no journal event becomes a told line by itself and that what arrives on such a read becomes one only where the module consuming it declares it, so those three declare what they consume and this document writes none. **The one reading of this document's that the diary does carry is the Blender version confirmed before launch** ([5.2](#52-what-the-launch-material-carries-and-it-is-composed-per-version) D7), and [Reporting a problem](Reporting%20a%20problem.md#3-what-goes-into-the-diary) J4 already places that told line at the act that reads it, which is `Rendering`'s — declared there, not here.

---

## 14. Unresolved

Each entry names what is not established, what breaks, and what holds without it. **None of them stopped this document**; [Section 15](#15-moments-that-reached-the-creator) records what was checked, including the one that did reach a stop and the ruling that closed it, and the one this document raised that has never been put.

**Every entry below that wants a rented machine or a further Blender research question is permanently unresolved, and not one of them is pending. Added 2026-09-02.** The Creator ruled on **2026-08-31** that there will be **no further Blender research and no further rented machine for answering a question**; a machine rented at Build to run the app on is a different act, ruled the same day, and revives nothing here. **The entries that ruling reaches are 1's rollback boundary, 4, 5, 6's restart half, 8, 11, 13's strips-absent half, 16, 18, 19a, 22, 23, 24, 26, 27, 28 and 29.** *(27, 28 and 29 added 2026-09-05, out of a code round, and each carries its own permanence line.)* *(19a added to this set 2026-09-02: it was filed 2026-08-31 as an open Blender question with no label of its own, so it sat outside the set and read as a question still owed to a round.)* Each of them states what breaks and what holds without it, and **none may be read as work that is coming, or left a space in any document for an answer that will not arrive.** Where an entry carries its own permanence line, that line is the record and this paragraph does not repeat it. **One thing this ruling does not reach:** Fact 44 — what an outright power loss leaves, in entries 1 and 7 — could never have been posed by a rental at all, and its one open route is the Creator's own act of installing virtualisation software on their own computer, held at [What Build inherits](What%20Build%20inherits.md) item 4. **It is not scheduled either**, and nothing here waits on it.

**What the ruling does not reach, named by number so that nobody sweeps them in later. Added 2026-09-02.** **Item 2**'s standing half is a seam with [The shape](The%20shape.md#5-job-files-and-outputs) §5's scope line, recorded rather than proposed: it is closed by a sentence in another document and by no observation of anything. **Item 10** names a loss at the receiving end — the user's own computer — at a boundary `Download` owns and has rows for; no rented machine sits at that end of it. **Item 12** is a residue of what the reading reaches: a path this stage finds by no route at all, so what would close it is a further reading route decided here, not a fact from outside. **Item 15** is the record of how narrow the batch behind every rented-machine reading is; its own *Breaks* line names nothing, so it promises nothing and waits on nothing, and it is kept so that no later document reads a figure here as a description of rented machines. **Item 20** is a split whose deciding order nothing in the reading records, and [I14a](#73-the-count) already makes the grouping deterministic — so what is left is a choice this document has taken, not a question outside it. **Item 21** is [Method](Method.md)'s third escalation — a cost to the user no spec grants — and it is closed by a Creator ruling, whose record [Section 15](#15-moments-that-reached-the-creator) now carries. **Item 25** is a hole above this stage, and its own line says what closes it: a Creator ruling at Specs under Rule 7. **None of these seven is permanently unresolved and none may be written as though it were.** **The entries named in neither set — 3, 9, 14, 17 and 19 — each carry a close or an answer at their own head**, item 14's being the spec's own *permanently unresolved* label rather than this ruling's, so none of them needs a place in either set.

1. **Fact 78's single-rename half beyond ext4 — and the rental this batch used was a third filesystem nobody has posed it on.** Rename atomicity was observed on **ext4** with *"no crash injection at the rename boundary"*; a rental with an attached volume presented **xfs**; and the box of 2026-08-17, which is the one this document's readings were taken on, presented **`overlayfs`** ([One box with Blender on it](One%20box%20with%20Blender%20on%20it%20—%20the%20reading,%20the%20listing,%20and%20the%20names%20that%20do%20not%20come%20back.md), *The machine*). **ANSWERED, 2026-08-18, on all three filesystems — and this item said the register did not hold the overlayfs case until 2026-08-21, while three other documents had already been brought current.** Fact 78: the interruption boundaries were posed on **ext4** and **xfs** on the virtual machine and on **overlayfs** on the container, *"and every death was a real forcible kill without unwinding — which is the crash injection this entry recorded as never run, at every one of the four commit boundaries."* `State, identity and recovery` §13 item 2 and `Rented-server runtime` §16 item 3 both carry the update; this one did not. **What is genuinely still open is narrower, and it is what this item now names:** the **rollback** boundary, which was never interrupted mid-flight and is blocked twice over, and Fact 44's outright power loss. **Breaks — as this item stood, and now historical for the three tested boundaries:** M5's single-rename publication of a job's material, and B29's publication of a derivative — a publication interrupted at the rename boundary could leave a half-published job record that reads as a job, or part-arrived bytes at a derivative's final name. **What holds without it:** [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#96-where-a-unit-must-be-atomic-and-the-ground-for-it-is-not-established) §9.6, so the units are written as atomic and this document says so; M6, which removes the need for the POSIX-rename extension from the job-material unit entirely because a job identity is never reused; and [Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#75-publication-is-the-last-step-and-it-is-one-step) §7.5's S15 pre-state and S17's declared degradation, which make the derivative case a recorded act naming both paths rather than a silent one.

2. **PARTLY CLOSED 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close: `The shape` §5's scope line now says the root's areas are five with the Blender area among them, which was the half this entry shared with `Server Setup` §13 item 8. The rest of the entry stands.** The original entry follows, unchanged. **The shape's scope line says this document owns *atomic publication of job outputs into the job folder*, and for the files a render writes that cannot be kept.** Blender writes straight to the final name and the file grows in place — no temporary name ever appears — so **there is no publication step for a render's own outputs and no route to add one**. What this document does publish under the contract is everything **Smoozy itself** places: the job's submission material (M5), the attempt records (M8), and the derivatives (B29). **This is a seam with [The shape](The%20shape.md#5-job-files-and-outputs), recorded rather than a change proposed to it.** **Breaks:** nothing that was ever buildable — the whole of [Section 10](#10-completeness-already-there-the-last-frame-the-exit-status-and-the-stop) exists because a render's outputs cannot be published atomically, and a reader who took the scope line at face value would think the zero-byte rule and the last-frame rule were belt and braces rather than the only thing there is. **What holds without it:** the contract itself, unchanged, for everything it can reach.

3. **CLOSED 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close: `State, identity and recovery` §1.2 now carries the job record directory as a store, listing all eight kinds, and corrects its own head clause — the job-record area is a sibling of the package area rather than inside it, and *outside every job output folder* is the property everything there rests on.** **One half of that correction's wording was overtaken the same day and is routed rather than edited from here:** it reads *a sibling of the package area under the same root*, which holds only where [1.1](#11-one-root-one-mount-chosen-once) L4a leaves the package area under the root; where L4a places it apart the job-record area is a sibling of the root's other areas and of nothing on the package area's mount. **The property that section itself names as load-bearing — outside every job output folder — holds on both arrangements**, so nothing there is built on the half that moved. The original entry follows, unchanged. **The per-job record kinds are not named in [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#12-on-each-rented-server) §1.2's inventory of machine stores.** That inventory names the machine-local queue, the operation and command records, the event journal, the per-job note, the captured streams and the machine's own history copy; it names no submission manifest, no recorded reading, no inventory, no collision record, no pick and no attempt record. **This is a seam rather than a fact**, of exactly the shape [Server protocol and transfers](Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#12-unresolved) §12 item 13 already records for the per-transfer record, and it is named in the report to the runner rather than decided here: that inventory's one home is §1.2 and writing it a second time would give a fact two homes. **Breaks:** a machine-side store built to §1.2 as it stands has nowhere for M1, M8 or [4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) to land, and with them Rows 122, 126, 133 and 134. **What holds without it:** nothing about the records' shape or contents, which are settled in [Section 3](#3-the-immutable-submission-manifest-and-the-per-job-record) and are what the owning document needs in order to name them.

4. **Blender 5.1 was not exercised anywhere in the batch of 2026-08-17.** It was not installed and *"nothing here speaks for it."* **Breaks:** D8's property name for a node's folder, I2's node name recipe, the rewrite route of [5.3](#53-each-destination-setting-is-written-on-its-own), and the derivative routes of B26 are each observed in 4.5.9, 5.0.1 and 5.2.0 and inferred for 5.1 from the research's own version boundary. **What holds without it:** the research settles the boundary itself — the two values are named one way in 4.5 and another from 5.0 — so 5.1 is inside the from-5.0 branch on every one of them; the risk is a version-specific difference nobody has looked for, not a branch nobody has chosen.

5. **Fact 70's second half.** Whether *the render printed nothing* can be told from *nothing was collected* is unresolved; the plain half is answered — both streams are captured separately, kept, read back with nobody connected and searchable. **Breaks:** C28's route from an absent path to the line naming it, which is Row 136's only signal. **What holds without it:** C27's completeness statement, which is produced from the inventory and one listing and needs no stream at all; the absent and empty paths are named whether or not anything was printed.

6. **~~Fact 6's remaining half: a machine with more than one physical disk.~~ ANSWERED 2026-08-22 by the multi-provider batch, and the answer reverses the old one.** This entry used to read *the answer is that a rental's second filesystem is a quota on the same disk*. **It can be either**: one seller ships two real virtio devices, 97 GB on `/` and 787 GB on `/ephemeral`, as standard. L4 carries both shapes. **What remains open in its place, and it is narrower:** whether that large mount survives a stop, a start or a restart was **not posed**, and its name is the seller's claim. **Breaks:** nothing — L2 chooses by available space read per mount, which is right on both shapes. **What holds without it:** L4, which now forbids the expectation in both directions.

7. **Facts 1 and 2 at promise level, and Fact 44.** ~~Fact 1's promise level and Fact 2's promise level both wait on Batch E~~ — **both were posed on 2026-08-18 and this item is down to Fact 44.** **Fact 2 is answered yes**: the interrupt reaches Blender itself and never a shell, under all three signals on both machine kinds, so **C32's assumption is observed rather than assumed**. **Fact 1 is answered yes for a reboot and for a genuine kernel panic** — so H4's hold survives both, and it was watched surviving both with its hold unreleased — **and no for the disk-full form**, where the supervisor cannot start at all and, on a systemd machine, does not recover by itself. **What that does to C1 and C18 is nothing they did not already carry**: every durable write on a full disk was **refused loudly** with errno 28 rather than half-completing, so the machine's records stay truthful and the failure is the ordinary disk-full failure with its ordinary diagnosis. What is new is that reconciliation does not run at all until something outside the machine restarts the supervisor, which `Rented-server runtime` Section 16 item 12 carries and `Preparation/Groundwork/Open Threads.md` parks. Rows 123, 126, 133, 134 and 138 all touch them. Fact 44 — what a machine losing power outright leaves — is **unresolved and unscheduled, and it is not that no route exists. Corrected 2026-09-02: this read that it is not posable at all today**, which is behind this document's own paragraph opening this section and behind the register. [Facts](Facts.md) Fact 44 was corrected on 2026-08-22 away from that form: **the blocker is what is installed rather than what exists.** No rental could ever have posed it — the power of a rented machine cannot be cut over the network — so the ruling of 2026-08-31 does not reach it and does not close it. **The one route left is the Creator's own act of installing virtualisation software on their own computer**, a guest killed at the hypervisor, which touches no physical power and is held at [What Build inherits](What%20Build%20inherits.md) item 4. **Nobody may read that as scheduled**; it closes one half and leaves the other, since a guest machine still does not remove a physical drive's write cache, which is the half Fact 44's own reasoning rests on. **Breaks:** C1's reckoning and C18's attempt record both assume the machine's durable writes are on the disk afterwards; H4's hold assumes work waits for a person with nobody connected; C32's stop assumes the interrupt reaches the render. **What holds without it:** C20, which covers a lost attempt record by rendering the whole range; C13, which refuses an empty file whatever left it; and the nearest observed case, a kernel-level reset that left *"no frame's contents at all"* including a frame reported saved seconds earlier — which is the case C15 already states as the limit rather than the case anything here claims to survive.

8. **A launch script that cannot be read does not fail loudly.** Observed once, on one box, in one version: a run whose script sat in a folder the login could not read printed `Permission denied`, **rendered the factory scene anyway** at the wrong size on the wrong engine, wrote a file, and **exited 0**. [Open Threads](../Groundwork/Open%20Threads.md) files it as a Blender behaviour owed to Blender Research and states that no architecture document may rest on a launch script failing loudly. **Breaks:** [5.1](#51-the-route-is-launch-material-and-not-a-copy)'s route, in the one shape where the material is placed and the render cannot read it — the job then renders something else entirely and every gate this project has, the exit status and the frames on disk, would pass. **What holds without it:** D19's read-back confirms the placement under the login the render will run as, which closes the case that is Smoozy's own doing; D16's marker makes the failure readable after the fact; and C27's completeness statement would show a job whose expected files are all absent. **None of those makes it fail loudly and none is offered as doing so.**

9. **ANSWERED AT SPECS, 2026-09-02, and it was never this stage's to answer. Re-pointed 2026-09-02; this read that the question is marked `[PENDING]` in the spec and names no stage, which was true until that date.** Row 137's product half — what a re-run does for the user when the file changed and the folder layout moved with it — is settled in [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md): the re-run goes ahead, and **the telling is what was settled** — the job's entry says the file changed between attempts and what became of the frames that were already finished, said and never asked, because the re-run is automatic and the user may not be at the computer. **The settled sentence was corrected on 2026-09-04, out of a code round, and this entry with it**: it read *that frames finished under the earlier arrangement were rendered again rather than reused*, which holds only where the arrangement moved, and the ordinary way a file changes moves none of it — see R14's correction and C12a. **Breaks:** nothing built here, then or now. **What holds, and what the settled answer rests on:** R13 keeps every map the job has had, each named by the attempt that used it; R14 makes the already-there test read the map in force for the attempt being started, which is the reading the spec's settled sentence names by clause; and the telling itself is `Queue & History`'s — **written 2026-09-02 at its [§10](Queue%20&%20History.md#10-the-note-the-figure-and-what-the-read-fills-in) N9a, answering Row 516, so the route this entry names now ends at a clause.** **This entry is kept rather than struck** because R14, [Section 16](#16-what-this-document-does-not-own) and Row 137 all cite it.

10. **A name that reaches the machine intact cannot always be got back, and the final name is this document's.** Seventeen names written on the rental came back as fourteen files, with **every fetch reporting success**, and the control proves the loss is at the receiving end. **This document exposed nothing new; what it exposes is where the consequence lands** — the inventory names files the user's computer may fold together, so an arrival count taken on the user's side can be short of the count with nothing said. **Breaks:** `Download`'s arrival counting against this document's number, and Rows 303 and 307. **What holds without it:** N14, which states that the inventory is a statement about the machine; the count itself, which is right about the machine; and the fact that the loss is at a boundary another document owns and has a row for.

11. **Whether a read of a `.blend` or the production of a derivative disturbs a running render.** Not answered: five reads during a render all succeeded and the render finished whole, but three testers shared four cores and the loads cannot be separated; the derivative's cost rose from about 0.8 s to about 2.4 s with the cores taken, with the output byte-identical and the render still progressing. **Breaks:** nothing in the correctness of R1 or B23; what is unmeasured is the cost to a render running beside them. **What holds without it:** every one of those runs completed correctly, and arbitration is `Rented-server runtime`'s with this document's declaration that a derivative never blocks a render.

12. **A path held in a custom property is reached by no route this stage has.** The reach question is closed — the Creator ruled on 2026-08-18 that the redirect extends to every further write place the walk over Blender's own path marks reaches, and [5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) D20 carries it. **This one residue survives that ruling**: the flat list and the relative sweep miss it, and the walk over Blender's own path marks misses it too ([4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) item 6, D21). It is therefore **not found at all** rather than found and not mapped, so it cannot be recorded, cannot be shown, and cannot hold a job. **Breaks:** the reach of Row 118's *every place the job would write*, for that single case — a job whose file writes through such a path writes outside the job's folder, `Download` never sees it, and nothing says so, because nothing here knows. **What holds without it:** every other recorded write place is now in the map, under the job's folder and in the folders shown; and D22's negative is unchanged, so the case fails no more loudly than it ever did.

13. **ANSWERED FOR THE SCENE-STRIP SHAPE, 2026-09-01, out of the repaired Blender research — and the observation this stage refused to build on turns out to have been right.** *File output nodes 13*: *"A File Output node in a scene whose render goes out through its own sequencer **holding a Scene strip** writes nothing, even though that scene is the one being rendered"* (its question 16).
