Label: **observed in a test**. **So the *Breaks* line below has fired**: [4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) item 1's gating conditions are incomplete for that shape — it says *"only the Compositing switch reaches a file output node"*, and a Scene strip reaches it too — and **I6's count was over-counting**, promising folders that are never made and reporting every such frame incomplete for ever. **Both are repaired**: I6 now asks the question as one of its six, and item 1 carries the condition. **What is still unasked is the strips-absent case** — with the Sequencer switch on and **no strips at all**, the compositor runs and its result reaches the file (*The compositor 7*), and whether a File Output node writes in that state was never asked in any version. **Breaks, narrowly:** a job whose scene has the Sequencer switch on and an empty strip list is counted as though its nodes write, which is the old fault at a much smaller width. **It will not be researched** — the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31 — so that half is permanently unresolved. *(The original entry follows, and refusing it at the time was right: the observation was a claim about Blender's behaviour, which this stage does not make. What was missing was the research, and the research has since arrived.)* **One observation from the batch that this stage may not build on.** A tester recorded that **with the sequencer strip active, the scene's own file output nodes wrote nothing**, isolated by changing that one variable, and the reader reported the same shape as data. **That is a claim about how Blender behaves and this stage does not make those**; it is filed as a question owed to Blender Research. **Breaks:** if it holds generally, [4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces)'s gating conditions and I6's count are wrong for any job whose scene carries an active sequencer strip, and the folders shown would promise folders that are never made. **What holds without it:** [4.2](#42-what-the-reading-produces) and [Section 7](#7-the-expected-output-inventory-the-count-and-the-collision-record) are written against what the research already says — a strip runs the reached scene's compositor and its nodes write where that scene's own compositing switch is on — and **nothing here is built on the observation**.

14. **The question behind Row 124's section is labelled permanently unresolved at Specs, and the spec's marker is gone. Re-pointed 2026-09-02; this read *the spec's own open marker at Row 124's section, checked and not stale*, and cited a marker that has since been cleared.** Whether Blender counts a node that will write nothing as a live file output node *"was never asked during the Blender research"*, and the Creator ruled on 2026-08-31 that there will be no further research, so [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md) carries the label **permanently unresolved** in its own words and states *"Nothing waits on it."* **Breaks:** nothing. The spec says the warning and the Creator's ruling hold whichever way it goes and that all the answer would change is how often the case comes up; **no row waits on it** and nothing here does.

15. **Every claim about a rented machine in this document is one or two boxes on one or two days.** The batch this document's readings rest on is **one container, one day, one filesystem, one provider reached over its proxy only, one Mac at the caller's end with one case-insensitive volume at default settings, one unprivileged login shape, one scene, Cycles only, one card**, with three testers sharing four cores throughout. **And one claim rests on a second machine**: the point-cache answer of D21b-i and D21d was run on the multi-provider batch's machine A on 2026-08-22, in 4.5.12 and 5.2.0, on one fixture — a different seller, a different filesystem and a different day from everything else above. **Breaks:** nothing named, because every rule above is written to hold on a container and a virtual machine alike and the readings that could not be relied on — the container's own uptime, its host-derived load, a free-space figure derived from a filesystem list — are called out as traps rather than used. It is recorded so that no later document reads a figure here as a description of rented machines.

16. **CLOSED AS UNRESOLVED, PERMANENTLY, 2026-09-02: whether a rented machine's clock is *correct* against the outside world over the length of a render is not established, and the break this entry named was in the wrong place. Re-written 2026-09-02; this read *Fact 92 is not answered and bears on nothing here*, which described the answer's state on 2026-08-17 and is now wrong in both halves.** **This is not a pending item and must not be read as one.** [Fact 92](Facts.md) was **answered on 2026-08-18 for stability during a render** — the realtime clock sampled once a second against the monotonic clock through a real 400-frame Cycles render, 1,499 samples over 25.0 minutes, inside a band of 0.0129 ms, with no jump and no slew. **Two qualifiers hold the whole of what is left.** The band is realtime **minus monotonic**, which moves only when the clock is **stepped**, so **no drift figure against true time can be recovered from it at all** — a reading of it as a parts-per-million figure was put and refuted. And **nothing was disciplining that clock** — `NTP=no`, `NTPSynchronized=no`, **zero time-service packets, ever** — on **one** rental, while Ubuntu ships a time service whose own manual says it *"will step the system clock for large offsets or slowly adjust it for smaller deltas"*. **A box that later synchronises is a different box for this question.** **Widening it wants a rented machine and there will be no further rental** (the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31), so it stays this way for the life of this document. **Breaks — and this is the relocation.** Nothing in this document, which computes no duration and subtracts no two readings. **What it does break is the figure the user is billed against:** a rented clock that steps states a duration that is not true, silently, in figures a user reads as the cost of a job. That break lands on `Queue & History`'s recorded start, end and duration under [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#3-desired-state-and-observed-state) §3's D5, and that module's own unresolved list already names the lever that would make a step visible — a monotonic reading taken beside each realtime stamp — and declines to decide it. **It is named here because this is where the clause pointing at the fact lives, and not because anything here rests on it. What holds without it:** the ordering of events, which comes from the journal's sequence numbers rather than from any clock, and the standing prohibition on subtracting readings taken on different clocks, which stops a machine's drift being compounded with the user's computer's own undefended one.

17. **CLOSED 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close, and it had been answerable since `Telegram` was written: that document's §12 owns the production — M1 cites the source, M4 settles what a stretch is and when it fires, M5 gives the kind its declarations and M6 its termination contract — and Row 268's answer already records this entry as closed from that side. The contact sheet turns out to be a selection record naming at most ten existing derivatives rather than a composed image, so B23's no-image-tool problem never reached it.** The original entry follows, unchanged. **The moving picture and the contact sheet are the media worker's, and this document defines only the still copies.** [The shape](The%20shape.md#each-rented-server) reads *"The preview worker produces the still copies; the media worker produces the moving picture and contact sheet"*, and [8.4](#84-the-derivatives-one-set-made-once-on-the-machine) B26's two routes are still-image routes. **This document names their source file** ([8.3](#83-the-previews-candidate-list)), which [Telegram](../Specs/Telegram.md) makes its own, and nothing else about them. **Breaks:** nothing built here — the seam is that no document yet states how a moving picture or a contact sheet is produced on a machine that carries no image tool outside Blender (B23), nor which colour treatment it takes, where B25 and B27 settle both for the still copies. **What holds without it:** [Section 11](#11-cleanup-and-the-lifetimes-this-document-applies) K1 now says which class of machine-side derivative its rule covers, so a media derivative is not silently governed by a rule written for another thing; and the source file is named, so whoever builds them is not choosing one.

18. **Whether a node in a scene reached through a sequencer strip stamps the frame number from 5.0.** The one observation is a scene reached through a strip in which 4.5.9 wrote `sec0001.png` with a number and 5.2.0 wrote `fixture_52sec.png` with none, and its own source states it narrowly and says *"the general rule was not established"* (I4). **This stage does not close it by asserting a Blender behaviour**; it is a question owed to Blender Research. **RE-DATED 2026-09-01, at the paperwork sweep: there will be no further Blender research, so this is permanently unresolved rather than owed.** The Creator ruled it on 2026-08-31. **Nothing about what breaks changes** — it is written above and stands — and what changes is that nobody should read this as work that is coming, or leave a space in a document for an answer that will not arrive. **Breaks:** the predicted name for a node destination in a sequencer-reached scene, and with it that job shape's count ([7.3](#73-the-count)) and its already-there test ([10.3](#103-the-zero-byte-rule-and-the-limit-it-does-not-cover)) — Row 125 brings those scenes into the destination set, so it is a case Smoozy meets. **What holds without it:** every other job shape, whose names I5 checked against a real render; and nothing here is built on the observation, exactly as item 13 fences the other sequencer observation.

19. **ANSWERED 2026-08-31, positively, and it was answered in the Blender research before this entry was written. The ordering holds and the read-back works.** *Writing 13* posed exactly this — five arrangements of the file, the setting and the script, in all four versions, on the Creator's Mac and on one rented Linux machine. **Three joined answers carry it and each quotation is attributed to its own — corrected 2026-08-31 the same day, after a checker found this sentence naming one document for words that are in another.** [Where a render's files go](../Blender%20Research/Across%20topics/Where%20a%20render's%20files%20go.md): *"A script handed in at the same launch sees whichever of the two was written before it, and the last of them on the line is the one the render uses."* [What a render really did](../Blender%20Research/Across%20topics/What%20a%20render%20really%20did.md): *"The arrangement that works is the file, then the setting, then the script."* And [What can be set from outside](../Blender%20Research/Across%20topics/What%20can%20be%20set%20from%20outside.md), which is the one [The map](../Blender%20Research/Across%20topics/The%20map.md) names for this question: *"A script that reads the setting back, compares it against what the caller intended and stops when they differ ended the launch at a failure status with nothing rendered and nothing written, and let the run through when they matched."* **The arrangement that works is the file, then the setting, then the script**, which is the order [Rendering](Rendering.md#13-the-window-and-the-order-of-the-line) L8 already composes — item 5 before item 7. **So D10's read-back sees the argument's value and not the file's own, and no attempt fails for this reason.** This entry read *Nothing observed establishes the ordering* and *It is a question owed to Blender Research*; both are struck, and the question was never owed — it was answered and unread.

    **Two riders come with the answer and both bind D10.** First, **what comes back is the raw text handed in**, template marks and hash marks still in it, not a finished file name — so D10's comparison is against the map's pair as text and never against a resolved name. Second, **an imposed output path reaches the active scene alone**, and *What can be set from outside*'s own limit note says that claim rests on one file holding two scenes, *"so whether it turns on which scene is active could not have shown"*. `Rendering` L8 places the scene argument at item 3, ahead of the path at item 5, so the job's scene is the one named before the path is given; **that the path therefore lands on it is the reasonable reading and not an observation.** Where it goes the other way, D10's read-back is what catches it — the material reads the job's scene's own path, mismatches the map, and the attempt fails before the render instruction rather than rendering to a place outside the job's folder. **That residue is item 19a below.** *(Answered by the Build stage's first sitting, 2026-08-31, by reading rather than by research — the reading three plans had been waiting on.)*

    *(The original item 19 follows, unchanged.)* **Whether the regular output path's command-line argument is already in force when the launch material runs.** D10 has the material read that value back inside the running Blender and fail the attempt on a mismatch, which is what makes [Section 12](#12-atomic-or-partial--this-documents-declarations)'s redirect unit atomic over the one pair no material sets. **Nothing observed establishes the ordering.** D11 fixes the argument's *position on the line* — after the file, before the render instruction — and that is a different claim from when its value lands relative to a script handed in at launch; the batch of 2026-08-17 read node properties back **off disk by a separate process** rather than from inside the same run, and [What can be set from outside](../Blender%20Research/Across%20topics/What%20can%20be%20set%20from%20outside.md) does not cover it. **It is a question owed to Blender Research** and this stage does not close it by assertion. **Breaks:** if the value is not yet in force, D10's read-back sees the file's own path, mismatches the map and **fails every attempt** — the redirect never runs and no job renders. **What holds without it:** the failure is safe rather than silent, because a mismatch stops the attempt before the render instruction rather than letting a wrong pair through; D16's marker names what was read; and the other direction of the risk does not exist — an ordering that holds costs nothing.

19a. **Whether an output path given at launch lands on the scene named earlier on the same line, rather than on whichever scene the file was saved showing. PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, 2026-09-02.** It is a Blender-behaviour question of exactly the kind *Writing 13* was posed for, and the Creator ruled on 2026-08-31 that there will be **no further Blender research and no further rented machine for answering a question** — so no round is coming, it is not a park, and nothing anywhere may be written as though the answer were on its way. It is added to the set the paragraph opening this section names, where it belonged from the day it was filed. Not observed: *Writing 13* posed the active-scene claim on one file with two scenes and its own limit note says the active-scene question could not have shown. **Breaks:** on a job whose manifest names a scene other than the file's showing scene, the redirect could reach the wrong scene, and the job's frames would go to the file's own recorded place rather than under the job's folder. **What holds:** D10's read-back is taken against the map's pair for the job's scene, so a miss stops the attempt before the render instruction with the marker naming what was read — safe and total rather than a silent write outside the folder. Filed 2026-08-31 when item 19 closed, because closing item 19 is what made this narrower question visible.

20. **A colliding pair whose two destinations carry different formats.** Added 2026-08-21 with Row 375. Two destinations predicting one path normally predict one ending and therefore one format, but an artist who switched the ending off can put two formats on one predicted name, and **which one the surviving file carries is not decidable before the render** — I16 settles only that what survives is the picture of the node built first, and nothing in the reading records that order. [I14a](#73-the-count) groups such an entry under the first destination the entry records so the split stays deterministic and its groups still sum to the count. **Breaks:** one file sitting in the wrong group of a figure on a screen, and only for a pair the collision record already names to the user and that already holds the job ([9.1](#91-the-three-conditions-and-where-each-is-established)). **What holds:** the count itself, which is entry-counted and unmoved; the split's total; and the collision record, which is what the user is actually warned by.

21. **A render filling the mount the package area sits on starves the machinery that records the render's ending, and on a machine with one writable mount nothing prevents it.** Added 2026-08-22, scenario replays Pile 1 R12. L4a places the package area apart where L2's candidate set offers a second mount, and L4 forbids expecting two mounts to be independently full — so on a one-mount machine, and on any machine whose mounts share what fills, the queue, the journal, the per-job note and the captured streams sit where the render writes. `Rented-server runtime` M4a's 64 MiB control-state reserve is arithmetic over Smoozy's own stream capture and claims no protection from the render's outputs, in its own words; a reserve that could genuinely hold bytes against the render on one filesystem would have to refuse to admit a render short of it, which is a cost to the user no spec grants — [Method](Method.md)'s third escalation. **That escalation has never been put to the Creator, and [Section 15](#15-moments-that-reached-the-creator) now carries the record of it instead of this half-sentence. Corrected 2026-09-02, disk-fill replay finding N-j; this read *reported to the runner rather than written here*, which reads as a thing in flight and is not one.** **Breaks:** the recording of the ending at the moment it happens — every durable write is refused loudly with errno 28 (item 7's answer), so nothing is silently mis-written, and the ending is learned only after space returns, under `Rented-server runtime` §16 item 12's condition. **What holds without it:** the refusals are loud rather than corrupting; the disk-full diagnosis and the supervisor-unavailable condition reach the user by the routes that own them; L4a, wherever the machine offers the second mount; and K3, which refuses to clear anything to make room.

22. **CLOSED AS UNRESOLVED, PERMANENTLY, 2026-08-31, with Row 313: three of the four added write places were never shown to honour a location assigned from outside the file where the artist recorded none.** This is not a pending item and must not be read as one. **[Fact 116](Facts.md) is answered for the physics point cache alone** — in 4.5.12 and 5.2.0, on a rented machine, at the four-value condition D21b-i states. **For the geometry-nodes bake folder, the sequencer proxy folder and the bake output path nothing was established** *(narrowed 2026-10-02: the geometry-nodes bake folder was run at the desk against real bakes, and is set as the file loads — D21d, D1a)*, and the attempts rather than any conclusion are what is recorded, here and at D21d: each assignment read back as made, and the fixture produced no bake, no proxy and no image bake respectively, so no assignment was ever tested against a real write. D21b-ii is why the read-backs settle nothing. **Settling the three needs a rented machine with Blender on it. No further rental and no further Blender research will be taken for them**, so the fact stays incomplete for the life of this document rather than until someone gets to it. **Breaks:** for those three, a job's bake, proxy or image bake may be written beside the `.blend` in the asset area — outside the job's folder, in no inventory, in no count, never brought home, destroyed with the machine — while the render finishes and reports success. That is D22's mode, and D21b-i is it observed once already, on the one place that was run. **What holds without it:** **D21f**, which reads the place's folder after the render rather than its setting before it and states on the completeness statement that the place produced nothing, so the negative is readable instead of silent — with D21g's boundary, since a place that produced nothing is not by itself a fault; **D21b-i**, the point cache's own settled condition, which is the one place the fact reached; and **D21b to D21e**, whose construction names each of the four as a setting and assigns it under the job's folder whether or not the walk read a value back, so nothing above depends on the walk having found anything.

23. **CLOSED AS UNRESOLVED, PERMANENTLY, 2026-09-01: whether a partway fill of the rented machine's mount leaves a truncated file at the final name is unposed on Linux, and **C12** to **C15** turn on the answer.** Added out of scenario replay *The server disk fills up during render*, finding N-b. **Fact 28's Linux run only ever posed the already-full case** — the volume was filled with `dd` first and every write opened onto a full volume — and the desktop evidence it borrows from (Fact 23) is about the three operating systems Smoozy runs on rather than about a rented Linux machine, where **filling during a write left a truncated file on every byte-loop run and an empty file on every copy-tool run**. **Only a rental settles it and there will be no further rental** (the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31), so it is written this way rather than left reading as needs-research. **Breaks — corrected 2026-09-02, because this entry contradicted C17 and C17 is right.** This read that a truncated file makes the carry-on open at the **next** frame, which C15's limit would do on its own and which C17 does not permit: **C17 re-renders the last frame Smoozy asked for that counts as already there, and on a fill that stops the render that frame is exactly the boundary frame**, present or truncated. So the boundary frame is not the exposure and never was. **The exposure is a truncated file that is not the last frame asked for that is present**, and the full-disk shape C23 records is what produces those in quantity: a node's write failing leaves the render **running on to its last frame at status 0**, so a fill part-way through a long range can leave short files at many frames while the reckoning walks back only to the last one. **Those are counted as already there, the carry-on skips them, and the user keeps broken pictures under finished frames' names for good** — the count coming up short being the only signal, and no signal at all where the files have bytes. That is the one outcome `Whole app`'s third promise does not tolerate. **If a partway fill leaves nothing instead, every such frame is absent and nothing is lost.** **What holds without it:** everything C12 to C15 say, unchanged, and C15's limit is stated rather than hidden — the exposure is the size of one frame per fill, not the job. **What would have closed it:** one hour on any Linux machine — start a write larger than the free space onto a volume not yet full, let it fail, list the destination. **That hour will not be taken.** The Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31 is above and this entry's own first paragraph already says only a rental settles it and there will be no further rental; the recipe is recorded so a reader can see how small the missing observation is, **not as work anyone may read as coming.** *(Corrected 2026-09-02: this sentence read "What would close it", in the present conditional, which is the one shape the paragraph opening this section forbids.)*


24. **CLOSED AS UNRESOLVED, PERMANENTLY, 2026-09-01: what a stop landing between a frame's two view files leaves has never been tried, in any version.** Added with I9a. **It is only reachable on a two-view scene**, which I9a now withholds the inventory for, so **nothing on the built path meets it** — a frame left with its left view written and its right view absent is not read as anything, because nothing is predicted for that job. **Breaks:** if the withholding at I9a were ever lifted without this being answered, a half-written frame pair would be the one shape the already-there test could not classify at all. **It will not be answered** — no further research, the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31 — so anything lifting I9a inherits it.

25. **ANSWERED 2026-10-03, by the build runner under the Creator's standing delegation, out of step 10c's map of the 38 promises (hole 15); no auditor of this document has seen it: Smoozy carries such a job and says so.** It runs as asked, I9a's withholding stands as written, and the user is told in one line on the settings page and the queue entry — the words are `Design/Render Settings`' and `Design/Queue & History`'s, under *What it will write* and *What the job writes*. Nothing in this document changes. What a stop landing between a frame's two files leaves stays untried. The original entry follows, unchanged. **ROUTED, NOT ANSWERED, 2026-09-01: whether Smoozy carries a job whose scene writes more than one view, and what the user is told about it, is a hole above this stage.** Added with I9a. **No spec mentions views anywhere** — not stereo, not multi-view, not a second camera eye — so there is no promise for an architecture document to read and none to keep. **This document has taken the narrowest act that closes the harm** (withhold the inventory, say why, read no absence as evidence) and has decided nothing about whether such a job runs, is refused, or is warned about. **Breaks:** nothing a builder builds — the withholding is complete on this side. **What closes it:** a Creator ruling under Rule 7, at Specs, if the shape is ever worth carrying.


26. **CLOSED AS UNRESOLVED, PERMANENTLY, 2026-09-02: what a stop leaves is bounded to the one batch and the one machine behind each half of it, and neither bound will be widened.** Added with the narrowing of C33 and C35, out of the downstream citation pass. **The zero-byte half** — *on Linux every short file was exactly zero bytes* — is **one batch's**, the largest of three sets of runs that answered the question three ways with none of them chosen, and a separate 160-run block over four image formats found a **spread of sizes on both machines**. **The ordering half** — a file output node writing before the frame's own file — was run on **both machines for 4.5 and on the Creator's Mac alone for 5.0, 5.1 and 5.2**, and **Smoozy renders on the machine that is not the Mac** ([Sources](Sources/Job%20files%20and%20outputs.sources.md) entry 58, which has carried both bounds since 2026-08-30 while the two clauses did not). **Either would want further Blender research and there will be none** (the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31), so this is written as permanent rather than as owed, and no document may leave a space for an answer to it. **Breaks:** for the zero-byte half, C13 catches fewer of the images a terminate or a kill leaves than C33 used to imply, so such an image falls to C15's limit and is caught only by C17; for the ordering half, nothing decided here, since the already-there test reads the frame as owing an absent file whichever of the two is written first. **What holds without it:** C32, which sends the interrupt, and the interrupt has never left a short image in any run, in any version, on either machine; C17, which re-renders the last frame asked for that is present, and a stop's short image is by construction that frame; and C13, unchanged for every empty file however it was left.

27. **NARROWED 2026-10-04, out of a test at the desk (entry 42a): from the output's own name and from the version, the width is observed, and I2 states it** — hash marks set it, four digits are a floor where there are none, and a wider number is written whole, alike in 4.5.12, 5.0.1, 5.1.2 and 5.2.2 at frames 1 to 3 and 1000. **What stays unposed is narrower**: a width taken from anything else in the scene, and frames outside those tried. The breaks below now reach only that. *(The entry as closed follows.)* **CLOSED AS UNRESOLVED, PERMANENTLY, 2026-09-05: how wide the frame number is in a name a file output node's recipe builds was never asked.** Added out of a code round. **The width rests on I2's two observed names** — `beauty0010.png` in 4.5.9 and `fixture_52beauty0010.png` in 5.2.0, both at frame 10 and both four digits — and **whether a node's number ever takes another width, from the scene's own padding, from the output's own name or from the version, was never posed.** I3's padding rule is the regular output path's and that path is asked of Blender rather than built, so it carries across to nothing. **There will be no further Blender research** (the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31), so this is permanently unresolved rather than owed, and no document may read it as work that is coming or leave a space for an answer. **Breaks:** every predicted name for every file output node is wrong by that much, silently — the already-there test of C12 finds every frame absent, so an interrupted job re-renders its whole range at the user's expense; C27's completeness statement reports every file missing over a job that wrote all of them; and `Download` shows a job that never finishes. **What holds without it:** I5's check of the two recipes against a real render — 105 predicted against 105 written, on one fixture in two versions; and I9a's discipline, which is the honest answer if the width is ever found to move.

28. **CLOSED AS UNRESOLVED, PERMANENTLY, 2026-09-05: which of a two-picture node's two input sockets the file holds first was never asked.** Added out of a code round. **B3's second limit settles which *side* carries the node's first input per version** — right in 4.5, left in 5.0, 5.1 and 5.2 — and **settles nothing about which side the file's own socket order puts first**, which is the other half of the same reading. **There will be no further Blender research** (the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31), so this is permanently unresolved rather than owed, and no document may read it as work that is coming or leave a space for an answer. **Breaks:** if a file holds its two sockets in the other order the version branch resolves backwards and the walk follows the input that did not come out, silently, reporting a destination the beauty never reached — and everything the walk feeds is then wrong: the main output, the folder *show outputs* opens, B9's duplicate-picture warning and B18's folder tiebreak for the preview. **It reaches any job with a splitting node in the compositor, which is ordinary work.** **What holds without it:** B3's own answer for every limit — the walk stops and names the node — which is what the reading would do if the order is ever found to move.

29. **CLOSED AS UNRESOLVED, PERMANENTLY, 2026-09-05: how the reading names a file output node inside a node group used twice in one tree was never asked.** Added out of a code round. **I15's first bullet says such a group *"is the same case as two nodes"*, and nothing anywhere says what the reading calls the two instances or whether it produces two destinations for them at all.** **There will be no further Blender research** (the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31), so this is permanently unresolved rather than owed, and no document may read it as work that is coming or leave a space for an answer. **Breaks:** if the reading produces one destination for a group used twice, the collision is invisible to Smoozy however the record is written — one picture is silently overwritten by the other, the user is warned about nothing and the job is not held, where [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md) says *"Smoozy can see it before the render rather than after, so it says so."* **What holds without it:** the collision record reads the destinations the reading holds apart rather than the node names they carry, so it is right wherever the reading does produce two.

30. **Whether a scene's half read beside every other scene's in one opening is the same as a reading that names that scene alone. Added 2026-10-10 with R4b, on the Creator's seventh notes, note 1.** **CLOSED 2026-10-10: they are the same** — byte-identical in 4.5.12, 5.0.1, 5.1.2 and 5.2.2 on a file of six scenes that each write differently, 24 of 24 (*observed in a test*: the desk test `every_scene_read_in_one_opening_is_the_same_as_a_reading_naming_it_in_all_four`; `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The Creator's seventh notes, piece Z8 — what landed.md`). The rest of this item is kept as it was written. No plan or research holds it; reading a scene other than the saved one by name is what the render-start reading already does, but never alongside the others in one opening. **Breaks:** if they differ, the page and the download box show one scene's formats and folders while the render writes another set — the Specs' *what the file will write* stated wrongly, silently. **What holds without it:** the builder proves it at the desk before building R4b, the way R4 item 5's narrowing was proved — a file of several scenes, in all four Blenders, each scene's half from the all-scenes reading set against a reading naming that scene; they must be byte-identical, and where they are not, the builder stops and reports, and R4b is not built.

31. **What most curly-brace variables stand for in a node's names, and whether an output's own name takes them. Added 2026-10-18, the Creator's thirteenth notes, notes 5, 9 and 10; no auditor has seen it.** *Dependencies 5* names the variables each version offers and where templates are taken, but records the value of only `{blend_name}` (observed, entry 42b) and `{blend_dir}` (its spelling not recorded), says nothing of a format specifier's effect, and names none of the File Output node's own strings. **Breaks:** a node name holding an unresolved expression is predicted under a name Blender never writes. **What holds without it:** the reading leaves such a name unresolved and the output owes no inventory entry (I2's **A curly-brace expression in a node's names**), so no frame is called missing and the file still comes down at the ending walk. Owed to Blender Research as a blind question (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/The thirteenth notes, the plan pass and the drawing pass — what was done.md`).

---

## 15. Moments that reached the Creator

**One, and it arrived from the round rather than from the writing.** The document as first written scoped the redirect to the regular output path and each file output node's folder, and recorded a bake output path, a point cache, a geometry-nodes bake folder and a sequencer proxy folder as *found and not mapped*. **That is [Method](Method.md)'s third escalation**: the answer cost the user a thing that can be lost — those files written on the rented machine, outside the job's folder, never brought home, destroyed with the machine, with nothing said — and no spec said they got that. It is not the first stop, because the walk over Blender's own path marks reaches them, so nothing established that the redirect *could not*. **The Creator ruled on 2026-08-18 that the redirect is extended to all four**; [5.5](#55-what-the-redirect-reaches-and-the-one-residue-it-does-not) D20 carries it, [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md) names the covered places, and [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 12 holds the one residue the ruling does not reach.

**A second third escalation was raised by this document and has never reached the Creator. It is recorded here because this is where a reader looks for it, and *one* above still counts what did reach them. Added 2026-09-02, out of the disk-fill replay's finding N-j.**

**What it is, in one sentence, and the sentence is the whole of it:** on a machine with one disk, a render that fills that disk leaves Smoozy unable to record what the render did until the user frees space; the alternative is Smoozy refusing to start a render when the disk is nearly full, which nobody asked for.

**Why it is a third escalation.** [Section 14](#14-unresolved) item 21 states the condition. `Rented-server runtime` M4a's control-state reserve is arithmetic over Smoozy's own stream capture and claims no protection from the render's outputs, in its own words, and [1.1](#11-one-root-one-mount-chosen-once) L4a's second mount is placement rather than protection wherever two mounts are not independently full. **A reserve that could genuinely hold bytes against the render on one filesystem would have to refuse to admit a render short of it** — a refusal the user meets, over a machine they are paying for by the hour, that no spec grants them.

**Its state, said plainly: it was never put.** It was raised on **2026-08-22** with the scenario replays' Pile 1 R12 and **reported to the runner** rather than written into this document. The routed-clauses file of that day filed it as *a conditional Creator moment, not yet live*, reaching the Creator only if they asked for item 21's residual to be closed. **The runner has since ruled otherwise and filed it as one sentence owed to the Creator** (`Audits/Architecture/Routed elsewhere.md` item 28). **The two filings disagreed about whether anything was owed; the runner's is the later, and this document follows it.** No architecture document records the escalation as put or as ruled, and **this one does not invent an answer for it**.

**What that costs.** Nothing that is built. Item 21 is written, its *breaks* and its *holds* are stated, and every durable write on a full disk is refused loudly with errno 28 rather than half-completing, so the machine's records stay truthful and the ending is learned once space returns. **What is lost is the choice**: the Creator has never been offered the one trade this document found, cannot take it, and a reader of item 21 could not tell an unasked question from an answered one. That is the fault this entry closes, and the only one it closes.

**What holds meanwhile.** L4a wherever the machine offers a second mount; K3, which refuses to clear anything to make room; the loud refusals; and item 21 itself, which states the exposure rather than hiding it.

**A third is raised here and is not put either, and this one is a first escalation rather than a third. Added 2026-09-04, out of a code round, with R14's correction and C12a.**

**What it is, as a moment rather than as a mechanism.** A user is rendering, the job is interrupted, and before the automatic re-run they open the `.blend`, change a material and save. The re-run notices the file changed (R13). **What they get today** is the rest of the frames rendered from the new file and the frames already finished kept as they were — one folder holding the work as it stood before the change and as it stands after — with the entry saying so. **What they would get instead** is every frame rendered again from the new file: one consistent set of frames, and a second bill for the frames they had already paid for, over a machine charged by the hour.

**Why it is not taken here.** It changes what the user gets and what they pay for, and [Method](Method.md) reserves that to the Creator. It also runs against Rule 8 in the direction this project has ruled on before: replacing the file mid-job is the user's own act, the frames already written are their own work and are not damaged by being kept, and the promise that binds is that Smoozy says what happened — which the corrected telling now does. **A mechanism for it exists and is not built**: C12 would have to read which attempt or which file wrote a frame, which today it does not (it reads presence and size alone), and the reckoning would then hand over frames whose files are on disk.

**What is built instead is the telling, and only the telling.** [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md) is corrected so that its two cases are both stated, C12a produces the fact that separates them, and `Queue & History` N9a says which one happened. **That is a false statement made true and nothing more** — the same standing the project gave the identical shape at `Server Setup` — so it needed no ruling. **What is owed to the Creator is this one choice**, and until they take it the answer above is what is built.

**Seven answers were checked against [Method](Method.md)'s third escalation — does this put anything in front of the user that no spec says they get, a place to look, something to type, or a thing that can be lost.** None reached it, and the checks are recorded because no gate catches this one and because this family is the likeliest in the stage to hit it.

**The redirect route of [5.1](#51-the-route-is-launch-material-and-not-a-copy).** The launch-material route puts nothing in front of anyone: the user has one `.blend`, the one they uploaded, and every place that names a file names that one. **The copy route was the one weighed and declined on this test as well as on the others** — a `.blend` Smoozy made, sitting beside the artist's own in the folder they browse, is a thing they can move, delete, overwrite or wonder about, and no spec says they get it. **The opposite ruling would have been the escalation.**

**The mount the root sits on, [1.1](#11-one-root-one-mount-chosen-once).** [Open Threads](../Groundwork/Open%20Threads.md) parks this at Architecture and says it becomes Specs' only if the user should be offered the choice. **They are not offered it**, so nothing new reaches them; the alternative — a question about which disk at setup or at submission — is a thing to decide that no spec grants. **The second half of that reason is withdrawn, 2026-08-22.** It used to add that *a rental's second filesystem turns out to be a quota on the same disk rather than a meaningfully different place, so the choice would cost a decision and buy little*, and L4 records that this is no longer true: one seller's second filesystem is a real device eight times the size of the root. **The decision stands on L2's ground alone** — the mount is chosen by the free-space reading, which picks the right one on that machine without asking anybody.

**The third hold condition, [9.1](#91-the-three-conditions-and-where-each-is-established).** A path too long to fit under the job's folder holds the job and asks the user. **That is not a new question**: the spec already grants exactly that hold and that choice for a path Smoozy cannot bring under the job's folder, and a length that will not fit is one such path. What was checked is that it does not ask **more often** than the promise anticipates — the rule deepens paths by construction, so the case is real — and the conclusion is that the honest alternative, writing outside the job's folder quietly, is the harm the promise exists to prevent.

**Not asking twice, [9.2](#92-where-the-hold-is-placed-and-what-releases-it).** A user who pre-loaded and submitted has already answered the writes-nothing and colliding-pair questions, so the hold is not placed again for them. Placing it again would be a second question about a decision they had made, which no spec grants.

**The picks held in the history record, [8.2](#82-the-two-picks-made-once-and-never-recomputed).** Keeping two small values for the life of an entry the user controls is what the spec already promises twice over — the pick is made once and kept, and nothing expires. **The opposite ruling would have been the escalation**: a pick recomputed at each opening would make a finished job's preview a thing that changes under the user, which is the one failure that sentence exists to prevent.

**The derivatives kept until the job record is deleted or their own age ending is reached, whichever comes first, [Section 11](#11-cleanup-and-the-lifetimes-this-document-applies).** *(Corrected 2026-09-02: this named the deletion alone, where [What binds this before anything is decided](#what-binds-this-before-anything-is-decided) and [Section 11](#11-cleanup-and-the-lifetimes-this-document-applies) K1 and K2 all carry the age ending the Creator chose on 2026-08-22 over a size cap and over leaving it unbounded. **The period is thirty days**, `State, identity and recovery` §11.1 R16, ruled 2026-09-02 under the Creator's standing delegation of that date; that document's §13 item 12 is answered and is no longer a park. This clause said it was one until later the same day.)* This is [State, identity and recovery](State,%20identity%20and%20recovery.md#113-the-two-rulings-those-documents-were-promised) §11.3's ruling applied rather than a fresh decision, and that document already recorded the same check. It is not a place to look, something to type, or a thing that can be lost.

**An eighth was checked on 2026-08-21 with Row 375 and is not one either.** [I14a](#73-the-count) counts an inventory that already exists and routes figures the spec already promises — the file count, the folders and the list of formats — to the page that draws them. **No new surface, nothing to type, nothing that can be lost**, and no reading is taken for it: where no reading stands the page states nothing rather than asking for one. **Declined.**

**The completeness statement, [10.6](#106-the-completeness-reading-and-the-one-signal-a-lost-write-leaves).** It produces no new surface and no new place to look: it is the count `Download` already shows and the raw record `Queue & History` already shows, with the absent paths named so the second is worth reading against the first. **What it deliberately does not do is claim a cause**, which would be the app being wrong on screen about what happened.

**Three more were checked on 2026-08-25, with Rows 493, 495 and 496, and none is one either.** I16a records a name the reading already holds each destination apart by and routes it to a warning the spec already grants — nothing new in front of the user, nothing to type, nothing that can be lost. H1a's widening holds a job the spec's hold exists for — one that costs a machine's time for no files — and the opposite reading was the escalation: a whole range rendered and paid for producing nothing, with the statement the spec promises never drawn. B31a records a name beside a record that already travels, and shows nothing that was not already true of the picture. Declined, all three.

---

## 16. What this document does not own

Cited, and deliberately not restated or decided here.

- **The job's identity, its folder's meaning to the user, what a queue entry and a history entry show, the frame preview's presentation and when it refreshes, *show outputs* as an action, adoption, catch-up, deletion and the machine's own history copy** — `Queue & History`, Rows 47 to 87 and 302.
- **Atomic job submission, the Blender launch contract, scene and frame arguments, engine and GPU selection, the first-frame hold, sending the stop, re-run and carry-on as user actions** — `Rendering`, Rows 1 to 23 and 294.
- **Dependency inspection, local search, upload deduplication, asset ownership and invalidation, what the remap says, linked-file handling and iteration** — `Dependency Upload & Remap`, Rows 159 to 179.
- **Manual and automatic download requests, format narrowing, finished-file detection, destination rules, local collision handling, continuation and request-level progress; the record of what an arrangement has already fetched** — `Download`, Rows 202 to 220 and 298–299.
- **The staging-and-publication contract itself, request framing, versioning, correlation, idempotency, the authentication boundary, capability negotiation, per-waiter deadlines, transfer channels, concurrency, priority, rate enforcement, and the free-space reading's own mechanism** — `Server protocol and transfers`, Rows 281, 287, 289, 291 and 306.
- **The machine-local endpoint, boot registration, the package commit and rollback, per-operation and role-worker lifecycles, recorded execution-group identities, per-kind termination contracts and signal targets, scheduling and exclusion, the machine-held answer-release path, the setup-in-progress state, and the captured streams with their explicit collection outcome** — `Rented-server runtime`, Row 286.
- **Every identity's issuer and rules, desired versus observed state, the event journal and acknowledgement, the operation record and the four outcomes, reconciliation on both sides, failure records, the atomic-or-partial boundary itself, tombstones, and the local lifetimes** — `State, identity and recovery`, Rows 285 and 292.
- **The local database and its migrations, the platform adapters, receiving-filesystem collision judgement, the presentation of every failure and progress state, and the update pipeline** — `Desktop app and delivery`, Rows 280, 282, 284, 288, 290, 303 and 304–305.
- **The secret store, the local record's protection, the crossing lists, host trust, the erasure order, logging redaction, and the licence trust boundary** — `Security and licence`, Row 278.
- **Readiness and runtime-health inspection, Blender and add-on installation, privilege escalation, resumable steps, restart and safe re-run** — `Server Setup`, Rows 88 to 117, 295 and 308–309.
- **Remote listing, permission handling, upload requests, stale listings, and the live-job dependency warning** — `File Browser`, Rows 180 to 201 and 307. **Checked 2026-08-29 and left as it stands.** The `File Browser` gate routed a correction here — that this locator undercounts by nine, **Rows 340 to 346, 428 and 486** being that document's too. **All nine are Design rows**, and every bullet of this list names its document's spec rows plus the spec rows cross-filed to it and omits its Design rows — this list's own locator convention, which `Render Settings` §15 states in terms. So **none of the nine are owed here** and the range is unchanged; what a bullet gives is a locator for the topics named, not an exhaustive ownership claim.
- **The single receiving slot, cross-machine routing, pinned-message serialization, report pacing, and everything the Telegram worker sends or receives** — `Telegram`, Rows 255 to 277 and 296.
- **The split between working and viewing formats**, which is the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-13 in [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md) and closed Row 297 at Specs. Applied here, built for nowhere.
- **What a re-run does when the file changed and the folder layout moved with it, and what the user is told about it** — a change to what the user gets rather than an architecture choice, and **settled at Specs on 2026-09-02** in [What a job writes](../Specs/What%20a%20job%20writes.md): the re-run goes ahead, and the job's entry says two things — that the file changed, and **what became of the frames that were already finished**, which is reused where the arrangement did not move and rendered again where it did. **The telling is `Queue & History`'s, and is written at its [§10](Queue%20&%20History.md#10-the-note-the-figure-and-what-the-read-fills-in) N9a.** *(Re-pointed 2026-09-02; this cited a `[PENDING]` naming no stage, and that marker is gone. N9a named 2026-09-02.)* *(**Corrected 2026-09-05, out of a code round.** This bullet still said the entry says frames finished under the earlier arrangement **were rendered again**, flat. That sentence was withdrawn on 2026-09-04, when [R14](#44-the-file-identity-and-when-a-recorded-reading-goes-stale) and [§14](#14-unresolved) item 9 were corrected and this bullet was missed. Nothing runs on it; it is repaired because this section is the first place a reader looks.)*
